# The Falconer distance conjecture in all dimensions

Coordinate `EN-110-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-ML-H-0169` | `PM-073.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `The-Falconer-distance-conjecture-in-all-dimensions-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint The-Falconer-distance-conjecture-in-all-dimensions-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). ```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We resolve the Falconer distance conjecture in every dimension. For every integer <i>d</i> ≥ 2, a compact subset of ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup> with Hausdorff dimension greater than $`d/2`$ determines a set of Euclidean distances of positive Lebesgue measure.",

      "end_byte": 295971,
      "heading": "References",
      "start_byte": 289227
    }
  ],
  "tokenizer": "o200k_base"
}

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the Falconer distance conjecture in every dimension. For every integer <i>d</i> ≥ 2, a compact subset of ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup> with Hausdorff dimension greater than $`d/2`$ determines a set of Euclidean distances of positive Lebesgue measure.

- **claim-positive-measure-distance-set** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $d\ge2$ be an integer and let $E\subset\mathbb R^d$ be compact. If $\dim_H E>d/2$, then $$\mathcal L^1(\Delta(E))>0.$$
  - Explanation: The paper asserts positive Lebesgue measure for the unpinned Euclidean distance set under a strict Hausdorff dimension hypothesis, without additional regularity assumptions.
- **claim-energy-restriction-projection** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-conv-energy)): Let $\mu$ be supported in a bounded subset of $\mathbb R^d$.* 1. *If $\mu(B(z,r))\le C r^s$ for $0<r\le1$, then $I_t(\mu)<\infty$ for every $0<t<s$.* 2. *If $I_t(\mu)<\infty$, then for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a compact restriction of mass at least $\mu(\mathbb R^d)-\varepsilon$ that satisfies a $t$-ball bound with a finite constant.* 3. *If $0<t<m\le d$, $I_t(\mu)<\infty$, and $P_V$ is orthogonal projection to a Haar-random $m$-plane, then $I_t((P_V)_\#\mu)<\infty$ for almost every $V$.
  - Explanation: Ball bounds give finite energy below their exponent; finite energy permits almost full compact restrictions with ball bounds and survives generic projections of adequate dimension.
- **claim-sequential-uniformity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-conv-uniformity)): For fixed background parameters, let $\mathcal C(\varepsilon,R)$ be classes of data, increasing in the allowed loss $\varepsilon>0$. Let $\mathcal G(c,R)$ be conclusions, weakening as the gain $c>0$ decreases. Suppose that for every sequence $$\varepsilon_j\downarrow0,\qquad R_j\to\infty,\qquad X_j\in\mathcal C(\varepsilon_j,R_j),$$ there are a subsequence and a fixed $c>0$ on which $X_j$ satisfies $\mathcal G(c,R_j)$ for all sufficiently large $j$. Then there exist $\varepsilon_0,c_0>0$ and $R_0<\infty$ such that every $X\in\mathcal C(\varepsilon_0,R)$, $R\ge R_0$, satisfies $\mathcal G(c_0,R)$.
  - Explanation: A gain that persists on subsequences with vanishing losses implies a uniform positive gain at sufficiently large scales, with background parameters fixed.
- **claim-dyadic-regular-classes** (construction, [source](#stmt-reg-regularization)): Let $n\ge1$, let $\rho$ be a probability on a unit dyadic cube in $\mathbb R^n$, and put $R=2^N$. For every fixed $0<\lambda<1$ and all sufficiently large $N$, there are pairwise disjoint sets $E_\alpha$, each a union of depth-$N$ cubes, with the following properties:* 1. *Their number is at most $N^{C_n/\lambda}$, each has $\rho(E_\alpha)\ge R^{-\lambda}$, and $$\rho\Bigl(\mathbb R^n\setminus\bigcup_\alpha E_\alpha\Bigr) \le R^{-2}+N^{C_n/\lambda}R^{-\lambda} =R^{-\lambda+o(1)}.$$* 2. *For $\rho_\alpha=\rho\|_{E_\alpha}/\rho(E_\alpha)$ there is a nondecreasing $n$-Lipschitz function $m_\alpha:[0,1]\to[0,n]$, with $m_\alpha(0)=0$, such that every occupied depth-$j$ cube satisfies $$\label{reg:cell-mass} R^{-m_\alpha(j/N)-C_n\lambda} \le\rho_\alpha(Q)\le R^{-m_\alpha(j/N)+C_n\lambda},\qquad 0\le j\le N.$$* 3. *If $\rho(B(x,r))\le Kr^s$ for $R^{-1}\le r\le1$, with $0\le s\le n$ and $K\ge1$, then $$\label{reg:profile-lower-bound} m_\alpha(u)\ge su-C_n\lambda-\log_R K, \qquad 0\le u\le1.$$* *Here and below an occupied cube means a cube of positive mass for the specified class. In particular, for occupied $P\subset Q$ at depths $b\ge a$, $$\label{reg:conditional-mass} \frac{\rho_\alpha(P)}{\rho_\alpha(Q)} =R^{-[m_\alpha(b/N)-m_\alpha(a/N)]\pm C_n\lambda}.$$ Constants depend only on $n$; the threshold for $N$ can depend on $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: Finite dyadic regularization retains disjoint classes with controlled branching, Lipschitz profiles, cell mass comparisons and inherited lower profile bounds.
- **claim-pruning-multiplies-fibres** (construction, [source](#stmt-reg-pruning-multiplication)): Let $\rho$ be a source probability and $\nu$ a finite pin measure, both on $\mathbb R^n$, and let $0\le a_0<\cdots<a_J\le N$ be a fixed finite collection of dyadic depths. Suppose pin–source distances on the sets in question are between fixed positive constants. Let $G'$ be a measurable set of pairs. For each $j<J$, suppose there is a measurable set $E_j\supset G'$ and a number $f_j\ge0$ such that every $(x,z)\in G'$, with $z\in Q$ at depth $a_j$, satisfies $$\label{reg:local-fibre} \rho\{z'\in Q\cap(E_j)_x: \|\pi_x(z')-\pi_x(z)\|\le C2^{-a_{j+1}}\} \le f_j\rho(Q).$$ The constant $C$ may be any sufficiently large fixed constant, depending on the geometry and on the fixed angular width to be used below. For every $e>0$ one can delete pair mass at most $(J+1)R^{-e}\nu(\mathbb R^n)$ from $G'$ and obtain $G''$ such that, for each pin $x$, each initial cube $Q_0$ at depth $a_0$, and every spherical ball $B$ of radius $C_12^{-a_J}$, $$\label{reg:multiplied-fibre} \rho\{z\in Q_0\cap G''_x:\pi_x(z)\in B\} \le R^{eJ}\Bigl(\prod_{j=0}^{J-1}f_j\Bigr)\rho(Q_0).$$ An identical conclusion holds without a lower pin–source separation if Equation <a href="#reg:local-fibre" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="reg:local-fibre">[reg:local-fibre]</a> instead tests the perpendicular distance to the full line $x+\mathbb R(z-x)$ at width $C2^{-a_{j+1}}$, provided pin and source sets remain bounded and pairs on the diagonal have been removed.
  - Explanation: Deleting low-density witnesses lets local directional bounds multiply through selected depths. The density test concerns the pair relation before the final deletion.
- **claim-linear-angular-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-linear-gain)): Fix $k\ge2$ and $$k-1\le q\le k-\tfrac12<t<k.$$ Fix a tube-width constant $C$. In the case $q=k-1$, also fix $\zeta>0$. There exist $\epsilon,c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\delta$, the following holds. Let $\mu$ be a bounded point subprobability in $\mathbb R^{k+1}$ and $\nu$ a direction subprobability on $S^k$ with respective $t$- and $q$-ball bounds of loss $\delta^{-\epsilon}$ down to $\delta$. At $q=k-1$ assume in addition $$\label{ang:endpoint-plate} \nu\{w:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(w,H)\le r\}\le\delta^{-\epsilon}r^\zeta \qquad(\delta\le r\le1)$$ for every linear hyperplane $H$. Let $E\subset\mathbb R^{k+1}\times S^k$ be a measurable incidence relation, and write $E_w=\{x:(x,w)\in E\}$. If each direction fiber $E_w$ is covered by at most $\delta^{-q-\epsilon}$ tubes parallel to $w$ of width $C\delta$, then $$(\mu\otimes\nu)(E)\le\delta^c.$$ All constants are uniform over the measures and the incidence relations with these fixed parameters.
  - Explanation: Sparse parallel tube covers capture only power-small incidence between the stated point and direction laws. The integer endpoint additionally requires hyperplane nonconcentration.
- **claim-planar-furstenberg-covering** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-furstenberg)): Let $0<b<1$ and $b<f<2$. There is $\epsilon_0>0$ such that the following holds for sufficiently small $\delta$. Suppose that $P$ is a nonempty bounded $(\delta,f,\delta^{-\epsilon_0})$-set and that each $x\in P$ has a nonempty family $\mathcal T_x$ of incident $\delta$-tubes whose line parameters form a $(\delta,b,\delta^{-\epsilon_0})$-set. Then $$\left\|\bigcup_{x\in P}\mathcal T_x\right\|_\delta \ge\delta^{-2b-\epsilon_0}.$$ The exponent may be decreased to absorb fixed bounded charts and fixed width multiples.
  - Explanation: Nonconcentrated planar points with nonconcentrated incident tube families force a large union in line-parameter space.
- **claim-labelled-planar-incidence-obstruction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ang-planar-measures)): Fix $0<b<1$ and $b<f<2$. There is no sequence $\delta\to0$ with the following data. Let $\mu,\nu$ be subprobabilities on Borel label sets $X,W$ in Euclidean spaces, and let $$p:X\longrightarrow\mathbb R^2,\qquad v:W\longrightarrow S^1$$ be measurable coordinate maps with fixed bounded images. Suppose that $p_\#\mu$ and $v_\#\nu$ have respective $f$- and $b$-ball bounds of loss $\delta^{-o(1)}$ down to $\delta$. Let $E\subset X\times W$ be a measurable incidence relation with $$(\mu\otimes\nu)(E)\ge\delta^{o(1)}.$$ For each original direction label $\omega\in W$, put $E_\omega=\{\xi\in X:(\xi,\omega)\in E\}$. Assume that the set of point coordinates $p(E_\omega)$ is covered by at most $\delta^{-b-o(1)}$ strips of width $C\delta$ parallel to $v(\omega)$.
  - Explanation: Subpower incidence cannot coexist with the stated ball bounds and sparse strip covers. Covers remain attached to original labels even when geometric coordinates coincide.
- **claim-haar-band-probabilities** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-haar)): Let $0\le h\le n-1$, and let $V$ be a Haar-random codimension-$h$ linear subspace of $\mathbb R^n$. For a unit vector $w$, $$\Pr\{\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(w,V)\le\delta\}\asymp\delta^h.$$ If $j\le n-h$ unit vectors $w_1,\ldots,w_j$ have singular values $d_1\ge\cdots\ge d_j\ge0$, then $$\label{ang:haar-multiple} \delta^{-jh}\Pr\{\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(w_i,V)\le\delta\text{ for all }i\} \le C_{n,j}\prod_{i=1}^j(d_i+\delta)^{-h}.$$ When $h=0$, the probability and the factors with exponent $h$ are one.
  - Explanation: Random subspace band probabilities are controlled by codimension and the singular values of the tested vector configuration, with an explicit trivial codimension case.
- **claim-controlled-slice-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-ang-slice-selection)): Let $0\le h\le n-2$. Let point and direction subprobabilities $\mu,\nu$ in $\mathbb R^n$ have $t$- and $q$-ball bounds with subpower losses, where $t,q>h$. Assume the directions lie in a fixed small projective chart. Let an incidence relation $E$ have mass at least subpower, and suppose each direction fiber is covered by at most $\delta^{-q-o(1)}$ tubes of width $C\delta$.* *For Haar-random codimension-$h$ subspaces $V$ and $z\in V^\perp$, set $$\begin{aligned} d\nu_V(w)&=\delta^{-h}\mathbf 1_{\{\|P_{V^\perp}w\|\le\delta\}}\,d\nu(w),\\ d\mu_{V,z}(x)&=\delta^{-h} \mathbf 1_{\{\|P_{V^\perp}x-z\|\le\delta\}}\,d\mu(x). \end{aligned}$$ Integrate $z$ over a fixed sufficiently large ball. There are families of restrictions $\widetilde\nu_V\le\nu_V$, $\widetilde\mu_{V,z}\le\mu_{V,z}$, and relations $\widetilde E_{V,z}\subset E$, measurable in the displayed parameters and restricted to the two bands defining $\mu_{V,z}$ and $\nu_V$, such that $$\mathbb E_V\int (\widetilde\mu_{V,z}\otimes\widetilde\nu_V)(\widetilde E_{V,z})\,dz \ge\delta^{o(1)},$$ and the following hold:* 1. *The restricted measures have masses and ball losses at most $\delta^{-o(1)}$, with exponents $q-h$ and $t-h$, respectively.* 2. *Each remaining direction fiber is covered by at most $\delta^{-(q-h)-o(1)}$ width-$C'\delta$ tubes.* 3. *Put $$p_{V,z}(x)=P_Vx,\qquad v_V(w)=P_Vw/\|P_Vw\| \quad\text{for }\|P_{V^\perp}w\|\le\delta.$$ The first map is projection to $V+z$ followed by translation to $V$; the second is well-defined on the direction band for small $\delta$. The coordinate pushforwards of the restricted measures under these maps satisfy the ball bounds in item 1, with fixed constant changes. For every original direction label $w$ in that band, the coordinates $p_{V,z}(\{x:(x,w)\in\widetilde E_{V,z}\})$ have the tube cover in item 2 with direction $v_V(w)$. This cover remains attached to $w$ even when two projected direction coordinates coincide.* *The direction restriction $\widetilde\nu_V$ depends on $V$ only, and its mass has an a priori subpower upper bound valid for every $V$.
  - Explanation: Random band slicing followed by truncation retains incidence while controlling marginal masses, ball losses and active tube counts. Projected covers retain their original direction labels.
- **claim-transverse-tube-counting** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-loomis-whitney)): Let $v_1,\ldots,v_m$ be unit vectors in $\mathbb R^m$, $m\ge2$, and suppose $\|\det(v_1,\ldots,v_m)\|\ge\Delta>0$. If a bounded set $A$ is covered, for each $i$, by $M_i$ tubes of width $C\delta$ parallel to $v_i$, then $$\|A\|_{C_m\delta}\le C_{m,C}\Delta^{-m} \prod_{i=1}^m M_i^{1/(m-1)}.$$ Changing the fixed constant in the covering radius changes only the constant in this inequality.
  - Explanation: Simultaneous covers in transverse directions bound spatial covering number through the discrete Loomis–Whitney inequality, paying for the determinant loss.
- **claim-weak-radial-pruning** (construction, [source](#stmt-ang-weak-radial)): Fix $k\ge2$, $\zeta>0$, and $$k-2<\ell<\frac{(k-1)^2}{k}.$$ For every $e>0$ there exist $\epsilon,c>0$ with the following property for sufficiently small $\delta$. Suppose that a probability $\lambda$ in a bounded subset of $\mathbb R^k$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \lambda(B(x,r))&\le\delta^{-\epsilon}r^{k-1},\\ \lambda(H^{(r)})&\le\delta^{-\epsilon}r^\zeta \qquad(\delta\le r\le1) \end{aligned}$$ for every affine hyperplane $H$, where $H^{(r)}$ is its $r$-neighborhood. There is a measurable pair set $G$ such that $$(\lambda\otimes\lambda)(G^c)\lesssim\delta^c, \qquad \lambda\{z:(x,z)\in G,\ \pi_x(z)\in B(\theta,r)\} \le\delta^{-e}r^\ell$$ for every $x$, every spherical cap center $\theta$, and $\delta\le r\le1$. The diagonal is omitted from $G$. The conclusion also holds for balls in projective direction space, with a fixed change of constant, by taking the two antipodal caps.
  - Explanation: Ball and affine-hyperplane nonconcentration allow power-small pair deletion that leaves uniform radial cap bounds. Diagonal pairs are excluded.
- **claim-borel-bad-direction-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-bad-directions)): Fix the parameters of Theorem <a href="#ang:linear-gain" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ang:linear-gain">6</a>. There are $c_1,\epsilon_1>0$ such that the following holds at sufficiently small $\delta$. Let $\mu$ be a bounded point probability satisfying the $t$-ball bound with loss $\delta^{-\epsilon_1}$. Let $B_\delta$ be the set of directions for which at most $\delta^{-q-c_1}$ width-$C\delta$ tubes capture $\mu$-mass at least $\delta^{c_1}$. There is a Borel set $B_\delta^+\supset B_\delta$, depending only on the point law and this geometric test, such that every direction subprobability $\nu$ satisfying the $q$-ball bound with loss $\delta^{-\epsilon_1}$, and, when $q=k-1$, Equation <a href="#ang:endpoint-plate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:endpoint-plate">[ang:endpoint-plate]</a> with $\epsilon_1$ in place of $\epsilon$, satisfies $$\nu(B_\delta^+)\le\delta^{c_1}.$$ In particular this bounds the outer $\nu$-measure of the original geometric bad set. The direction law need not be normalized on either set or on any individual incidence fiber. All predetermined fixed widths are allowed.
  - Explanation: A common Borel envelope of geometrically bad directions has power-small mass for each admissible direction law, without normalizing that law on the envelope.
- **claim-profile-contact-interval** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prep-contact)): Suppose $m:[0,1]\to\mathbb R$ is nondecreasing and Lipschitz, $m(0)=0$, and $m(u)\ge su$. If $q<t<s$, there exist $0<A<B<1$, with $B<2A$, such that $$\begin{aligned} m(w)&\ge qw &&(0\le w\le A),\label{prep:contact-first}\\ m(w)-m(A)&\ge t(w-A)&&(A\le w\le B),\label{prep:contact-middle}\\ m(w)-m(B)&\ge q(w-B)&&(B\le w\le1).\label{prep:contact-last} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: A profile above the source slope admits a short interior interval with stronger middle growth and compatible outer growth inequalities.
- **claim-one-scale-cap-improvement** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-one-scale)): Fix $k\ge2$, $k-\tfrac12<s<k$, $k-1\le q<k-\tfrac12$, and $\zeta>0$. Fix bounded support, separation, and $s$-Frostman constants, as well as a plate-bound constant $C$. There are $\varepsilon_0,c>0$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently small dyadic $\delta$. Let $\rho,\nu$ be probabilities with the fixed bounds, and let $G$ be a Borel pair set whose two directional measures satisfy, for $\delta\le u\le1$, $$\lambda(B(w,u))\le\delta^{-\varepsilon_0}u^q, \qquad \lambda\{w:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(w,V)\le u\}\le C u^\zeta.$$ Then one can delete pair mass at most $C\delta^c$ so that both remaining directional measures give every cap of radius $\delta$ mass at most $\delta^{q+c}$. The constants are uniform over the measures and pair sets with these bounds.
  - Explanation: With fixed geometry and Frostman bounds, a directional cap estimate below the desired exponent improves at a single scale after power-small pair deletion.
- **claim-thin-tube-availability-upgrade** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prep-thin-upgrade)): Let $k\ge2$ and $k-\tfrac12<s<k$. Let $\rho,\nu$ be bounded, separated $s$-Frostman probabilities in $\mathbb R^{k+1}$. Suppose that the two directional measures of $G_N$ have plate bounds $Cu^\zeta$, uniformly for $2^{-N}\le u\le1$, with fixed $\zeta>0$. If $k-1$ is available, then $k-\tfrac12$ is available.
  - Explanation: Availability of the integer directional exponent upgrades to the half-integer endpoint when separated Frostman laws and uniform plate bounds are supplied.
- **claim-classical-distance-threshold** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-classical-plane)): Let $\rho$ be a compactly supported probability in $\mathbb R^n$, $n\ge2$, with an $s$-ball bound for some $s>(n+1)/2$. Then the distances between points of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\rho$ form a set of positive Lebesgue measure.
  - Explanation: The classical spherical-average argument gives positive measure for distances when the source ball exponent exceeds the classical threshold.
- **claim-simultaneous-projected-ball-bounds** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-projections)): Let $E\subset\mathbb R^d$ be compact and $\dim_HE>S=d/2$. There is a probability on $E$ with a ball exponent strictly greater than $S$. Finitely many successive generic orthogonal projections to dimensions strictly greater than $S$ preserve finite energies with exponents strictly greater than $S$. After a positive compact restriction of the original probability, all these projected laws, as well as the original law, have a common ball exponent $S+\chi$, with $\chi>0$.* *If $d=2k-1$ and the original measure gives zero mass to every affine $k$-plane, the projection to $\mathbb R^{k+1}$ may additionally be chosen to give zero mass to every affine hyperplane.
  - Explanation: A common positive restriction supplies supercritical ball bounds in the original and finitely many projected spaces. In the stated odd-dimensional case the auxiliary law also avoids hyperplane mass.
- **claim-radial-integrability** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prep-radial-lp)): Let $h\ge1$ and $0<\epsilon<\min(h,1)$. Let $\alpha,\beta$ be bounded, separated probabilities in $\mathbb R^{h+1}$ with finite $I_{h+\epsilon}$ energies. For some $p>1$, the radial measures $(\pi_x)_\#\alpha$ have jointly measurable densities $F(x,w)$ and $$\int\!\int_{S^h}F(x,w)^p\,d\sigma(w)\,d\beta(x)<\infty.$$ The same assertion holds in the reverse orientation. Positive compact restrictions can be chosen on both sides so that the two radial $L^p$ norms are uniformly bounded at every remaining pin.
  - Explanation: Separated laws with the specified finite energies have jointly measurable radial densities with superlinear integrability. Positive restrictions make both orientations uniformly bounded at retained pins.
- **claim-integer-cap-pruning-and-lifting** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-integer-caps)): Under the uniform conclusion of Lemma <a href="#prep:radial-lp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:radial-lp">20</a>, for every $e>0$ and $R=2^N$ one can remove pair mass $O(R^{-e(p-1)/2})$ so that the two remaining directional measures give every cap of radius $u$ mass at most $R^eu^h$, for all $R^{-1}\le u\le1$ and all sufficiently large $R$.* *The same cap bound lifts through a fixed orthogonal projection from a higher-dimensional space, provided the original and projected cross supports are bounded and separated. Constants may change, and may be absorbed by a smaller preliminary choice of $e$.
  - Explanation: Truncating radial densities produces integer cap bounds after power-small pair deletion. Fixed orthogonal projection lifts these bounds when both original and projected cross supports are separated.
- **claim-decreasing-heavy-plate-filter** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-coarse-filter)): Let $k\ge2$, and let $\rho_1,\rho_2$ be bounded separated $s$-Frostman probabilities in $\mathbb R^{k+1}$, with $k-\tfrac12<s<k$. Suppose $\rho=\rho_1+\rho_2$ gives zero mass to every affine hyperplane. For every fixed $0<\omega\le1$, there are decreasing Borel sets $\Gamma_N\subset\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\rho_1\times\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\rho_2$ such that* 1. *$(\rho_1\otimes\rho_2)(\bigcap_N\Gamma_N)>0$;* 2. *$\Gamma_N$ is a union of pairs of cells in a fixed dyadic grid of depth at most $\omega N+O(1)$;* 3. *both directional measures of $\Gamma_N$ satisfy $\lambda\{w:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(w,V)\le u\}\le C_\omega u^\zeta$ for $2^{-N}\le u\le1$, with some $\zeta>0$.
  - Explanation: Successive coarse exclusions of heavy plates preserve positive limiting product mass and supply directional plate bounds in both orientations.
- **claim-separated-starting-pairs** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-starting-pairs)): Let $E\subset\mathbb R^d$ be compact, $d\ge2$, and $\dim_HE>S=d/2$. Either Lemma <a href="#prep:classical-plane" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:classical-plane">18</a> already proves that its distance set has positive Lebesgue measure, or the following construction is possible.* *After a similarity there are compact separated sets $E_1,E_2\subset E$ and probabilities $\mu_i$ supported on them, each satisfying $$\mu_i(B(x,u))\le C u^{S+\chi}$$ for a fixed $\chi>0$. Their cross directions lie in a fixed arbitrarily small spherical patch. For every sufficiently small fixed $\omega>0$, there are decreasing Borel pair sets $\Gamma_N\subset E_1\times E_2$ such that* 1. *$(\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)(\bigcap_N\Gamma_N)>0$;* 2. *$\Gamma_N$ is a disjoint union of at most $C2^{2d\omega N}$ products of spatial Borel sets;* 3. *for every $e>0$, there are $c(e)>0$, $C_e<\infty$ and Borel $B_N\subset\Gamma_N$ with $(\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)(B_N)\le C_e2^{-c(e)N}$, such that for $G_N=\Gamma_N\setminus B_N$, all $2^{-N}\le u\le1$, and all spherical caps $B(w,u)$, $$\begin{aligned} \mu_2\{y:(x,y)\in G_N,\ \pi_x(y)\in B(w,u)\} &\le2^{eN}u^S,\\ \mu_1\{x:(x,y)\in G_N,\ \pi_y(x)\in B(w,u)\} &\le2^{eN}u^S. \end{aligned}$$* *The last bounds integrate the original opposite marginal, without normalizing its individual fibers. The $B_N$ need not have a product partition or be nested. All constants are independent of $N$, but may depend on the fixed measures, $\omega$, and $e$.
  - Explanation: Either the classical affine-plane case concludes the argument, or separated original-space probabilities admit decreasing product-partitioned filters and additional scale-dependent deletions with critical cap bounds.
- **claim-excess-profile-and-conditional-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-test-profile-bounds)): There is a dimensional constant $C_d$ such that $$A_0=0,\qquad \|A_i-A_j\|\le d\|i-j\|/N,\qquad A_n\ge\chi n/N-C_d\sigma.$$ Every occupied depth-$n$ cube has mass $M(n)R^{\pm C_d\sigma}$. In particular, a ball of radius $C2^{-g}$ contains at most $$\label{test:centre-count} C'R^{I(g,p)+C_d\sigma}$$ occupied depth-$p$ cube centres, for $0\le g\le p\le N$. Here $C$ is any fixed constant and $C'$ may depend on $C$. For integers $0\le p\le t\le N$ and an occupied depth-$p$ cube $P$, the full conditional probability $\rho_P=\rho\|_P/\rho(P)$, rescaled by $z\mapsto2^p(z-c_P)$, satisfies the $S$-ball bound down to $H^{-1}$, where $H=2^{t-p}$, with loss $$\label{test:conditional-ball-loss} K=R^{A_p-\min_{p\le n\le t}A_n+C_d\sigma}.$$
  - Explanation: Regular class profiles control occupied cell counts and the critical ball loss of full conditional laws through the minimum excess profile on the relevant depth interval.
- **claim-critical-angular-fourier-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-test-half-dimensional-fourier)): Let $1\le H\le R$, $K,L\ge1$, and $S=d/2$. Suppose $\alpha$ is a probability in a fixed bounded subset of $\mathbb R^d$ with $$\alpha(B(x,r))\le Kr^S\qquad(H^{-1}\le r\le1),$$ and $\xi$ is a subprobability on $S^{d-1}$ with $$\xi(B(w,r))\le Lr^S\qquad(R^{-1}\le r\le1).$$ Then, with Fourier convention $\widehat\alpha(v)=\int e^{-iz\cdot v}\,d\alpha(z)$, $$\label{test:fourier-estimate} \int_{-H}^{H}\int_{S^{d-1}}\|\widehat\alpha(sw)\|^2\,d\xi(w)\,ds \le C LK(1+\log H).$$ The constant depends only on $d$ and the fixed bound on the support’s diameter. In particular it does not require ball bounds below $H^{-1}$ or any Fourier decay.
  - Explanation: Critical ball and cap exponents cancel the annular frequency power, giving only logarithmic growth without finer-scale ball information or Fourier decay.
- **claim-small-test-failure-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-test-exceptional-directions)): The constant $C_d$ in Definition <a href="#test:good-directions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:good-directions">26</a> can be chosen depending only on $d$ so that the following holds for all fixed parameters and sufficiently large $N$. If $\xi$ is any spherical subprobability satisfying $$\xi(B(w,r))\le R^\sigma r^S \qquad(R^{-1}\le r\le1),$$ then every level-zero test, uniformly in the class and the occupied anchor cube, has $$\label{test:exceptional-bound} \xi(S^{d-1}\setminus G_{0,\mathfrak t}(P))\le R^{-\sigma}.$$
  - Explanation: A dimensional choice of the test threshold makes the failure mass of every level-zero test power-small for an unnormalized spherical law with the stated cap bounds.
- **claim-buffered-smooth-angular-masks** (construction, [source](#stmt-test-smooth-masks)): For a descriptor $\mathfrak t$ of nominal length $l'$, put $$D_{l'}=\max(1,2^{l'}R^{-50\sigma}).$$ For all sufficiently large $N$, every $0\le i<J_0$ admits a mask $w_{i,\mathfrak t}(x,w)\in[0,1]$, measurable in $x$ and smooth in $w$, such that, on every occupied anchor cube, $$\label{test:mask-properties} w_{i,\mathfrak t}=1\text{ on }G_{i,\mathfrak t},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}_w w_{i,\mathfrak t}\subset G_{i+1,\mathfrak t},\qquad \\|T_1\cdots T_jw_{i,\mathfrak t}\\|_\infty\le C_jD_{l'}^j.$$ Here the $T_j$ are any members of a fixed finite collection of smooth tangent fields spanning the tangent spaces of the sphere. The masks are antipodally symmetric and constant as functions of $x$ on each anchor cube; they are zero on unoccupied cubes and outside the root. Moreover, the $cD_{l'}^{-1}$-neighbourhood of $G_{i,\mathfrak t}$ lies in $G_{i+1,\mathfrak t}$, with the same assertion for its closure after decreasing the fixed $c$.
  - Explanation: Buffered test levels admit smooth antipodally symmetric masks. Their spatial constancy and angular derivative bounds support later oscillatory manipulations.
- **claim-mask-product-support-and-derivatives** (bound, [source](#stmt-test-product-symbols)): Consider a product with one factor for each occurrence in one or more descriptor lists whose total size is at most $N^{C_*}$, with $C_*$ fixed independently of $N$. A factor may be a mask $w_{i,\mathfrak t}$ for any $0\le i<J_0$, or a derivative of order $q$ divided by $D_{l'}^q$, where $l'$ is its nominal length. The indices may differ among factors. Assume the total order of these prescribed derivatives is bounded independently of $N$. A bounded number, independent of $N$, of measurable spatial multipliers and smooth angular factors are also allowed, with fixed numerical bounds for their sup norms and for all angular derivatives under consideration. These bounds are common to the family of products in any later supremum. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length, with $L=0$ for an empty list, and set $$\label{test:total-symbol-scale} D_*=R^\sigma\max(1,2^LR^{-50\sigma}).$$ At each fixed angular derivative order $j$, the resulting product $b$ satisfies $$\\|T_1\cdots T_jb\\|_\infty\le C_jD_*^j.$$ For each factor with descriptor $\mathfrak t$ and index $i$, the support of the product still imposes its $G_{i+1,\mathfrak t}$ condition, including when that factor was already differentiated. All these assertions are pointwise in the spatial variables. Constants may depend on the fixed list-size exponent, prescribed derivative budget, and numerical bounds for the extra factors, as well as on the previously fixed parameters.
  - Explanation: Polynomial-size products with a fixed derivative budget retain every imposed good-direction condition and have controlled derivatives at each fixed order.
- **claim-nested-descriptor-origins** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-test-descriptor-properties)): For a nonterminal $[a,t]$, the union of the blocks eligible for its choice is an integer interval $[a_*,t_*]$ with $$a+q_0\le a_*\le a+2q_0,\qquad t-2q_0\le t_*\le t-q_0, \qquad A_n\ge A_p\quad(a_*\le n\le t_*).$$ The lists $\mathcal M(a,t)$ are nested under interval containment. Across the scheduled lists, there are $O(1/\kappa)$ possible anchors and polynomially many descriptors. For every $\mathfrak t\in\mathcal M(a,t)$ with anchor $p$ and nominal length $l'$, there is an origin $[a',t']\subseteq[a,t]$ such that $$\label{test:descriptor-origin} [p-l',p+l']\subseteq[a',t'].$$ That origin also supplies every parallel descriptor $[g,p]$ with $p-l'\le g<p$. In particular $l'\le(t-a)/2$.
  - Explanation: Fixed descriptor schedules nest under interval containment. Each membership has an origin that supplies both symmetric depth reach and the associated parallel tests.
- **claim-symbol-derivatives-and-reached-anchors** (bound, [source](#stmt-osc-symbol-bounds)): Let $L$ be the largest nominal length in the symbol’s list, or in all lists for a product of symbols, with $L=0$ when those lists are empty, and put $$\label{osc:derivative-scale} D_*=R^\sigma\max(1,2^L R^{-50\sigma}).$$ For every fixed $j$, an admissible symbol, or a product of a fixed number of endpoint symbols, obeys $$\label{osc:angular-derivatives} \sup_x\\|b_x\\|_{C^j(S^{d-1})}\le C_{j,B}D_*^j$$ for sufficiently large $N$. The constant may depend on the fixed list-size exponents, parameters, derivative budgets and common angular bounds, but not on the spatial multipliers. On a depth-$a$ cell, the product of all mask factors whose anchors have depth at most $a$ is independent of the spatial integration variable and has uniformly bounded modulus. We call these anchors *reached* at depth $a$.
  - Explanation: Admissible symbols have uniform angular derivative bounds, while reached mask factors become spatially constant on the current cell and have bounded modulus.
- **claim-weighted-distance-shell-conversion** (bound, [source](#stmt-osc-shell-conversion)): Let $0\le a\le v\le N$ be integer depths, and let $X,Y$ be separated cells at depth $a$ of one or two classes. Let $m_x(w)$ and $n_y(w)$ be products of the undifferentiated masks at one index $i<J_0$ for two specified indexed descriptor lists. Assume both lists have at most $N^{C_{\mathcal A}}$ occurrences, where one exponent $C_{\mathcal A}$ is fixed independently of $N$ for the whole family under consideration, as in Definition <a href="#osc:symbols" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:symbols">32</a>. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length in either list, with $L=0$ when both lists are empty. For measurable $\|f\|,\|g\|\le1$ define a possibly complex measure $\nu$ on the distance line by $$\label{osc:weighted-measure} \begin{aligned} \int F(s)\,d\nu(s) &=\frac1{M_1(a)M_2(a)} \int_{X\times Y} F(D(x,y))\, (D(x,y)2^a)^{-n_*/2}\\ &\qquad\qquad\cdot f(x)\overline{g(y)}\, m_x(e(x,y))n_y(e(x,y))\, d\rho_1(x)d\rho_2(y). \end{aligned}$$ If $$\label{osc:shell-regime} v-a\ge20\sigma N,\qquad L\le(v-a)/2+\sigma N,$$ then for every fixed $T>0$, $$\label{osc:shell-estimate} 2^{-a}\int_{2^{v-1}}^{2^{v+1}}\|\widehat\nu(r)\|^2\,dr \le R^{o(1)}\sup\mathsf H_F(a,v)+O(R^{-T}).$$ The supremum has the same cells, class normalizations, lists and smoothing index as in Equation <a href="#osc:weighted-measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="osc:weighted-measure">[osc:weighted-measure]</a>. Its symbols are admissible with a derivative budget and common bounds for smooth angular factors depending only on $T,d,C_{\mathcal A}$ and the fixed parameters. All these bounds are independent of $N$. When $f=g=1$, the measure $\nu$ is positive. The assertion also holds on the fixed outer supports with $a=0$.
  - Explanation: A distance weight cancels the leading stationary distance power, converting a Fourier shell into shared-angle energy while retaining the endpoint lists and fixed symbol bounds.
- **claim-direct-inverse-sphere-expansions** (bound, [source](#stmt-osc-stationary)): Suppose $b\in C^\infty(S^{n_*})$ obeys $\\|b\\|_{C^m}\le C_m A^m$ at the finitely many orders needed below, where $A\ge1$. Let $\Delta\ne0$, $e=\Delta/\|\Delta\|$, $\Lambda=r\|\Delta\|$, and $r>0$. A cutoff $\psi$ is assumed to equal one on a fixed neighborhood of $e$ and zero on a fixed neighborhood of $-e$, with uniformly bounded derivatives. For every integer $J\ge1$, $$\label{osc:direct} \begin{aligned} \int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\cdot\Delta}\psi(w)b(w)\,dw &=\Lambda^{-n_*/2} \left[e^{i\Lambda}\sum_{j=0}^{J-1} \Lambda^{-j}(L_jb)(e)+E_J\right],\\ \|E_J\|&\le C_J\Lambda^{-J}A^{2J+d+2}, \end{aligned}$$ provided $\Lambda\ge1$. Without a cutoff the corresponding formula is $$\label{osc:full-sphere} \int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\cdot\Delta}b(w)\,dw =\Lambda^{-n_*/2} \left[\sum_{\epsilon\in\{+1,-1\}}e^{i\epsilon\Lambda} \sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\Lambda^{-j} (L_{j,\epsilon}b)(\epsilon e)+E_J'\right],$$ with the same remainder bound. Define global differential operators $$\label{osc:B-operators} B_0=L_0^{-1},\qquad B_j=-L_0^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^j L_h B_{j-h}.$$ Each $B_j$ is a polynomial in $\Delta_{S^{n_*}}$ of differential order at most $2j$. If, for a fixed $c>0$, $$\label{osc:stationary-regime} \Lambda\ge R^{c\sigma},\qquad A^2/\Lambda\le R^{-c\sigma},\qquad 1\le A\le R^2,$$ then for every prescribed $T>0$ a fixed sufficiently large $J$ gives $$\label{osc:inverse} e^{i\Lambda}b(e) =\Lambda^{n_*/2}\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\Lambda^{-j} \int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\cdot\Delta}\psi(w)(B_jb)(w)\,dw +O(R^{-T}).$$ The extracted remainders in Equations <a href="#osc:direct" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="osc:direct">[osc:direct]</a> and <a href="#osc:full-sphere" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="osc:full-sphere">[osc:full-sphere]</a> are also $O(R^{-T})$ after increasing $J$. All constants are uniform in $e$ in the stipulated geometry.* *For $r=2^v s$ with $s$ in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$, each coefficient $\Lambda^{-j}(L_{j,\epsilon}b)(\epsilon e)$ has uniformly bounded derivatives of every fixed order in $s$ under Equation <a href="#osc:stationary-regime" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="osc:stationary-regime">[osc:stationary-regime]</a>. Every differential coefficient retains the supports of all the mask factors in $b$.
  - Explanation: Global spherical differential operators produce direct and inverse stationary expansions with controlled remainders. Differentiation preserves the support of every mask factor.
- **claim-separable-spatial-distance-power** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-osc-spatial-separation)): On separated depth-$a$ cells, for every fixed $j\ge0$ there are bounded measurable functions $f_\ell$ on $X$ and $g_\ell$ on $Y$ and constants $c_\ell$ such that $$\label{osc:spatial-series} (D(x,y)2^a)^{-j} =\sum_\ell c_\ell f_\ell(x)g_\ell(y),\qquad \|f_\ell\|,\|g_\ell\|\le1,\qquad \sum_\ell\|c_\ell\|\le C_{d,j}.$$ The convergence is uniform. The same assertion holds in the fixed outer geometry, with a constant depending on that geometry.
  - Explanation: On separated cells, a smooth inverse distance power has an absolutely summable expansion into bounded endpoint factors, preserving endpoint separation in the oscillatory representation.
- **claim-independent-angular-graph-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-graph-estimate)): Let $\rho_1,\rho_2$ be two regularized class laws, possibly equal, and write $M_j$, $A_{j,n}$, $I_j$ and $k_j$ for their profiles and associated quantities from Section <a href="#test:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:section">6</a>. Let $X,Y$ be occupied cells of depth $a$. Put $X_1=X$, $X_2=Y$, and let $b^{(j)}_z(w)$ be an admissible endpoint symbol on $X_j$ in the sense of Definition <a href="#osc:symbols" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:symbols">32</a>. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length of a descriptor in either symbol, with $L=0$ if both lists are empty. Suppose that the integer depths satisfy $0\le a<p\le v\le N$ and $$\label{graph:range} p-a\le v-p+2\sigma N,\qquad L\le v-p+2\sigma N,\qquad v-p\ge10\sigma N.$$ At endpoint $j$, suppose that the symbol imposes the parallel test $[g_j,p]$ at a level $\ell_j\in\{0,\ldots,J_0\}$: for $\rho_j$-almost every $z\in X_j$ and every $w\in S^{d-1}$, $$\label{graph:support-test} b^{(j)}_z(w)\ne0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad w\in G^{(j)}_{\ell_j,[g_j,p]}(z).$$ The superscript $j$ indicates the class used in the test. Assume $$\label{graph:anchor-range} a\le g_j<p,\qquad g_j\le a+2\sigma N.$$* *Form the general independent energy $\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v)$ of Definition <a href="#osc:states" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:states">34</a> using these specified symbols. For every fixed $T>0$, $$\label{graph:bound} \begin{split} \mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v) &\le R^{-\sum_{j=1}^2(A_{j,p}-A_{j,a}) +\min_{j=1,2}k_j(a,p)+O_d(\sigma)+o(1)}\\ &\qquad\cdot \max_{\substack{P\subset X,\ Q\subset Y\\ P,Q\text{ occupied depth-}p\text{ cells}}} \mathsf H^{P,Q}(p,v) +O(R^{-T}). \end{split}$$ No separation of $X$ and $Y$ is required. Every child in <a href="#graph:bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:bound">[graph:bound]</a> is formed by restricting the same endpoint symbols to $P$ and $Q$. It retains every original mask factor, including any whose anchor is $p$. Constants are uniform over cells and allowed symbols with fixed list-size exponents and derivative budgets, and with fixed common numerical bounds for their smooth angular factors at the derivative orders used. The $o(1)$ tends to zero as $N\to\infty$ with these bounds, the parameters, and $T$ fixed.
  - Explanation: Parallel tests and nonstationary localization bound independent angular energy by child cell energies, with the profile increment and smaller degree cost explicitly accounted for.
- **claim-oscillatory-graph-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-graph-localization)): Under <a href="#graph:range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:range">[graph:range]</a>, replacing the expanded independent energy by its sum over the retained edges changes it by $O(R^{-T})$, for every fixed $T>0$. This replacement is made in the integrated oscillatory form, before taking absolute values of the expanded cell terms.
  - Explanation: Nonretained graph terms are discarded in integrated oscillatory form with arbitrary power accuracy, before taking absolute values.
- **claim-retained-graph-degree-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-graph-degrees)): For each fixed cap pair, both the row degrees and the column degrees of the retained graph are at most $$\label{graph:degree-bound} D_{\mathrm{gr}}= R^{\min_{j=1,2}k_j(a,p)+O_d(\sigma)+o(1)}.$$ This estimate does not require the two cap directions to be separated.
  - Explanation: Both row and column degrees are bounded by parallel-test capacities; no angular separation between the cap directions is required.
- **claim-separated-form-graph-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-graph-separated-form)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#graph:estimate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="graph:estimate">38</a>, suppose also that $X,Y$ and their supported laws have the separated geometry used to define $\mathsf H_F^{X,Y}(a,v)$ in Section <a href="#osc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:section">7</a>. Then <a href="#graph:bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:bound">[graph:bound]</a> holds with its left-hand side replaced by $\mathsf H_F^{X,Y}(a,v)$ and with the same child energies. This includes the outer root $a=0$ with separated actual supports inside possibly equal depth-zero cells $X,Y$.
  - Explanation: Angular Cauchy–Schwarz transfers the graph estimate to the separated shared-angle form without altering endpoint symbols or child energies.
- **claim-positive-measure-binning** (bound, [source](#stmt-rec-binning)): Let $\eta,\zeta$ be finite positive measures on the line and let $\varepsilon>0$. Write $$B_\varepsilon(\eta) =\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}\eta([j\varepsilon,(j+1)\varepsilon))^2.$$ For any fixed $C\ge1$, $$\begin{aligned} B_\varepsilon(\eta) &\le(\eta\otimes\eta)\{\|s-s'\|\le\varepsilon\}, \label{rec:bins-lower}\\ \sup_{u\in\mathbb R} (\eta\otimes\eta)\{\|s-s'-u\|\le C\varepsilon\} &\le C' B_\varepsilon(\eta), \label{rec:bins-shift}\\ \iint(1+\|s-s'\|/\varepsilon)^{-2}\,d\eta(s)d\zeta(s') &\le C' B_\varepsilon(\eta)^{1/2} B_\varepsilon(\zeta)^{1/2}. \label{rec:bins-kernel} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $$\label{rec:collision-fourier} (\eta\otimes\eta)\{\|s-s'\|\le C\varepsilon\} \le C'\varepsilon \int_{\|r\|\le\varepsilon^{-1}}\|\widehat\eta(r)\|^2\,dr.$$
  - Explanation: Positive bin masses control unshifted and translated collisions, decaying cross kernels and a Fourier collision majorant without assuming measure densities.
- **claim-legal-recursive-state-reductions** (classification, [source](#stmt-rec-reductions)): Suppose the current state is nonterminal, its smoothing index satisfies $i<J_0$, and $N$ is sufficiently large with all parameters fixed. For every prescribed $T>0$, it is bounded, apart from an error $O(R^{-T})$ from oscillatory operations, by a sum of child bounds whose number is polynomial in $N$, and possibly a low-frequency stopping term. For every non-low child, if the old start is $a$ and the new start is $a'$, its multiplier is $$\label{rec:transition-factor} R^{-2(A_{a'}-A_a)+\mathrm{cost}+C_d\sigma+o(1)}.$$ A low child has cost zero and the entire multiplier $R^{o(1)}$, with no fixed $C_d\sigma$ loss. A low-frequency stopping term is $R^{C_d\sigma+o(1)}$. The legal children are* <div class="center"> \| *Reduction* \| *Parent and child* \| *Cost* \| \|:------------\|:------------------------------------------------------------------\|:------------------------------\| \| *Suffix* \| *$\mathsf D_i(a,t)\to\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,p)$* \| *$A_p-\min_{[p,t]}A$* \| \| *Low* \| *$\mathsf D_i(a,t)\to\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,v)$* \| *$0$; full factor $R^{o(1)}$* \| \| *High* \| *$\mathsf D_i(a,t)\to\mathsf H_i^{[a,t]}(p,v)$* \| *$k(a,p)$* \| \| *Direct* \| *$\mathsf H_i^{\mathcal B}(a,v)\to\mathsf H_i^{\mathcal B}(p,v)$* \| *$k(a,p)$* \| \| *Near* \| *$\mathsf H_i^{\mathcal B}(a,v)\to\mathsf H_i^{\mathcal B}(h,v)$* \| *$0$* \| \| *Far* \| *$\mathsf H_i^{\mathcal B}(a,v)\to\mathsf D_{i+1}(b,v)$* \| *$0$* \| </div> *For a positive parent, $p$ is its current interval’s fixed policy choice. The suffix row is used when $2p>a+t$. When $2p\le a+t$, the shell index is an integer $a<v\le t$: the low row has $t-v\ge\sigma N$, and the high row has $0\le t-v<\sigma N$. A high child has base $[a,t]$ and carries the fixed enlarged derivative budget and common angular bounds produced by the inverse conversion; these depend on $T$ and the fixed parameters, not on $N$.* *For an angular parent, use $L_0,h$ from Equation <a href="#rec:angular-split-depth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rec:angular-split-depth">[rec:angular-split-depth]</a>. If $h\le a$, the direct row chooses a remaining descriptor of maximal length $L$, its anchor $p$, and an origin $[b_0,u_0]\subseteq\mathcal B$ for that membership. This choice has $L=L_0$, $a<p<v$, $$b_0\le p-L,\qquad p+L\le u_0,\qquad v-a\le2L,$$ and it supplies the parallel descriptor $[\max(a,p-L),p]$ used by the graph estimate. If $h>a$, the near row uses the displayed depth $h$. The far rows range over integer depths $b$ arising from the comparable far displacements, with $a<b\le h+O_d(1)<v$, and use separated depth-$b$ cell pairs. Direct and near children keep the same base, index, derivative budget and common angular bounds. A far child has the fresh positive list $\mathcal M(b,v)$.* *Finite maxima over child cells use the stated geometry. Whenever admissible symbols vary, take a supremum over the symbol family just specified. An empty collection of child terms contributes zero. Constants outside powers of $R$ may depend on the fixed parameters, derivative budgets and angular bounds. The constants $C_d$ in exponent losses depend only on $d$. A terminal state is bounded by $$\label{rec:terminal} R^{C_d(\kappa+\sigma)+o(1)}.$$
  - Explanation: The stated positive and angular states reduce through legal suffix, low, high, direct, near and far children. Each transition preserves the specified tests, normalization and symbol budget.
- **claim-two-depth-potential-payment** (bound, [source](#stmt-ledger-payment)): If a reduction of Proposition <a href="#rec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rec:reductions">45</a> has a potentially positive cost and parent interval $J$ and child interval $J'$, then $\mathcal V(J')$ is defined and $$\label{ledger:payment-inequality} \mathrm{cost}\le\mathcal V(J')-\mathcal V(J).$$ For every zero-cost reduction whose child potential is defined, the same inequality holds.
  - Explanation: Replacing an admissible depth pays every potentially positive transition cost by an increase in the current interval's potential. Defined child potentials also handle zero-cost containment.
- **claim-finite-reduction-and-smoothing-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-ledger-termination)): Every branch of the state reductions has at most $20/\kappa$ non-low transitions and at most $1/\sigma$ low transitions, for sufficiently small fixed parameters and sufficiently large $N$. Along such a branch the smoothing index increases by at most $20/\kappa+1/\sigma$. If the initial index is zero, every index required by the iteration, including the next support level used by a mask, is less than $$J_0=\left\lceil100(1/\kappa+1/\sigma)+100\right\rceil.$$
  - Explanation: Every branch has bounded depth for fixed parameters, and the available smoothing levels suffice for iteration from the initial index.
- **claim-single-profile-angular-potential-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-ledger-bound)): Let $\mathsf H_0^{[c,N]}(c,N)$ be a recursive angular state of nonterminal width, as in Definition <a href="#rec:angular-state" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rec:angular-state">43</a>. Fix its initial derivative budget $B$ and its common numerical angular bounds $Q_j$, independently of $N$, as in Definition <a href="#osc:symbols" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:symbols">32</a>. Then $$\label{ledger:bound-equation} \mathsf H_0^{[c,N]}(c,N) \le R^{2A_c-\mathcal V([c,N]) +C_d(\kappa+\sigma/\kappa)+o(1)}.$$ This bound holds uniformly over its two occupied cells, including two copies of the same cell, and over its admissible bounded spatial multipliers and symbols with these fixed bounds. The $o(1)$ term may depend on $B$ and the $Q_j$.
  - Explanation: Iteration telescopes profile increments and potential payments, leaving an angular energy bound with fixed parameter losses and no persistent derivative-budget growth.
- **claim-low-cost-entry-depth** (construction, [source](#stmt-asm-entry)): Fix $0<\beta<\min(\chi/10,1/10)$. If $\sigma$ is sufficiently small in terms of $d,\chi,\beta$, then, for all sufficiently large $N$, the integer $$\label{asm:entry-definition} c=\max\{n\in\{0,\ldots,N\}: \min(A_{1,n},A_{2,n})\le\beta\}$$ is defined and satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \label{asm:entry-range} &\left\lfloor\frac{\beta N}{2d}\right\rfloor\le c<N/4,\\ \label{asm:entry-lower} &A_{j,n}\ge\beta-d/N \quad(j=1,2,\ c\le n\le N),\\ \label{asm:entry-cost} &\min_{j=1,2} k_j(0,c)\le\beta+C_d\sigma. \end{aligned}$$ If $\kappa$ is sufficiently small and $q_0=\lceil\kappa N\rceil$, the interval $[c,N]$ is nonterminal. Its potentials from Definition <a href="#ledger:potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ledger:potential">46</a> obey $$\label{asm:entry-potential} \mathcal V_j([c,N]) :=\min_{\substack{c\le u<v\le N\\v-u\ge10q_0}} (A_{j,u}+A_{j,v}) \ge2\beta-2d/N.$$
  - Explanation: The last depth where either excess profile is small gives a low entry cost while both subsequent profiles supply a larger potential saving.
- **claim-restored-mass-and-nonzero-limit** (bound, [source](#stmt-asm-retained-measures)): There is $\eta>0$, depending on the fixed parameters and starting measures, such that $$\label{asm:tv-approximation} 0\le\tau_N\le\alpha_N,\qquad \\|\alpha_N-\tau_N\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\lesssim2^{-\eta N}.$$ Moreover $\alpha_N$ decreases to the nonzero finite positive measure $$\label{asm:limit-measure} \alpha_\infty=D_\#\bigl(W\mathbf 1_{\Gamma_\infty} (\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)\bigr),\qquad \Gamma_\infty=\bigcap_N\Gamma_N.$$ All these measures are carried by the compact set of original distances $\Delta(E)$.
  - Explanation: Restoring original class masses yields positive approximants with power-small total variation error and a decreasing family with nonzero limit on the original distance set.
- **claim-summable-approximant-shell-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-asm-shell-decay)): The fixed parameters can be chosen, in the order $\beta,\kappa,\sigma,\omega$, so that some $\gamma>0$ satisfies $$\label{asm:shell-bound} \int_{2^{N-1}\le \|r\|\le2^{N+1}} \|\widehat{\tau_N}(r)\|^2\,dr \lesssim2^{-\gamma N}$$ for all sufficiently large $N$.
  - Explanation: Ordered parameter choices make the entry saving survive recursive losses and product-partition recombination, producing decaying Fourier shell energy.
- **claim-monotone-shell-absolute-continuity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-asm-monotone-shell)): Let $(\alpha_N)_{N\ge K}$ be finite positive Borel measures on $\mathbb R$ with $\alpha_{N+1}\le\alpha_N$, and let $\alpha_\infty=\lim_N\alpha_N$. Let $\tau_N$ be finite complex Borel measures such that $$\label{asm:criterion-hypotheses} \sum_{N\ge K}\\|\alpha_N-\tau_N\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}<\infty, \qquad \sum_{N\ge K} \left(\int_{2^{N-1}\le \|r\|\le2^{N+1}} \|\widehat{\tau_N}(r)\|^2\,dr\right)^{1/2}<\infty.$$ Then $\alpha_\infty$ is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure. In particular, the conclusion holds if the two quantities inside these sums are respectively $O(2^{-\eta N})$ and $O(2^{-\gamma N/2})$ for arbitrary $\eta,\gamma>0$.
  - Explanation: A decreasing positive family has an absolutely continuous limit when approximation errors and Fourier shell norm bounds are summable. No quantitative rate of monotone convergence is needed.
- **claim-original-distance-measure-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-asm-completion)): For the starting pair in Proposition <a href="#prep:starting-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:starting-pairs">23</a>, there is a nonzero finite positive measure, absolutely continuous with respect to $\mathcal L^1$, carried by the original distance set $\Delta(E)$. Consequently $\mathcal L^1(\Delta(E))>0$.
  - Explanation: The limiting measure is nonzero, absolutely continuous and carried by original Euclidean distances, so the original distance set has positive Lebesgue measure.

{

      },

      "exact_statement": "Let $d\\ge2$ be an integer and let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact. If $\\dim_H E>d/2$, then $$\\mathcal L^1(\\Delta(E))>0.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-conv-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be supported in a bounded subset of $\\mathbb R^d$.*\n\n1.  *If $\\mu(B(z,r))\\le C r^s$ for $0<r\\le1$, then $I_t(\\mu)<\\infty$ for every $0<t<s$.*\n\n2.  *If $I_t(\\mu)<\\infty$, then for every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is a compact restriction of mass at least $\\mu(\\mathbb R^d)-\\varepsilon$ that satisfies a $t$-ball bound with a finite constant.*\n\n3.  *If $0<t<m\\le d$, $I_t(\\mu)<\\infty$, and $P_V$ is orthogonal projection to a Haar-random $m$-plane, then $I_t((P_V)_\\#\\mu)<\\infty$ for almost every $V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-conv-uniformity",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed background parameters, let $\\mathcal C(\\varepsilon,R)$ be classes of data, increasing in the allowed loss $\\varepsilon>0$. Let $\\mathcal G(c,R)$ be conclusions, weakening as the gain $c>0$ decreases. Suppose that for every sequence $$\\varepsilon_j\\downarrow0,\\qquad R_j\\to\\infty,\\qquad\n X_j\\in\\mathcal C(\\varepsilon_j,R_j),$$ there are a subsequence and a fixed $c>0$ on which $X_j$ satisfies $\\mathcal G(c,R_j)$ for all sufficiently large $j$. Then there exist $\\varepsilon_0,c_0>0$ and $R_0<\\infty$ such that every $X\\in\\mathcal C(\\varepsilon_0,R)$, $R\\ge R_0$, satisfies $\\mathcal G(c_0,R)$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-reg-regularization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$, let $\\rho$ be a probability on a unit dyadic cube in $\\mathbb R^n$, and put $R=2^N$. For every fixed $0<\\lambda<1$ and all sufficiently large $N$, there are pairwise disjoint sets $E_\\alpha$, each a union of depth-$N$ cubes, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *Their number is at most $N^{C_n/\\lambda}$, each has $\\rho(E_\\alpha)\\ge R^{-\\lambda}$, and $$\\rho\\Bigl(\\mathbb R^n\\setminus\\bigcup_\\alpha E_\\alpha\\Bigr)\n          \\le R^{-2}+N^{C_n/\\lambda}R^{-\\lambda}\n          =R^{-\\lambda+o(1)}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For $\\rho_\\alpha=\\rho|_{E_\\alpha}/\\rho(E_\\alpha)$ there is a nondecreasing $n$-Lipschitz function $m_\\alpha:[0,1]\\to[0,n]$, with $m_\\alpha(0)=0$, such that every occupied depth-$j$ cube satisfies $$\\label{reg:cell-mass}\n          R^{-m_\\alpha(j/N)-C_n\\lambda}\n          \\le\\rho_\\alpha(Q)\\le\n          R^{-m_\\alpha(j/N)+C_n\\lambda},\\qquad 0\\le j\\le N.$$*\n\n\n3.  *If $\\rho(B(x,r))\\le Kr^s$ for $R^{-1}\\le r\\le1$, with $0\\le s\\le n$ and $K\\ge1$, then $$\\label{reg:profile-lower-bound}\n          m_\\alpha(u)\\ge su-C_n\\lambda-\\log_R K,\n          \\qquad 0\\le u\\le1.$$*\n\n\n*Here and below an occupied cube means a cube of positive mass for the specified class. In particular, for occupied $P\\subset Q$ at depths $b\\ge a$, $$\\label{reg:conditional-mass}\n\\frac{\\rho_\\alpha(P)}{\\rho_\\alpha(Q)}\n =R^{-[m_\\alpha(b/N)-m_\\alpha(a/N)]\\pm C_n\\lambda}.$$ Constants depend only on $n$; the threshold for $N$ can depend on $\\lambda$.",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-reg-regularization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$, let $\\rho$ be a probability on a unit dyadic cube in $\\mathbb R^n$, and put $R=2^N$. In particular, for occupied $P\\subset Q$ at depths $b\\ge a$, $$\\label{reg:conditional-mass}\n\\frac{\\rho_\\alpha(P)}{\\rho_\\alpha(Q)}\n =R^{-[m_\\alpha(b/N)-m_\\alpha(a/N)]\\pm C_n\\lambda}.$$ Constants depend only on $n$; the threshold for $N$ can depend on $\\lambda$.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $n\\ge1$, let $\\rho$ be a probability on a unit dyadic cube in $\\mathbb R^n$, and put $R=2^N$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-reg-pruning-multiplication",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho$ be a source probability and $\\nu$ a finite pin measure, both on $\\mathbb R^n$, and let $0\\le a_0<\\cdots<a_J\\le N$ be a fixed finite collection of dyadic depths. Suppose pin–source distances on the sets in question are between fixed positive constants. Let $G'$ be a measurable set of pairs. For each $j<J$, suppose there is a measurable set $E_j\\supset G'$ and a number $f_j\\ge0$ such that every $(x,z)\\in G'$, with $z\\in Q$ at depth $a_j$, satisfies $$\\label{reg:local-fibre}\n\\rho\\{z'\\in Q\\cap(E_j)_x:\n     |\\pi_x(z')-\\pi_x(z)|\\le C2^{-a_{j+1}}\\}\n \\le f_j\\rho(Q).$$ The constant $C$ may be any sufficiently large fixed constant, depending on the geometry and on the fixed angular width to be used below. For every $e>0$ one can delete pair mass at most $(J+1)R^{-e}\\nu(\\mathbb R^n)$ from $G'$ and obtain $G''$ such that, for each pin $x$, each initial cube $Q_0$ at depth $a_0$, and every spherical ball $B$ of radius $C_12^{-a_J}$, $$\\label{reg:multiplied-fibre}\n\\rho\\{z\\in Q_0\\cap G''_x:\\pi_x(z)\\in B\\}\n \\le R^{eJ}\\Bigl(\\prod_{j=0}^{J-1}f_j\\Bigr)\\rho(Q_0).$$ An identical conclusion holds without a lower pin–source separation if Equation <a href=\"#reg:local-fibre\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:local-fibre\">[reg:local-fibre]</a> instead tests the perpendicular distance to the full line $x+\\mathbb R(z-x)$ at width $C2^{-a_{j+1}}$, provided pin and source sets remain bounded and pairs on the diagonal have been removed.",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-reg-pruning-multiplication",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho$ be a source probability and $\\nu$ a finite pin measure, both on $\\mathbb R^n$, and let $0\\le a_0<\\cdots<a_J\\le N$ be a fixed finite collection of dyadic depths. Suppose pin–source distances on the sets in question are between fixed positive constants. Let $G'$ be a measurable set of pairs. For each $j<J$, suppose there is a measurable set $E_j\\supset G'$ and a number $f_j\\ge0$ such that every $(x,z)\\in G'$, with $z\\in Q$ at depth $a_j$, satisfies $$\\label{reg:local-fibre}\n\\rho\\{z'\\in Q\\cap(E_j)_x:\n     |\\pi_x(z')-\\pi_x(z)|\\le C2^{-a_{j+1}}\\}\n \\le f_j\\rho(Q).$$ The constant $C$ may be any sufficiently large fixed constant, depending on the geometry and on the fixed angular width to be used below. For every $e>0$ one can delete pair mass at most $(J+1)R^{-e}\\nu(\\mathbb R^n)$ from $G'$ and obtain $G''$ such that, for each pin $x$, each initial cube $Q_0$ at depth $a_0$, and every spherical ball $B$ of radius $C_12^{-a_J}$, $$\\label{reg:multiplied-fibre}\n\\rho\\{z\\in Q_0\\cap G''_x:\\pi_x(z)\\in B\\}\n \\le R^{eJ}\\Bigl(\\prod_{j=0}^{J-1}f_j\\Bigr)\\rho(Q_0).$$ An identical conclusion holds without a lower pin–source separation if Equation <a href=\"#reg:local-fibre\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:local-fibre\">[reg:local-fibre]</a> instead tests the perpendicular distance to the full line $x+\\mathbb R(z-x)$ at width $C2^{-a_{j+1}}$, provided pin and source sets remain bounded and pairs on the diagonal have been removed.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\rho$ be a source probability and $\\nu$ a finite pin measure, both on $\\mathbb R^n$, and let $0\\le a_0<\\cdots<a_J\\le N$ be a fixed finite collection of dyadic depths. Suppose pin–source distances on the sets in question are between fixed positive constants. Let $G'$ be a measurable set of pairs. For each $j<J$, suppose there is a measurable set $E_j\\supset G'$ and a number $f_j\\ge0$ such that every $(x,z)\\in G'$, with $z\\in Q$ at depth $a_j$, satisfies $$\\label{reg:local-fibre}\n\\rho\\{z'\\in Q\\cap(E_j)_x:\n     |\\pi_x(z')-\\pi_x(z)|\\le C2^{-a_{j+1}}\\}\n \\le f_j\\rho(Q).$$ The constant $C$ may be any sufficiently large fixed constant, depending on the geometry and on the fixed angular width to be used below. For every $e>0$ one can delete pair mass at most $(J+1)R^{-e}\\nu(\\mathbb R^n)$ from $G'$ and obtain $G''$ such that, for each pin $x$, each initial cube $Q_0$ at depth $a_0$, and every spherical ball $B$ of radius $C_12^{-a_J}$, $$\\label{reg:multiplied-fibre}\n\\rho\\{z\\in Q_0\\cap G''_x:\\pi_x(z)\\in B\\}\n \\le R^{eJ}\\Bigl(\\prod_{j=0}^{J-1}f_j\\Bigr)\\rho(Q_0).$$ An identical conclusion holds without a lower pin–source separation if Equation <a href=\"#reg:local-fibre\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:local-fibre\">[reg:local-fibre]</a> instead tests the perpendicular distance to the full line $x+\\mathbb R(z-x)$ at width $C2^{-a_{j+1}}$, provided pin and source sets remain bounded and pairs on the diagonal have been removed.",
      "explanation": "Deleting low-density witnesses lets local directional bounds multiply through selected depths. The density test concerns the pair relation before the final deletion.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ang-linear-gain",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\ge2$ and $$k-1\\le q\\le k-\\tfrac12<t<k.$$ Fix a tube-width constant $C$. In the case $q=k-1$, also fix $\\zeta>0$. There exist $\\epsilon,c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\\delta$, the following holds. Let $\\mu$ be a bounded point subprobability in $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$ and $\\nu$ a direction subprobability on $S^k$ with respective $t$- and $q$-ball bounds of loss $\\delta^{-\\epsilon}$ down to $\\delta$. At $q=k-1$ assume in addition $$\\label{ang:endpoint-plate}\n \\nu\\{w:\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,H)\\le r\\}\\le\\delta^{-\\epsilon}r^\\zeta\n       \\qquad(\\delta\\le r\\le1)$$ for every linear hyperplane $H$. Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^{k+1}\\times S^k$ be a measurable incidence relation, and write $E_w=\\{x:(x,w)\\in E\\}$. If each direction fiber $E_w$ is covered by at most $\\delta^{-q-\\epsilon}$ tubes parallel to $w$ of width $C\\delta$, then $$(\\mu\\otimes\\nu)(E)\\le\\delta^c.$$ All constants are uniform over the measures and the incidence relations with these fixed parameters.",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-linear-gain",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\ge2$ and $$k-1\\le q\\le k-\\tfrac12<t<k.$$ Fix a tube-width constant $C$. In the case $q=k-1$, also fix $\\zeta>0$. There exist $\\epsilon,c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\\delta$, the following holds. Let $\\mu$ be a bounded point subprobability in $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$ and $\\nu$ a direction subprobability on $S^k$ with respective $t$- and $q$-ball bounds of loss $\\delta^{-\\epsilon}$ down to $\\delta$. At $q=k-1$ assume in addition $$\\label{ang:endpoint-plate}\n \\nu\\{w:\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,H)\\le r\\}\\le\\delta^{-\\epsilon}r^\\zeta\n       \\qquad(\\delta\\le r\\le1)$$ for every linear hyperplane $H$. Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^{k+1}\\times S^k$ be a measurable incidence relation, and write $E_w=\\{x:(x,w)\\in E\\}$. If each direction fiber $E_w$ is covered by at most $\\delta^{-q-\\epsilon}$ tubes parallel to $w$ of width $C\\delta$, then $$(\\mu\\otimes\\nu)(E)\\le\\delta^c.$$ All constants are uniform over the measures and the incidence relations with these fixed parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $k\\ge2$ and $$k-1\\le q\\le k-\\tfrac12<t<k.$$ Fix a tube-width constant $C$. In the case $q=k-1$, also fix $\\zeta>0$. There exist $\\epsilon,c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\\delta$, the following holds. Let $\\mu$ be a bounded point subprobability in $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$ and $\\nu$ a direction subprobability on $S^k$ with respective $t$- and $q$-ball bounds of loss $\\delta^{-\\epsilon}$ down to $\\delta$. At $q=k-1$ assume in addition $$\\label{ang:endpoint-plate}\n \\nu\\{w:\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,H)\\le r\\}\\le\\delta^{-\\epsilon}r^\\zeta\n       \\qquad(\\delta\\le r\\le1)$$ for every linear hyperplane $H$. Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^{k+1}\\times S^k$ be a measurable incidence relation, and write $E_w=\\{x:(x,w)\\in E\\}$. If each direction fiber $E_w$ is covered by at most $\\delta^{-q-\\epsilon}$ tubes parallel to $w$ of width $C\\delta$, then $$(\\mu\\otimes\\nu)(E)\\le\\delta^c.$$ All constants are uniform over the measures and the incidence relations with these fixed parameters.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<b<1$ and $b<f<2$. There is no sequence $\\delta\\to0$ with the following data.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ang-haar",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le h\\le n-1$, and let $V$ be a Haar-random codimension-$h$ linear subspace of $\\mathbb R^n$. For a unit vector $w$, $$\\Pr\\{\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,V)\\le\\delta\\}\\asymp\\delta^h.$$ If $j\\le n-h$ unit vectors $w_1,\\ldots,w_j$ have singular values $d_1\\ge\\cdots\\ge d_j\\ge0$, then $$\\label{ang:haar-multiple}\n \\delta^{-jh}\\Pr\\{\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w_i,V)\\le\\delta\\text{ for all }i\\}\n       \\le C_{n,j}\\prod_{i=1}^j(d_i+\\delta)^{-h}.$$ When $h=0$, the probability and the factors with exponent $h$ are one.",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-haar",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le h\\le n-1$, and let $V$ be a Haar-random codimension-$h$ linear subspace of $\\mathbb R^n$.           "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-ang-slice-selection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le h\\le n-2$. Let point and direction subprobabilities $\\mu,\\nu$ in $\\mathbb R^n$ have $t$- and $q$-ball bounds with subpower losses, where $t,q>h$. Assume the directions lie in a fixed small projective chart. Let an incidence relation $E$ have mass at least subpower, and suppose each direction fiber is covered by at most $\\delta^{-q-o(1)}$ tubes of width $C\\delta$.*\n\n*For Haar-random codimension-$h$ subspaces $V$ and $z\\in V^\\perp$, set $$\\begin{aligned}\n d\\nu_V(w)&=\\delta^{-h}\\mathbf 1_{\\{|P_{V^\\perp}w|\\le\\delta\\}}\\,d\\nu(w),\\\\\n d\\mu_{V,z}(x)&=\\delta^{-h}\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{|P_{V^\\perp}x-z|\\le\\delta\\}}\\,d\\mu(x).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Integrate $z$ over a fixed sufficiently large ball. There are families of restrictions $\\widetilde\\nu_V\\le\\nu_V$, $\\widetilde\\mu_{V,z}\\le\\mu_{V,z}$, and relations $\\widetilde E_{V,z}\\subset E$, measurable in the displayed parameters and restricted to the two bands defining $\\mu_{V,z}$ and $\\nu_V$, such that $$\\mathbb E_V\\int\n (\\widetilde\\mu_{V,z}\\otimes\\widetilde\\nu_V)(\\widetilde E_{V,z})\\,dz\n \\ge\\delta^{o(1)},$$ and the following hold:*\n\n1.  *The restricted measures have masses and ball losses at most $\\delta^{-o(1)}$, with exponents $q-h$ and $t-h$, respectively.*\n\n2.  *Each remaining direction fiber is covered by at most $\\delta^{-(q-h)-o(1)}$ width-$C'\\delta$ tubes.*\n\n3.  *Put $$p_{V,z}(x)=P_Vx,\\qquad v_V(w)=P_Vw/|P_Vw|\n     \\quad\\text{for }|P_{V^\\perp}w|\\le\\delta.$$ The first map is projection to $V+z$ followed by translation to $V$; the second is well-defined on the direction band for small $\\delta$. The coordinate pushforwards of the restricted measures under these maps satisfy the ball bounds in item 1, with fixed constant changes. For every original direction label $w$ in that band, the coordinates $p_{V,z}(\\{x:(x,w)\\in\\widetilde E_{V,z}\\})$ have the tube cover in item 2 with direction $v_V(w)$. This cover remains attached to $w$ even when two projected direction coordinates coincide.*\n\n*The direction restriction $\\widetilde\\nu_V$ depends on $V$ only, and its mass has an a priori subpower upper bound valid for every $V$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-ang-loomis-whitney",
        "exact_quote": "Let $v_1,\\ldots,v_m$ be unit vectors in $\\mathbb R^m$, $m\\ge2$, and suppose $|\\det(v_1,\\ldots,v_m)|\\ge\\Delta>0$.         "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\ge2$, $k-\\tfrac12<s<k$, $k-1\\le q<k-\\tfrac12$, and $\\zeta>0$. Fix bounded support, separation, and $s$-Frostman constants, as well as a plate-bound constant $C$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-projections",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact and $\\dim_HE>S=d/2$. There is a probability on $E$ with a ball exponent strictly greater than $S$. Finitely many successive generic orthogonal projections to dimensions strictly greater than $S$ preserve finite energies with exponents strictly greater than $S$. After a positive compact restriction of the original probability, all these projected laws, as well as the original law, have a common ball exponent $S+\\chi$, with $\\chi>0$.*\n\n*If $d=2k-1$ and the original measure gives zero mass to every affine $k$-plane, the projection to $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$ may additionally be chosen to give zero mass to every affine hyperplane.",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact and $\\dim_HE>S=d/2$. There is a probability on $E$ with a ball exponent strictly greater than $S$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-starting-pairs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact, $d\\ge2$, and $\\dim_HE>S=d/2$. Either Lemma <a href=\"#prep:classical-plane\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prep:classical-plane\">18</a> already proves that its distance set has positive Lebesgue measure, or the following construction is possible.*\n\n*After a similarity there are compact separated sets $E_1,E_2\\subset E$ and probabilities $\\mu_i$ supported on them, each satisfying $$\\mu_i(B(x,u))\\le C u^{S+\\chi}$$ for a fixed $\\chi>0$. Their cross directions lie in a fixed arbitrarily small spherical patch. For every sufficiently small fixed $\\omega>0$, there are decreasing Borel pair sets $\\Gamma_N\\subset E_1\\times E_2$ such that*\n\n1.  *$(\\mu_1\\otimes\\mu_2)(\\bigcap_N\\Gamma_N)>0$;*\n\n2.  *$\\Gamma_N$ is a disjoint union of at most $C2^{2d\\omega N}$ products of spatial Borel sets;*\n\n3.  *for every $e>0$, there are $c(e)>0$, $C_e<\\infty$ and Borel $B_N\\subset\\Gamma_N$ with $(\\mu_1\\otimes\\mu_2)(B_N)\\le C_e2^{-c(e)N}$, such that for $G_N=\\Gamma_N\\setminus B_N$, all $2^{-N}\\le u\\le1$, and all spherical caps $B(w,u)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     \\mu_2\\{y:(x,y)\\in G_N,\\ \\pi_x(y)\\in B(w,u)\\}\n         &\\le2^{eN}u^S,\\\\\n     \\mu_1\\{x:(x,y)\\in G_N,\\ \\pi_y(x)\\in B(w,u)\\}\n         &\\le2^{eN}u^S.\n    \\end{aligned}$$*\n\n*The last bounds integrate the original opposite marginal, without normalizing its individual fibers. The $B_N$ need not have a product partition or be nested. All constants are independent of $N$, but may depend on the fixed measures, $\\omega$, and $e$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-test-profile-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "There is a dimensional constant $C_d$ such that $$A_0=0,\\qquad |A_i-A_j|\\le d|i-j|/N,\\qquad\nA_n\\ge\\chi n/N-C_d\\sigma.$$ Every occupied depth-$n$ cube has mass $M(n)R^{\\pm C_d\\sigma}$. In particular, a ball of radius $C2^{-g}$ contains at most $$\\label{test:centre-count}\nC'R^{I(g,p)+C_d\\sigma}$$ occupied depth-$p$ cube centres, for $0\\le g\\le p\\le N$. Here $C$ is any fixed constant and $C'$ may depend on $C$. For integers $0\\le p\\le t\\le N$ and an occupied depth-$p$ cube $P$, the full conditional probability $\\rho_P=\\rho|_P/\\rho(P)$, rescaled by $z\\mapsto2^p(z-c_P)$, satisfies the $S$-ball bound down to $H^{-1}$, where $H=2^{t-p}$, with loss $$\\label{test:conditional-ball-loss}\nK=R^{A_p-\\min_{p\\le n\\le t}A_n+C_d\\sigma}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-test-half-dimensional-fourier",
        "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le H\\le R$, $K,L\\ge1$, and $S=d/2$. Suppose $\\alpha$ is a probability in a fixed bounded subset of $\\mathbb R^d$ with $$\\alpha(B(x,r))\\le Kr^S\\qquad(H^{-1}\\le r\\le1),$$ and $\\xi$ is a subprobability on $S^{d-1}$ with $$\\xi(B(w,r))\\le Lr^S\\qquad(R^{-1}\\le r\\le1).$$ Then, with Fourier convention $\\widehat\\alpha(v)=\\int e^{-iz\\cdot v}\\,d\\alpha(z)$, $$\\label{test:fourier-estimate}\n\\int_{-H}^{H}\\int_{S^{d-1}}|\\widehat\\alpha(sw)|^2\\,d\\xi(w)\\,ds\n \\le C LK(1+\\log H).$$ The constant depends only on $d$ and the fixed bound on the support’s diameter. In particular it does not require ball bounds below $H^{-1}$ or any Fourier decay.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-test-exceptional-directions",
        "exact_quote": "The constant $C_d$ in Definition <a href=\"#test:good-directions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"test:good-directions\">26</a> can be chosen depending only on $d$ so that the following holds for all fixed parameters and sufficiently large $N$. If $\\xi$ is any spherical subprobability satisfying $$\\xi(B(w,r))\\le R^\\sigma r^S\n       \\qquad(R^{-1}\\le r\\le1),$$ then every level-zero test, uniformly in the class and the occupied anchor cube, has $$\\label{test:exceptional-bound}\n\\xi(S^{d-1}\\setminus G_{0,\\mathfrak t}(P))\\le R^{-\\sigma}.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-test-smooth-masks",
        "exact_quote": "For a descriptor $\\mathfrak t$ of nominal length $l'$, put $$D_{l'}=\\max(1,2^{l'}R^{-50\\sigma}).$$ For all sufficiently large $N$, every $0\\le i<J_0$ admits a mask $w_{i,\\mathfrak t}(x,w)\\in[0,1]$, measurable in $x$ and smooth in $w$, such that, on every occupied anchor cube, $$\\label{test:mask-properties}\nw_{i,\\mathfrak t}=1\\text{ on }G_{i,\\mathfrak t},\\qquad\n\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}_w w_{i,\\mathfrak t}\\subset G_{i+1,\\mathfrak t},\\qquad\n\\|T_1\\cdots T_jw_{i,\\mathfrak t}\\|_\\infty\\le C_jD_{l'}^j.$$ Here the $T_j$ are any members of a fixed finite collection of smooth tangent fields spanning the tangent spaces of the sphere. The masks are antipodally symmetric and constant as functions of $x$ on each anchor cube; they are zero on unoccupied cubes and outside the root. Moreover, the $cD_{l'}^{-1}$-neighbourhood of $G_{i,\\mathfrak t}$ lies in $G_{i+1,\\mathfrak t}$, with the same assertion for its closure after decreasing the fixed $c$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-test-product-symbols",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a product with one factor for each occurrence in one or more descriptor lists whose total size is at most $N^{C_*}$, with $C_*$ fixed independently of $N$. A factor may be a mask $w_{i,\\mathfrak t}$ for any $0\\le i<J_0$, or a derivative of order $q$ divided by $D_{l'}^q$, where $l'$ is its nominal length. The indices may differ among factors. Assume the total order of these prescribed derivatives is bounded independently of $N$. A bounded number, independent of $N$, of measurable spatial multipliers and smooth angular factors are also allowed, with fixed numerical bounds for their sup norms and for all angular derivatives under consideration. These bounds are common to the family of products in any later supremum. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length, with $L=0$ for an empty list, and set $$\\label{test:total-symbol-scale}\nD_*=R^\\sigma\\max(1,2^LR^{-50\\sigma}).$$ At each fixed angular derivative order $j$, the resulting product $b$ satisfies $$\\|T_1\\cdots T_jb\\|_\\infty\\le C_jD_*^j.$$ For each factor with descriptor $\\mathfrak t$ and index $i$, the support of the product still imposes its $G_{i+1,\\mathfrak t}$ condition, including when that factor was already differentiated. All these assertions are pointwise in the spatial variables. Constants may depend on the fixed list-size exponent, prescribed derivative budget, and numerical bounds for the extra factors, as well as on the previously fixed parameters.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-test-descriptor-properties",
        "exact_quote": "For a nonterminal $[a,t]$, the union of the blocks eligible for its choice is an integer interval $[a_*,t_*]$ with $$a+q_0\\le a_*\\le a+2q_0,\\qquad\nt-2q_0\\le t_*\\le t-q_0,\n\\qquad A_n\\ge A_p\\quad(a_*\\le n\\le t_*).$$ The lists $\\mathcal M(a,t)$ are nested under interval containment. Across the scheduled lists, there are $O(1/\\kappa)$ possible anchors and polynomially many descriptors. For every $\\mathfrak t\\in\\mathcal M(a,t)$ with anchor $p$ and nominal length $l'$, there is an origin $[a',t']\\subseteq[a,t]$ such that $$\\label{test:descriptor-origin}\n[p-l',p+l']\\subseteq[a',t'].$$ That origin also supplies every parallel descriptor $[g,p]$ with $p-l'\\le g<p$. In particular $l'\\le(t-a)/2$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-osc-symbol-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be the largest nominal length in the symbol’s list, or in all lists for a product of symbols, with $L=0$ when those lists are empty, and put $$\\label{osc:derivative-scale}\n D_*=R^\\sigma\\max(1,2^L R^{-50\\sigma}).$$ For every fixed $j$, an admissible symbol, or a product of a fixed number of endpoint symbols, obeys $$\\label{osc:angular-derivatives}\n \\sup_x\\|b_x\\|_{C^j(S^{d-1})}\\le C_{j,B}D_*^j$$ for sufficiently large $N$. The constant may depend on the fixed list-size exponents, parameters, derivative budgets and common angular bounds, but not on the spatial multipliers. On a depth-$a$ cell, the product of all mask factors whose anchors have depth at most $a$ is independent of the spatial integration variable and has uniformly bounded modulus. We call these anchors *reached* at depth $a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-osc-symbol-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be the largest nominal length in the symbol’s list, or in all lists for a product of symbols, with $L=0$ when those lists are empty, and put $$\\label{osc:derivative-scale}\n D_*=R^\\sigma\\max(1,2^L R^{-50\\sigma}).$$ For every fixed $j$, an admissible symbol, or a product of a fixed number of endpoint symbols, obeys $$\\label{osc:angular-derivatives}\n \\sup_x\\|b_x\\|_{C^j(S^{d-1})}\\le C_{j,B}D_*^j$$ for sufficiently large $N$. The constant may depend on the fixed list-size exponents, parameters, derivative budgets and common angular bounds, but not on the spatial multipliers. On a depth-$a$ cell, the product of all mask factors whose anchors have depth at most $a$ is independent of the spatial integration variable and has uniformly bounded modulus. We call these anchors *reached* at depth $a$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
