    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-osc-shell-conversion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le a\\le v\\le N$ be integer depths, and let $X,Y$ be separated cells at depth $a$ of one or two classes. Let $m_x(w)$ and $n_y(w)$ be products of the undifferentiated masks at one index $i<J_0$ for two specified indexed descriptor lists. Assume both lists have at most $N^{C_{\\mathcal A}}$ occurrences, where one exponent $C_{\\mathcal A}$ is fixed independently of $N$ for the whole family under consideration, as in Definition <a href=\"#osc:symbols\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:symbols\">32</a>. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length in either list, with $L=0$ when both lists are empty. For measurable $|f|,|g|\\le1$ define a possibly complex measure $\\nu$ on the distance line by $$\\label{osc:weighted-measure}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\int F(s)\\,d\\nu(s)\n &=\\frac1{M_1(a)M_2(a)}\n   \\int_{X\\times Y} F(D(x,y))\\,\n       (D(x,y)2^a)^{-n_*/2}\\\\\n &\\qquad\\qquad\\cdot f(x)\\overline{g(y)}\\,\n       m_x(e(x,y))n_y(e(x,y))\\,\n       d\\rho_1(x)d\\rho_2(y).\n \\end{aligned}$$ If $$\\label{osc:shell-regime}\n v-a\\ge20\\sigma N,\\qquad L\\le(v-a)/2+\\sigma N,$$ then for every fixed $T>0$, $$\\label{osc:shell-estimate}\n 2^{-a}\\int_{2^{v-1}}^{2^{v+1}}|\\widehat\\nu(r)|^2\\,dr\n \\le R^{o(1)}\\sup\\mathsf H_F(a,v)+O(R^{-T}).$$ The supremum has the same cells, class normalizations, lists and smoothing index as in Equation <a href=\"#osc:weighted-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:weighted-measure\">[osc:weighted-measure]</a>. Its symbols are admissible with a derivative budget and common bounds for smooth angular factors depending only on $T,d,C_{\\mathcal A}$ and the fixed parameters. All these bounds are independent of $N$. When $f=g=1$, the measure $\\nu$ is positive. The assertion also holds on the fixed outer supports with $a=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-osc-stationary",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $b\\in C^\\infty(S^{n_*})$ obeys $\\|b\\|_{C^m}\\le C_m A^m$ at the finitely many orders needed below, where $A\\ge1$. Let $\\Delta\\ne0$, $e=\\Delta/|\\Delta|$, $\\Lambda=r|\\Delta|$, and $r>0$. A cutoff $\\psi$ is assumed to equal one on a fixed neighborhood of $e$ and zero on a fixed neighborhood of $-e$, with uniformly bounded derivatives. For every integer $J\\ge1$, $$\\label{osc:direct}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\\cdot\\Delta}\\psi(w)b(w)\\,dw\n &=\\Lambda^{-n_*/2}\n   \\left[e^{i\\Lambda}\\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\n                    \\Lambda^{-j}(L_jb)(e)+E_J\\right],\\\\\n |E_J|&\\le C_J\\Lambda^{-J}A^{2J+d+2},\n \\end{aligned}$$ provided $\\Lambda\\ge1$. Without a cutoff the corresponding formula is $$\\label{osc:full-sphere}\n \\int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\\cdot\\Delta}b(w)\\,dw\n =\\Lambda^{-n_*/2}\n   \\left[\\sum_{\\epsilon\\in\\{+1,-1\\}}e^{i\\epsilon\\Lambda}\n       \\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\\Lambda^{-j}\n                 (L_{j,\\epsilon}b)(\\epsilon e)+E_J'\\right],$$ with the same remainder bound. Define global differential operators $$\\label{osc:B-operators}\n B_0=L_0^{-1},\\qquad\n B_j=-L_0^{-1}\\sum_{h=1}^j L_h B_{j-h}.$$ Each $B_j$ is a polynomial in $\\Delta_{S^{n_*}}$ of differential order at most $2j$. If, for a fixed $c>0$, $$\\label{osc:stationary-regime}\n \\Lambda\\ge R^{c\\sigma},\\qquad\n A^2/\\Lambda\\le R^{-c\\sigma},\\qquad 1\\le A\\le R^2,$$ then for every prescribed $T>0$ a fixed sufficiently large $J$ gives $$\\label{osc:inverse}\n e^{i\\Lambda}b(e)\n =\\Lambda^{n_*/2}\\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\\Lambda^{-j}\n       \\int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\\cdot\\Delta}\\psi(w)(B_jb)(w)\\,dw\n       +O(R^{-T}).$$ The extracted remainders in Equations <a href=\"#osc:direct\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:direct\">[osc:direct]</a> and <a href=\"#osc:full-sphere\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:full-sphere\">[osc:full-sphere]</a> are also $O(R^{-T})$ after increasing $J$. All constants are uniform in $e$ in the stipulated geometry.*\n\n*For $r=2^v s$ with $s$ in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, each coefficient $\\Lambda^{-j}(L_{j,\\epsilon}b)(\\epsilon e)$ has uniformly bounded derivatives of every fixed order in $s$ under Equation <a href=\"#osc:stationary-regime\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:stationary-regime\">[osc:stationary-regime]</a>. Every differential coefficient retains the supports of all the mask factors in $b$.",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "exact_quote": "On separated depth-$a$ cells, for every fixed $j\\ge0$ there are bounded measurable functions $f_\\ell$ on $X$ and $g_\\ell$ on $Y$ and constants $c_\\ell$ such that $$\\label{osc:spatial-series}\n (D(x,y)2^a)^{-j}\n   =\\sum_\\ell c_\\ell f_\\ell(x)g_\\ell(y),\\qquad\n |f_\\ell|,|g_\\ell|\\le1,\\qquad\n \\sum_\\ell|c_\\ell|\\le C_{d,j}.$$ The convergence is uniform. The same assertion holds in the fixed outer geometry, with a constant depending on that geometry.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-graph-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho_1,\\rho_2$ be two regularized class laws, possibly equal, and write $M_j$, $A_{j,n}$, $I_j$ and $k_j$ for their profiles and associated quantities from Section <a href=\"#test:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"test:section\">6</a>. Let $X,Y$ be occupied cells of depth $a$. Put $X_1=X$, $X_2=Y$, and let $b^{(j)}_z(w)$ be an admissible endpoint symbol on $X_j$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#osc:symbols\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:symbols\">32</a>. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length of a descriptor in either symbol, with $L=0$ if both lists are empty. Suppose that the integer depths satisfy $0\\le a<p\\le v\\le N$ and $$\\label{graph:range}\n p-a\\le v-p+2\\sigma N,\\qquad\n L\\le v-p+2\\sigma N,\\qquad v-p\\ge10\\sigma N.$$ At endpoint $j$, suppose that the symbol imposes the parallel test $[g_j,p]$ at a level $\\ell_j\\in\\{0,\\ldots,J_0\\}$: for $\\rho_j$-almost every $z\\in X_j$ and every $w\\in S^{d-1}$, $$\\label{graph:support-test}\n b^{(j)}_z(w)\\ne0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n w\\in G^{(j)}_{\\ell_j,[g_j,p]}(z).$$ The superscript $j$ indicates the class used in the test. Assume $$\\label{graph:anchor-range}\n a\\le g_j<p,\\qquad g_j\\le a+2\\sigma N.$$*\n\n\n*Form the general independent energy $\\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v)$ of Definition <a href=\"#osc:states\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:states\">34</a> using these specified symbols. For every fixed $T>0$, $$\\label{graph:bound}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v)\n &\\le\n R^{-\\sum_{j=1}^2(A_{j,p}-A_{j,a})\n       +\\min_{j=1,2}k_j(a,p)+O_d(\\sigma)+o(1)}\\\\\n &\\qquad\\cdot\n \\max_{\\substack{P\\subset X,\\ Q\\subset Y\\\\\n                   P,Q\\text{ occupied depth-}p\\text{ cells}}}\n       \\mathsf H^{P,Q}(p,v)\n +O(R^{-T}).\n \\end{split}$$ No separation of $X$ and $Y$ is required. Every child in <a href=\"#graph:bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"graph:bound\">[graph:bound]</a> is formed by restricting the same endpoint symbols to $P$ and $Q$. It retains every original mask factor, including any whose anchor is $p$. Constants are uniform over cells and allowed symbols with fixed list-size exponents and derivative budgets, and with fixed common numerical bounds for their smooth angular factors at the derivative orders used.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rec-binning",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\eta,\\zeta$ be finite positive measures on the line and let $\\varepsilon>0$. Write $$B_\\varepsilon(\\eta)\n =\\sum_{j\\in\\mathbb Z}\\eta([j\\varepsilon,(j+1)\\varepsilon))^2.$$ For any fixed $C\\ge1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n B_\\varepsilon(\\eta)\n &\\le(\\eta\\otimes\\eta)\\{|s-s'|\\le\\varepsilon\\},\n \\label{rec:bins-lower}\\\\\n \\sup_{u\\in\\mathbb R}\n (\\eta\\otimes\\eta)\\{|s-s'-u|\\le C\\varepsilon\\}\n &\\le C' B_\\varepsilon(\\eta),\n \\label{rec:bins-shift}\\\\\n \\iint(1+|s-s'|/\\varepsilon)^{-2}\\,d\\eta(s)d\\zeta(s')\n &\\le C' B_\\varepsilon(\\eta)^{1/2}\n                    B_\\varepsilon(\\zeta)^{1/2}.\n \\label{rec:bins-kernel}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $$\\label{rec:collision-fourier}\n (\\eta\\otimes\\eta)\\{|s-s'|\\le C\\varepsilon\\}\n \\le C'\\varepsilon\n       \\int_{|r|\\le\\varepsilon^{-1}}|\\widehat\\eta(r)|^2\\,dr.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-binning",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\eta,\\zeta$ be finite positive measures on the line and let $\\varepsilon>0$.           "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rec-reductions",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the current state is nonterminal, its smoothing index satisfies $i<J_0$, and $N$ is sufficiently large with all parameters fixed. For every prescribed $T>0$, it is bounded, apart from an error $O(R^{-T})$ from oscillatory operations, by a sum of child bounds whose number is polynomial in $N$, and possibly a low-frequency stopping term. For every non-low child, if the old start is $a$ and the new start is $a'$, its multiplier is $$\\label{rec:transition-factor}\n R^{-2(A_{a'}-A_a)+\\mathrm{cost}+C_d\\sigma+o(1)}.$$ A low child has cost zero and the entire multiplier $R^{o(1)}$, with no fixed $C_d\\sigma$ loss. A low-frequency stopping term is $R^{C_d\\sigma+o(1)}$. The legal children are*\n\n<div class=\"center\">\n\n| *Reduction* | *Parent and child*                                                | *Cost*                        |\n|:------------|:------------------------------------------------------------------|:------------------------------|\n| *Suffix*    | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,p)$*                       | *$A_p-\\min_{[p,t]}A$*         |\n| *Low*       | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,v)$*                       | *$0$; full factor $R^{o(1)}$* |\n| *High*      | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{[a,t]}(p,v)$*                   | *$k(a,p)$*                    |\n| *Direct*    | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(p,v)$* | *$k(a,p)$*                    |\n| *Near*      | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(h,v)$* | *$0$*                         |\n| *Far*       | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(b,v)$*          | *$0$*                         |\n\n</div>\n\n*For a positive parent, $p$ is its current interval’s fixed policy choice. The suffix row is used when $2p>a+t$. When $2p\\le a+t$, the shell index is an integer $a<v\\le t$: the low row has $t-v\\ge\\sigma N$, and the high row has $0\\le t-v<\\sigma N$. A high child has base $[a,t]$ and carries the fixed enlarged derivative budget and common angular bounds produced by the inverse conversion; these depend on $T$ and the fixed parameters, not on $N$.*\n\n*For an angular parent, use $L_0,h$ from Equation <a href=\"#rec:angular-split-depth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rec:angular-split-depth\">[rec:angular-split-depth]</a>. If $h\\le a$, the direct row chooses a remaining descriptor of maximal length $L$, its anchor $p$, and an origin $[b_0,u_0]\\subseteq\\mathcal B$ for that membership. This choice has $L=L_0$, $a<p<v$, $$b_0\\le p-L,\\qquad p+L\\le u_0,\\qquad v-a\\le2L,$$ and it supplies the parallel descriptor $[\\max(a,p-L),p]$ used by the graph estimate. If $h>a$, the near row uses the displayed depth $h$. The far rows range over integer depths $b$ arising from the comparable far displacements, with $a<b\\le h+O_d(1)<v$, and use separated depth-$b$ cell pairs. Direct and near children keep the same base, index, derivative budget and common angular bounds. A far child has the fresh positive list $\\mathcal M(b,v)$.*\n\n\n*Finite maxima over child cells use the stated geometry. Whenever admissible symbols vary, take a supremum over the symbol family just specified. An empty collection of child terms contributes zero. Constants outside powers of $R$ may depend on the fixed parameters, derivative budgets and angular bounds. The constants $C_d$ in exponent losses depend only on $d$. A terminal state is bounded by $$\\label{rec:terminal}\n R^{C_d(\\kappa+\\sigma)+o(1)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-reductions",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose the current state is nonterminal, its smoothing index satisfies $i<J_0$, and $N$ is sufficiently large with all parameters fixed. For every prescribed $T>0$, it is bounded, apart from an error $O(R^{-T})$ from oscillatory operations, by a sum of child bounds whose number is polynomial in $N$, and possibly a low-frequency stopping term. For every non-low child, if the old start is $a$ and the new start is $a'$, its multiplier is $$\\label{rec:transition-factor}\n R^{-2(A_{a'}-A_a)+\\mathrm{cost}+C_d\\sigma+o(1)}.$$ A low child has cost zero and the entire multiplier $R^{o(1)}$, with no fixed $C_d\\sigma$ loss. A low-frequency stopping term is $R^{C_d\\sigma+o(1)}$. The legal children are*\n\n<div class=\"center\">\n\n| *Reduction* | *Parent and child*                                                | *Cost*                        |\n|:------------|:------------------------------------------------------------------|:------------------------------|\n| *Suffix*    | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,p)$*                       | *$A_p-\\min_{[p,t]}A$*         |\n| *Low*       | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,v)$*                       | *$0$; full factor $R^{o(1)}$* |\n| *High*      | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{[a,t]}(p,v)$*                   | *$k(a,p)$*                    |\n| *Direct*    | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(p,v)$* | *$k(a,p)$*                    |\n| *Near*      | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(h,v)$* | *$0$*                         |\n| *Far*       | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(b,v)$*          | *$0$*                         |\n\n</div>\n\n*For a positive parent, $p$ is its current interval’s fixed policy choice. The suffix row is used when $2p>a+t$. When $2p\\le a+t$, the shell index is an integer $a<v\\le t$: the low row has $t-v\\ge\\sigma N$, and the high row has $0\\le t-v<\\sigma N$. A high child has base $[a,t]$ and carries the fixed enlarged derivative budget and common angular bounds produced by the inverse conversion; these depend on $T$ and the fixed parameters, not on $N$.*\n\n*For an angular parent, use $L_0,h$ from Equation <a href=\"#rec:angular-split-depth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rec:angular-split-depth\">[rec:angular-split-depth]</a>. If $h\\le a$, the direct row chooses a remaining descriptor of maximal length $L$, its anchor $p$, and an origin $[b_0,u_0]\\subseteq\\mathcal B$ for that membership. This choice has $L=L_0$, $a<p<v$, $$b_0\\le p-L,\\qquad p+L\\le u_0,\\qquad v-a\\le2L,$$ and it supplies the parallel descriptor $[\\max(a,p-L),p]$ used by the graph estimate. If $h>a$, the near row uses the displayed depth $h$. The far rows range over integer depths $b$ arising from the comparable far displacements, with $a<b\\le h+O_d(1)<v$, and use separated depth-$b$ cell pairs. Direct and near children keep the same base, index, derivative budget and common angular bounds. A far child has the fresh positive list $\\mathcal M(b,v)$.*\n\n\n*Finite maxima over child cells use the stated geometry. Whenever admissible symbols vary, take a supremum over the symbol family just specified. An empty collection of child terms contributes zero. Constants outside powers of $R$ may depend on the fixed parameters, derivative budgets and angular bounds. The constants $C_d$ in exponent losses depend only on $d$. A terminal state is bounded by $$\\label{rec:terminal}\n R^{C_d(\\kappa+\\sigma)+o(1)}.$$",

A low-frequency stopping term is $R^{C_d\\sigma+o(1)}$.         "anchor": "stmt-ledger-termination",
        "exact_quote": "Every branch of the state reductions has at most $20/\\kappa$ non-low transitions and at most $1/\\sigma$ low transitions, for sufficiently small fixed parameters and sufficiently large $N$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathsf H_0^{[c,N]}(c,N)$ be a recursive angular state of nonterminal width, as in Definition <a href=\"#rec:angular-state\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rec:angular-state\">43</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-asm-entry",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\beta<\\min(\\chi/10,1/10)$. If $\\sigma$ is sufficiently small in terms of $d,\\chi,\\beta$, then, for all sufficiently large $N$, the integer $$\\label{asm:entry-definition}\n c=\\max\\{n\\in\\{0,\\ldots,N\\}:\n                  \\min(A_{1,n},A_{2,n})\\le\\beta\\}$$ is defined and satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\label{asm:entry-range}\n &\\left\\lfloor\\frac{\\beta N}{2d}\\right\\rfloor\\le c<N/4,\\\\\n \\label{asm:entry-lower}\n &A_{j,n}\\ge\\beta-d/N\n       \\quad(j=1,2,\\ c\\le n\\le N),\\\\\n \\label{asm:entry-cost}\n &\\min_{j=1,2} k_j(0,c)\\le\\beta+C_d\\sigma.\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $\\kappa$ is sufficiently small and $q_0=\\lceil\\kappa N\\rceil$, the interval $[c,N]$ is nonterminal. Its potentials from Definition <a href=\"#ledger:potential\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ledger:potential\">46</a> obey $$\\label{asm:entry-potential}\n \\mathcal V_j([c,N])\n :=\\min_{\\substack{c\\le u<v\\le N\\\\v-u\\ge10q_0}}\n                         (A_{j,u}+A_{j,v})\n \\ge2\\beta-2d/N.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-073.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-asm-retained-measures",
        "exact_quote": "There is $\\eta>0$, depending on the fixed parameters and starting measures, such that $$\\label{asm:tv-approximation}\n 0\\le\\tau_N\\le\\alpha_N,\\qquad\n \\|\\alpha_N-\\tau_N\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\lesssim2^{-\\eta N}.$$ Moreover $\\alpha_N$ decreases to the nonzero finite positive measure $$\\label{asm:limit-measure}\n \\alpha_\\infty=D_\\#\\bigl(W\\mathbf 1_{\\Gamma_\\infty}\n                               (\\mu_1\\otimes\\mu_2)\\bigr),\\qquad\n \\Gamma_\\infty=\\bigcap_N\\Gamma_N.$$ All these measures are carried by the compact set of original distances $\\Delta(E)$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Euclidean distance set** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $E\subset\mathbb R^d$, its Euclidean distance set is $$\Delta(E)=\{\|x-y\|:x,y\in E\}.$$
- **Subprobability** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A subprobability has mass at most one.
- **Unnormalized restrictions** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Restrictions are left unnormalized unless normalization is explicitly specified.
- **Energy and Fourier convention** ([source](#pm-h-8)): We write $$I_s(\mu)=\iint \|x-y\|^{-s}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\mu(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\mu(y), \qquad \widehat\mu(\xi)=\int e^{-i x\cdot\xi}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\mu(x).$$
- **Ball bound** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A measure has an $s$-ball bound with loss $K$, down to scale $\delta$, if $$\mu(B(z,r))\le K r^s \quad\text{for every }z\text{ and }\delta\le r\le1.$$
- **Spherical cap bound** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A cap bound on a sphere has the analogous meaning for its Euclidean chordal metric.
- **Radial direction and distance maps** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For $x\ne y$, put $$\pi_x(y)=\frac{y-x}{\|y-x\|},\qquad D(x,y)=\|x-y\|.$$
- **Subpower upper loss** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For a sequence $R\to\infty$, a positive factor is at most subpower if it is bounded by $R^{o(1)}$.
- **Subpower retained mass** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A retained mass is at least subpower if it is bounded below by $R^{-o(1)}$.
- **Available directional exponent** ([source](#stmt-prep-availability)): Let $\rho,\nu$ be fixed separated probabilities in $\mathbb R^{k+1}$, and let $G_N$ be Borel pair sets. An exponent $q$ is *available* for this family if, for every $e>0$, there are $c_e>0$, $C_e<\infty$, and Borel $B_N\subset G_N$, for all sufficiently large $N$, such that $$(\nu\otimes\rho)(B_N)\le C_e2^{-c_eN},$$ and both directional measures of $G_N\setminus B_N$ give every spherical cap of radius $u\in[2^{-N},1]$ mass at most $2^{eN}u^q$. The estimates hold at every pin in the retained supports, after removal of null fibers if necessary.
- **Profile quantities** ([source](#test:profile-definitions)): $$\label{test:profile-definitions} \begin{aligned} M(n)&=R^{-m(n/N)},& A_n&=m(n/N)-Sn/N,\\ I(g,p)&=m(p/N)-m(g/N),& k(g,p)&=A_p-\min_{g\le n\le p}A_n. \end{aligned}$$ Every minimum over depths is over integers unless stated otherwise. The quantity $M(n)$ is the model mass of a depth-$n$ cell, and $I(g,p)$ records the profile increment between two depths. The residual $A_n$ subtracts the critical slope $S$, while $k(g,p)$ is the rise of $A_p$ above its minimum since $g$. These quantities will give the cancellations in the two directional estimates below.
- **Directional descriptor** ([source](#pm-h-25)): A descriptor $\mathfrak t$ consists of a test type and a pair of integer depths.
- **Good directions** ([source](#stmt-test-good-directions)): Fix a class, a descriptor $\mathfrak t$ with anchor $p$, and an occupied depth-$p$ cube $P$, of centre $c_P$. Its level-$i$ good set $G_{i,\mathfrak t}(P)\subset S^{d-1}$ is defined as follows, with one sufficiently large dimensional constant $C_d$ for all tests. 1. For a parallel descriptor $[g,p]$, a direction $w$ is good if the number of occupied depth-$p$ cube centres in $B(c_P,10(d+1)2^{-g})$ whose distance to $c_P+\mathbb Rw$ is at most $W_i(p)$ is at most $$\label{test:parallel-threshold} R^{k(g,p)+C_d\sigma}.$$ 2. For a projection descriptor $[p,t]$, let $\rho_P=\rho\|_P/\rho(P)$. A direction $w$ is good if $$\label{test:projection-threshold} (\rho_P\otimes\rho_P) \{\|(z-z')\cdot w\|\le W_i(t)\} \le2^{-(t-p)} R^{A_p-\min_{p\le n\le t}A_n+C_d\sigma}.$$ For an unoccupied anchor cube set $G_{i,\mathfrak t}(P)=\varnothing$. For $x$ in the root cube, write $G_{i,\mathfrak t}(x)=G_{i,\mathfrak t}(P)$ for its unique depth-$p$ descendant $P$, and set $G_{i,\mathfrak t}(x)=\varnothing$ outside the root. The parallel test uses all occupied class centres in its stated ball and tube. The projection test uses the full conditional class probability in $P$. Subsequent pair restrictions do not change either test.
- **Admissible endpoint symbols** ([source](#stmt-osc-symbols)): Fix an indexed descriptor list $\mathcal A=(\mathfrak t_\nu)_{\nu\in\mathcal I}$ with $\#\mathcal I\le N^{C_{\mathcal A}}$, a smoothing index $i\in\{0,\ldots,J_0-1\}$, and a nonnegative integer derivative budget $B$. The exponent $C_{\mathcal A}$ and budget $B$ are fixed independently of $N$ and are common to the symbol family under consideration. At each angular order under consideration, also fix a common numerical bound $Q_j$ independent of $N$. An admissible endpoint symbol has the form $$\label{osc:symbol-form} b_x(w)=f(x)q(w) \prod_{\nu\in\mathcal I} D_{l_{\mathfrak t_\nu}}^{-\|\alpha_\nu\|} \Omega^{\alpha_\nu}w_{i,\mathfrak t_\nu}(x,w), \qquad \sum_{\nu\in\mathcal I}\|\alpha_\nu\|\le B.$$ The index $\nu$ distinguishes occurrences, so repeated descriptors may receive different derivative words. Here $f$ is an arbitrary measurable spatial function with $\|f\|\le1$, and $q$ is a smooth angular function satisfying $\\|q\\|_{C^j(S^{d-1})}\le Q_j$ at every order under consideration. The constants in estimates may depend on $C_{\mathcal A}$, $B$ and the fixed $Q_j$. Fixed scalar factors can be absorbed in those estimates. Empty lists and empty products are allowed, as are complex conjugates of such symbols. Symbols do not depend on the radial variable $r$.
- **Independent and shared-angle energies** ([source](#stmt-osc-states)): For $0\le a\le v\le N$, the independent angular energy is $$\label{osc:H} \mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v) =2^{d(v-a)}\int\Psi(r/2^v) \left(\int_{S^{d-1}}\|U_X(r,w)\|^2\,dw\right) \left(\int_{S^{d-1}}\|V_Y(r,w)\|^2\,dw\right) \frac{dr}{2^v}.$$ It is defined without any separation condition. In separated geometry we also use the form with one shared angle $$\label{osc:HF} \mathsf H_F^{X,Y}(a,v) =2^{d(v-a)}\int\Psi(r/2^v) \left\|\int_{S^{d-1}}\psi(w) U_X(r,w)\overline{V_Y(r,w)}\,dw\right\|^2 \frac{dr}{2^v}.$$ These are values for the specified class laws, cells and endpoint symbols, including the lists present in those symbols. We use maxima over finite collections of cell pairs and suprema when symbols vary; the family in a supremum will be stated.
- **Positive distance state** ([source](#stmt-osc-positive-count)): Let $0\le a\le t\le N$ be integer depths, let $i\in\{0,\ldots,J_0\}$, and let $X,Y$ be a separated pair of occupied depth-$a$ cells of $\rho$, in the geometry of Equation <a href="#osc:separation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="osc:separation">[osc:separation]</a>. Define the set of pairs satisfying the tests $$\mathcal P_i^{X,Y}(a,t)= \bigl\{(x,y)\in X\times Y: e(x,y)\in G_{i,\mathfrak t}(x)\cap G_{i,\mathfrak t}(y) \text{ for every }\mathfrak t\in\mathcal M(a,t)\bigr\},$$ and let $$\theta_i^{X,Y}(a,t) =\mathbf1_{\mathcal P_i^{X,Y}(a,t)} (\rho\|_X\otimes\rho\|_Y).$$ This is an unnormalized pair restriction. Put $C_0=100(d+1)$ and $$\label{osc:count} \mathsf D_i^{X,Y}(a,t) =2^{t-a}M(a)^{-4} \int\mathbf1_{\{\|D(x,y)-D(x',y')\|\le C_0 2^{-t}\}} \,d\theta_i^{X,Y}(a,t)(x,y) \,d\theta_i^{X,Y}(a,t)(x',y').$$ A positive state consists of these depths, index and cells, with value $\mathsf D_i^{X,Y}(a,t)$. Its current interval is $[a,t]$; it has no separate retained base. We suppress the cells in $\mathsf D_i(a,t)$ when they are fixed or are varied in a stated maximum.
- **Recursive angular state** ([source](#stmt-rec-angular-state)): Let $\mathcal B=[a_0,t_0]\subseteq[0,N]$ be an integer interval, called the retained base, and let $[a,v]$ be the current interval, with $$\label{osc:base-contract} 0\le a_0\le a\le v\le t_0\le N, \qquad t_0\le v+\sigma N.$$ Write $$\mathcal M_{>a}(\mathcal B)= \{\mathfrak t\in\mathcal M(a_0,t_0): \text{the anchor of }\mathfrak t\text{ is deeper than }a\}.$$ A recursive angular state consists of these intervals, an index $i\in\{0,\ldots,J_0-1\}$, two occupied depth-$a$ cells $X,Y$ of $\rho$, and two admissible endpoint symbols. No separation of the cells is required. Each symbol’s descriptor list consists of exactly one occurrence of each descriptor in $\mathcal M_{>a}(\mathcal B)$, at the common index $i$. Lemma <a href="#test:descriptor-properties" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:descriptor-properties">31</a> gives a common fixed polynomial size bound for these lists throughout the recursive family. The state also specifies a fixed derivative budget and common numerical bounds for its smooth angular factors, as in Definition <a href="#osc:symbols" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:symbols">32</a>; these bounds are independent of $N$. The value of this state is the already defined general energy for those data: $$\mathsf H_i^{\mathcal B}(a,v):=\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v).$$ The compact notation displays the current interval, base and index. The cells, symbols and fixed bounds remain part of the state data even when they are not displayed. We use a maximum only for finite choices of cells, and a supremum whenever admissible symbols vary. Neither operation varies the displayed base or index, or the fixed bounds, unless this is stated.
- **Two-depth potential** ([source](#stmt-ledger-potential)): For an integer interval $J=[a,t]$ of width at least $10q_0$, put $$\label{ledger:potential-formula} \mathcal V(J)= \min_{\substack{a\le i<j\le t\\j-i\ge10q_0}}(A_i+A_j).$$ We leave $\mathcal V(J)$ undefined for shorter intervals.
- **Original-mass distance measures** ([source](#asm:measures)): Let $\Gamma_N$ be the decreasing pair filters of Proposition <a href="#prep:starting-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:starting-pairs">23</a>. With $D_\#$ denoting pushforward under the scalar map $D$, define $$\label{asm:measures} \alpha_N=D_\#\bigl(W\mathbf 1_{\Gamma_N} (\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)\bigr),\qquad \tau_N=D_\#\bigl(W\mathbf 1_{\Gamma_N}\Theta_N (\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)\bigr).$$ These measures use the original class masses. Only $\tau_N$ depends on the shell’s class decomposition and angular masks.

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho,\\nu$ be fixed separated probabilities in $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$, and let $G_N$ be Borel pair sets.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-test-good-directions",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a class, a descriptor $\\mathfrak t$ with anchor $p$, and an occupied depth-$p$ cube $P$, of centre $c_P$. Its level-$i$ good set $G_{i,\\mathfrak t}(P)\\subset S^{d-1}$ is defined as follows, with one sufficiently large dimensional constant $C_d$ for all tests.\n\n\n1.  For a parallel descriptor $[g,p]$, a direction $w$ is good if the number of occupied depth-$p$ cube centres in $B(c_P,10(d+1)2^{-g})$ whose distance to $c_P+\\mathbb Rw$ is at most $W_i(p)$ is at most $$\\label{test:parallel-threshold}\n          R^{k(g,p)+C_d\\sigma}.$$\n\n2.  For a projection descriptor $[p,t]$, let $\\rho_P=\\rho|_P/\\rho(P)$. A direction $w$ is good if $$\\label{test:projection-threshold}\n          (\\rho_P\\otimes\\rho_P)\n          \\{|(z-z')\\cdot w|\\le W_i(t)\\}\n          \\le2^{-(t-p)}\n               R^{A_p-\\min_{p\\le n\\le t}A_n+C_d\\sigma}.$$\n\nFor an unoccupied anchor cube set $G_{i,\\mathfrak t}(P)=\\varnothing$. For $x$ in the root cube, write $G_{i,\\mathfrak t}(x)=G_{i,\\mathfrak t}(P)$ for its unique depth-$p$ descendant $P$, and set $G_{i,\\mathfrak t}(x)=\\varnothing$ outside the root. The parallel test uses all occupied class centres in its stated ball and tube. The projection test uses the full conditional class probability in $P$. Subsequent pair restrictions do not change either test.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }

      "exact_definition": "Fix a class, a descriptor $\\mathfrak t$ with anchor $p$, and an occupied depth-$p$ cube $P$, of centre $c_P$.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-osc-symbols",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an indexed descriptor list $\\mathcal A=(\\mathfrak t_\\nu)_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal I}$ with $\\#\\mathcal I\\le N^{C_{\\mathcal A}}$, a smoothing index $i\\in\\{0,\\ldots,J_0-1\\}$, and a nonnegative integer derivative budget $B$. The exponent $C_{\\mathcal A}$ and budget $B$ are fixed independently of $N$ and are common to the symbol family under consideration. At each angular order under consideration, also fix a common numerical bound $Q_j$ independent of $N$. An admissible endpoint symbol has the form $$\\label{osc:symbol-form}\n b_x(w)=f(x)q(w)\n       \\prod_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal I}\n       D_{l_{\\mathfrak t_\\nu}}^{-|\\alpha_\\nu|}\n       \\Omega^{\\alpha_\\nu}w_{i,\\mathfrak t_\\nu}(x,w),\n \\qquad \\sum_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal I}|\\alpha_\\nu|\\le B.$$ The index $\\nu$ distinguishes occurrences, so repeated descriptors may receive different derivative words. Here $f$ is an arbitrary measurable spatial function with $|f|\\le1$, and $q$ is a smooth angular function satisfying $\\|q\\|_{C^j(S^{d-1})}\\le Q_j$ at every order under consideration. The constants in estimates may depend on $C_{\\mathcal A}$, $B$ and the fixed $Q_j$. Fixed scalar factors can be absorbed in those estimates. Empty lists and empty products are allowed, as are complex conjugates of such symbols. Symbols do not depend on the radial variable $r$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],

The exponent $C_{\\mathcal A}$ and budget $B$ are fixed independently of $N$ and are common to the symbol family under consideration. At each angular order under consideration, also fix a common numerical bound $Q_j$ independent of $N$. An admissible endpoint symbol has the form $$\\label{osc:symbol-form}\n b_x(w)=f(x)q(w)\n       \\prod_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal I}\n       D_{l_{\\mathfrak t_\\nu}}^{-|\\alpha_\\nu|}\n       \\Omega^{\\alpha_\\nu}w_{i,\\mathfrak t_\\nu}(x,w),\n \\qquad \\sum_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal I}|\\alpha_\\nu|\\le B.$$ The index $\\nu$ distinguishes occurrences, so repeated descriptors may receive different derivative words. Here $f$ is an arbitrary measurable spatial function with $|f|\\le1$, and $q$ is a smooth angular function satisfying $\\|q\\|_{C^j(S^{d-1})}\\le Q_j$ at every order under consideration. In separated geometry we also use the form with one shared angle $$\\label{osc:HF}\n \\mathsf H_F^{X,Y}(a,v)\n =2^{d(v-a)}\\int\\Psi(r/2^v)\n   \\left|\\int_{S^{d-1}}\\psi(w)\n           U_X(r,w)\\overline{V_Y(r,w)}\\,dw\\right|^2\n   \\frac{dr}{2^v}.$$ These are values for the specified class laws, cells and endpoint symbols, including the lists present in those symbols. Put $C_0=100(d+1)$ and $$\\label{osc:count}\n \\mathsf D_i^{X,Y}(a,t)\n =2^{t-a}M(a)^{-4}\n   \\int\\mathbf1_{\\{|D(x,y)-D(x',y')|\\le C_0 2^{-t}\\}}\n   \\,d\\theta_i^{X,Y}(a,t)(x,y)\n   \\,d\\theta_i^{X,Y}(a,t)(x',y').$$ A positive state consists of these depths, index and cells, with value $\\mathsf D_i^{X,Y}(a,t)$.           "anchor": "stmt-rec-angular-state",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal B=[a_0,t_0]\\subseteq[0,N]$ be an integer interval, called the retained base, and let $[a,v]$ be the current interval, with $$\\label{osc:base-contract}\n 0\\le a_0\\le a\\le v\\le t_0\\le N,\n \\qquad t_0\\le v+\\sigma N.$$ Write $$\\mathcal M_{>a}(\\mathcal B)=\n \\{\\mathfrak t\\in\\mathcal M(a_0,t_0):\n                 \\text{the anchor of }\\mathfrak t\\text{ is deeper than }a\\}.$$ A recursive angular state consists of these intervals, an index $i\\in\\{0,\\ldots,J_0-1\\}$, two occupied depth-$a$ cells $X,Y$ of $\\rho$, and two admissible endpoint symbols. | id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-dimension-threshold | \dim_H E | > | $d/2$ | The ambient dimension is an integer in the stated range. |
| q-regular-class-count | Number of retained regular classes | ≤ | $N^{C_n/\lambda}$ | Fixed regularization loss and sufficiently large depth. |
| q-regular-class-minimum-mass | \rho(E_\alpha) | ≥ | $R^{-\lambda}$ | Fixed loss and sufficiently large dyadic depth. |
| q-planar-tube-union | \left\|\bigcup_{x\in P}\mathcal T_x\right\|_\delta | ≥ | $\delta^{-2b-\epsilon_0}$ | Fixed admissible exponents and sufficiently small scale. |
| q-transverse-spatial-cover | \|A\|_{C_m\delta} | ≤ | $C_{m,C}\Delta^{-m} \prod_{i=1}^m M_i^{1/(m-1)}$ | Fixed dimension, width multiple and determinant lower bound. |
| q-weak-radial-exponent-upper-end | \ell | < | $\frac{(k-1)^2}{k}$ | The lower endpoint and strict upper endpoint must both hold. |
| q-classical-ball-exponent-threshold | s | > | $(n+1)/2$ | A strict sufficient condition for the classical distance argument. |
| q-starting-product-partition-count | Number of disjoint spatial products in \Gamma_N | ≤ | $C2^{2d\omega N}$ | Fixed measures and coarse cutoff; constants are independent of depth. |
| q-critical-angular-fourier-energy | Spherical-radial Fourier energy | ≤ | $C LK(1+\log H)$ | Each bound is required only on its stated radius interval. |
| q-test-failure-mass | \xi(S^{d-1}\setminus G_{0,\mathfrak t}(P)) | ≤ | $R^{-\sigma}$ | Uniform over occupied anchor cubes and level-zero descriptors. |
| q-shell-conversion-frequency-margin | v-a | ≥ | $20\sigma N$ | The nominal-length condition is required along with this margin. |
| q-graph-frequency-margin | v-p | ≥ | $10\sigma N$ | The other graph depth inequalities and support tests remain necessary. |
| q-non-low-branch-length | Number of non-low transitions on a branch | ≤ | $20/\kappa$ | Parameters are fixed before depth increases. |
| q-low-branch-length | Number of low transitions on a branch | ≤ | $1/\sigma$ | The low transition loss differs from the non-low loss. |
| q-entry-depth-upper-bound | c | < | $N/4$ | The entry depth is the last qualifying integer depth. |
| q-entry-cost-upper-bound | \min_{j=1,2} k_j(0,c) | ≤ | $\beta+C_d\sigma$ | The graph estimate uses the smaller profile cost. |
| q-entry-potential-lower-bound | \mathcal V_j([c,N]) | ≥ | $2\beta-2d/N$ | Both depths lie beyond entry and obey the potential separation condition. |
| q-scalar-approximation-order | \tau_N | ≤ | $\alpha_N$ | The shell mask lies between zero and one. |

          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$, let $\\rho$ be a probability on a unit dyadic cube in $\\mathbb R^n$, and put $R=2^N$. For every fixed $0<\\lambda<1$ and all sufficiently large $N$, there are pairwise disjoint sets $E_\\alpha$, each a union of depth-$N$ cubes, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *Their number is at most $N^{C_n/\\lambda}$, each has $\\rho(E_\\alpha)\\ge R^{-\\lambda}$, and $$\\rho\\Bigl(\\mathbb R^n\\setminus\\bigcup_\\alpha E_\\alpha\\Bigr)\n          \\le R^{-2}+N^{C_n/\\lambda}R^{-\\lambda}\n          =R^{-\\lambda+o(1)}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For $\\rho_\\alpha=\\rho|_{E_\\alpha}/\\rho(E_\\alpha)$ there is a nondecreasing $n$-Lipschitz function $m_\\alpha:[0,1]\\to[0,n]$, with $m_\\alpha(0)=0$, such that every occupied depth-$j$ cube satisfies $$\\label{reg:cell-mass}\n          R^{-m_\\alpha(j/N)-C_n\\lambda}\n          \\le\\rho_\\alpha(Q)\\le\n          R^{-m_\\alpha(j/N)+C_n\\lambda},\\qquad 0\\le j\\le N.$$*\n\n\n3.  *If $\\rho(B(x,r))\\le Kr^s$ for $R^{-1}\\le r\\le1$, with $0\\le s\\le n$ and $K\\ge1$, then $$\\label{reg:profile-lower-bound}\n          m_\\alpha(u)\\ge su-C_n\\lambda-\\log_R K,\n          \\qquad 0\\le u\\le1.$$*\n\n\n*Here and below an occupied cube means a cube of positive mass for the specified class.           "anchor": "stmt-prep-starting-pairs",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact, $d\\ge2$, and $\\dim_HE>S=d/2$. Either Lemma <a href=\"#prep:classical-plane\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prep:classical-plane\">18</a> already proves that its distance set has positive Lebesgue measure, or the following construction is possible.*\n\n*After a similarity there are compact separated sets $E_1,E_2\\subset E$ and probabilities $\\mu_i$ supported on them, each satisfying $$\\mu_i(B(x,u))\\le C u^{S+\\chi}$$ for a fixed $\\chi>0$. Their cross directions lie in a fixed arbitrarily small spherical patch. For every sufficiently small fixed $\\omega>0$, there are decreasing Borel pair sets $\\Gamma_N\\subset E_1\\times E_2$ such that*\n\n1.  *$(\\mu_1\\otimes\\mu_2)(\\bigcap_N\\Gamma_N)>0$;*\n\n2.  *$\\Gamma_N$ is a disjoint union of at most $C2^{2d\\omega N}$ products of spatial Borel sets;*\n\n3.  *for every $e>0$, there are $c(e)>0$, $C_e<\\infty$ and Borel $B_N\\subset\\Gamma_N$ with $(\\mu_1\\otimes\\mu_2)(B_N)\\le C_e2^{-c(e)N}$, such that for $G_N=\\Gamma_N\\setminus B_N$, all $2^{-N}\\le u\\le1$, and all spherical caps $B(w,u)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     \\mu_2\\{y:(x,y)\\in G_N,\\ \\pi_x(y)\\in B(w,u)\\}\n         &\\le2^{eN}u^S,\\\\\n     \\mu_1\\{x:(x,y)\\in G_N,\\ \\pi_y(x)\\in B(w,u)\\}\n         &\\le2^{eN}u^S.\n    \\end{aligned}$$*\n\n*The last bounds integrate the original opposite marginal, without normalizing its individual fibers. The $B_N$ need not have a product partition or be nested. All constants are independent of $N$, but may depend on the fixed measures, $\\omega$, and $e$.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le H\\le R$, $K,L\\ge1$, and $S=d/2$. Suppose $\\alpha$ is a probability in a fixed bounded subset of $\\mathbb R^d$ with $$\\alpha(B(x,r))\\le Kr^S\\qquad(H^{-1}\\le r\\le1),$$ and $\\xi$ is a subprobability on $S^{d-1}$ with $$\\xi(B(w,r))\\le Lr^S\\qquad(R^{-1}\\le r\\le1).$$ Then, with Fourier convention $\\widehat\\alpha(v)=\\int e^{-iz\\cdot v}\\,d\\alpha(z)$, $$\\label{test:fourier-estimate}\n\\int_{-H}^{H}\\int_{S^{d-1}}|\\widehat\\alpha(sw)|^2\\,d\\xi(w)\\,ds\n \\le C LK(1+\\log H).$$ The constant depends only on $d$ and the fixed bound on the support’s diameter. In particular it does not require ball bounds below $H^{-1}$ or any Fourier decay.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-osc-shell-conversion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le a\\le v\\le N$ be integer depths, and let $X,Y$ be separated cells at depth $a$ of one or two classes. Let $m_x(w)$ and $n_y(w)$ be products of the undifferentiated masks at one index $i<J_0$ for two specified indexed descriptor lists. Assume both lists have at most $N^{C_{\\mathcal A}}$ occurrences, where one exponent $C_{\\mathcal A}$ is fixed independently of $N$ for the whole family under consideration, as in Definition <a href=\"#osc:symbols\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:symbols\">32</a>. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length in either list, with $L=0$ when both lists are empty. For measurable $|f|,|g|\\le1$ define a possibly complex measure $\\nu$ on the distance line by $$\\label{osc:weighted-measure}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\int F(s)\\,d\\nu(s)\n &=\\frac1{M_1(a)M_2(a)}\n   \\int_{X\\times Y} F(D(x,y))\\,\n       (D(x,y)2^a)^{-n_*/2}\\\\\n &\\qquad\\qquad\\cdot f(x)\\overline{g(y)}\\,\n       m_x(e(x,y))n_y(e(x,y))\\,\n       d\\rho_1(x)d\\rho_2(y).\n \\end{aligned}$$ If $$\\label{osc:shell-regime}\n v-a\\ge20\\sigma N,\\qquad L\\le(v-a)/2+\\sigma N,$$ then for every fixed $T>0$, $$\\label{osc:shell-estimate}\n 2^{-a}\\int_{2^{v-1}}^{2^{v+1}}|\\widehat\\nu(r)|^2\\,dr\n \\le R^{o(1)}\\sup\\mathsf H_F(a,v)+O(R^{-T}).$$ The supremum has the same cells, class normalizations, lists and smoothing index as in Equation <a href=\"#osc:weighted-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:weighted-measure\">[osc:weighted-measure]</a>. Its symbols are admissible with a derivative budget and common bounds for smooth angular factors depending only on $T,d,C_{\\mathcal A}$ and the fixed parameters. All these bounds are independent of $N$. When $f=g=1$, the measure $\\nu$ is positive. The assertion also holds on the fixed outer supports with $a=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],

For every fixed $T>0$, $$\\label{graph:bound}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v)\n &\\le\n R^{-\\sum_{j=1}^2(A_{j,p}-A_{j,a})\n       +\\min_{j=1,2}k_j(a,p)+O_d(\\sigma)+o(1)}\\\\\n &\\qquad\\cdot\n \\max_{\\substack{P\\subset X,\\ Q\\subset Y\\\\\n                   P,Q\\text{ occupied depth-}p\\text{ cells}}}\n       \\mathsf H^{P,Q}(p,v)\n +O(R^{-T}).\n \\end{split}$$ No separation of $X$ and $Y$ is required. Constants are uniform over cells and allowed symbols with fixed list-size exponents and derivative budgets, and with fixed common numerical bounds for their smooth angular factors at the derivative orders used. The $o(1)$ tends to zero as $N\\to\\infty$ with these bounds, the parameters, and $T$ fixed.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-energy-transfer** [claim](#stmt-conv-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-energy): Dyadic annuli, potential truncation and Haar averaging. -> Finite energies, compact ball-bounded restrictions and projected energy preservation.
- **step-sequential-uniformity** [claim](#stmt-conv-uniformity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-uniformity): Contradiction using monotonicity and a diagonal sequence. -> Uniform positive loss threshold and gain.
- **step-regularize-dyadic-tree** [claim](#stmt-reg-regularization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reg-regularization): Mass binning and upward branching labels. -> Disjoint regular classes and Lipschitz profiles.
- **step-density-pruning** [claim](#stmt-reg-pruning-multiplication) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reg-pruning-multiplication): Endpoint density deletion and iteration over hit children. -> Multiplied final fiber bound after controlled pair deletion.
- **step-planar-furstenberg-input** [claim](#stmt-ang-furstenberg) / [proof](#pm-cite-35): Invoke the cited discretized planar tube theorem. -> Tube-union covering lower bound.
- **step-labelled-planar-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-ang-planar-measures) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-planar-measures): Sample original labels, control multiplicities and compare tube-union counts. -> Contradiction to subpower incidence.
- **step-haar-band-estimates** [claim](#stmt-ang-haar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-haar): Spherical coordinates and conditional orthonormal frames. -> Single and multiple band probability estimates.
- **step-select-controlled-slices** [claim](#stmt-ang-slice-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-slice-selection): Average normalized bands and truncate masses, ball failures and active covers. -> Measurable slices with retained incidence and projected bounds.
- **step-transverse-tube-cover** [claim](#stmt-ang-loomis-whitney) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-loomis-whitney): Change to transverse coordinates and apply discrete Loomis–Whitney. -> Spatial covering upper bound.
- **step-weak-radial-estimate** [claim](#stmt-ang-weak-radial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-weak-radial): Regularize, remove troublesome local tubes, obtain transverse tuples and multiply local bounds. -> Power-small pair deletion and weak radial cap control.
- **step-linear-angular-gain** [claim](#stmt-ang-linear-gain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-weak-radial-2): Slice to the planar obstruction or use a transverse triple in the endpoint regime. -> Uniform power-small incidence.
- **step-borel-bad-directions** [claim](#stmt-ang-bad-directions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-bad-directions): Round tube anchors and select from finite candidate lists. -> Common Borel bad-direction envelope with small mass.
- **step-contact-profile-interval** [claim](#stmt-prep-contact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-contact): Study the future minimum of the excess over the lower target slope. -> Contact interval with compatible outer and middle growth.
- **step-one-scale-improvement** [claim](#stmt-prep-one-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-one-scale): Profile compactness, outer Markov bounds, middle good-direction gain and density multiplication. -> Improved final-scale cap exponent with power deletion.
- **step-bootstrap-available-exponents** [claim](#stmt-prep-thin-upgrade) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-thin-upgrade): Downward closure, increasing-limit closure and repeated single-scale gain. -> Half-integer exponent availability.
- **step-classical-distance-case** [claim](#stmt-prep-classical-plane) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-classical-plane): Spherical kernel decay, annular mass bounds, stationary phase and Plancherel. -> Nonzero distance measure with a square-integrable density.
- **step-common-projected-restriction** [claim](#stmt-prep-projections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-projections): Generic energy-preserving projections and simultaneous pulled-back potential restrictions. -> Common original restriction with projected ball bounds.
- **step-radial-integrability** [claim](#stmt-prep-radial-lp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-radial-lp): Energy duality, angular integration and weak limits of smooth sources. -> Joint radial densities and uniform good-pin restrictions.
- **step-integer-caps-and-lifting** [claim](#stmt-prep-integer-caps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-integer-caps): Truncate radial densities and use the Lipschitz projected direction map. -> Integer cap bounds with controlled pair deletion.
- **step-coarse-heavy-plate-filter** [claim](#stmt-prep-coarse-filter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-coarse-filter): Heavy-plate nets, transverse intersection control and successive coarse exclusions. -> Decreasing filters with positive intersection mass and plate bounds.
- **step-starting-pair-assembly** [claim](#stmt-prep-starting-pairs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-starting-pairs): Separate original supports, prepare auxiliary radial laws and pull filters back. -> Original-space pair filters, product partition and critical cap preparation.
- **step-profile-conditional-bounds** [claim](#stmt-test-profile-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-profile-bounds): Compare regular cell masses and divide by actual occupied parent mass. -> Excess-profile bounds, occupied-center counts and conditional ball losses.
- **step-critical-fractal-fourier** [claim](#stmt-test-half-dimensional-fourier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-half-dimensional-fourier): Frequency-shell cap control, smoothing and a positive Gaussian majorant. -> Logarithmic spherical-radial Fourier bound.
- **step-test-failure-estimates** [claim](#stmt-test-exceptional-directions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-exceptional-directions): Annular center counting or Fourier collision averaging followed by Markov. -> Power-small failure mass uniformly in anchor cells.
- **step-buffered-angular-masks** [claim](#stmt-test-smooth-masks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-smooth-masks): Compare consecutive width gaps and convolve buffered spherical neighborhoods. -> Smooth masks retaining the next-level support condition.
- **step-products-preserve-tests** [claim](#stmt-test-product-symbols) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-product-symbols): Fixed-order product rule and polynomial multiplicity absorption. -> Product derivative bounds and retained supports.
- **step-nested-descriptor-schedule** [claim](#stmt-test-descriptor-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-descriptor-properties): Fixed block representatives and membership-specific origin witnesses. -> Nested lists, polynomial descriptor count and origin geometry.
- **step-symbol-bounds** [claim](#stmt-osc-symbol-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-osc-symbol-bounds): Apply product estimates and spatial constancy of reached anchor masks. -> Uniform derivatives and bounded reached factors.
- **step-global-stationary-expansions** [claim](#stmt-osc-stationary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-osc-stationary): Quadratic stationary charts, global operator identification and formal inversion with controlled remainders. -> Direct and inverse expansions preserving mask supports.
- **step-separated-spatial-series** [claim](#stmt-osc-spatial-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-osc-spatial-separation): Extend the smooth distance coefficient to a torus and split its Fourier series. -> Uniform absolutely summable endpoint expansion.
- **step-weighted-shell-conversion** [claim](#stmt-osc-shell-conversion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-osc-spatial-separation-2): Inverse stationary expansion, distance-power cancellation and endpoint factorization. -> Shared-angle energy bound with unchanged lists.
- **step-nonstationary-edge-localization** [claim](#stmt-graph-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-graph-localization): Radial or tangent-field integration by parts on entire cell-cap terms. -> Arbitrary-power error outside retained edges.
- **step-row-column-degree-control** [claim](#stmt-graph-degrees) / [proof](#proof-stmt-graph-degrees): Use eligible witnesses and parallel tests, then confine the second unknown by scalar and perpendicular constraints. -> Matching row and column degree bounds.
- **step-independent-angular-graph** [claim](#stmt-graph-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-graph-degrees-2): Retain oscillatory edges, apply both degree bounds and cancel profile normalizations. -> Child independent energies retaining every endpoint mask.
- **step-shared-angle-graph-transfer** [claim](#stmt-graph-separated-form) / [proof](#proof-stmt-graph-separated-form): Angular Cauchy–Schwarz with identical endpoint data. -> The independent graph bound for the shared-angle parent.
- **step-positive-collision-binning** [claim](#stmt-rec-binning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rec-binning): Cauchy–Schwarz on bin offsets and a positive Fourier majorant. -> Shifted, kernel and Fourier collision bounds.
- **step-legal-state-reductions** [claim](#stmt-rec-reductions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rec-reductions): Suffix linearization, low shell positivity, high graph entry and near-far angular splitting. -> Legal child states with prescribed costs and retained supports.
- **step-potential-payment** [claim](#stmt-ledger-payment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ledger-payment): Replace one admissible depth using the policy's inner-interval minimum. -> Transition cost bounded by potential increase.
- **step-branch-and-index-budget** [claim](#stmt-ledger-termination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ledger-termination): Charge transitions to decreasing endpoints, passed representatives and angular episodes. -> Finite depth and sufficient smoothing budget.
- **step-single-profile-recursive-bound** [claim](#stmt-ledger-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ledger-bound): Telescope start increments and potential payments, bound the last defined potential and fix all error budgets. -> Uniform single-profile angular estimate.
- **step-common-entry-depth** [claim](#stmt-asm-entry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-asm-entry): Choose the last low excess-profile depth and use Lipschitz and Frostman lower bounds. -> Small entry cost and positive potential on the retained interval.
- **step-original-mass-restoration** [claim](#stmt-asm-retained-measures) / [proof](#proof-stmt-asm-retained-measures): Estimate failures using original opposite marginals and sum class weights. -> Positive approximants with summable error and a nonzero original-distance limit.
- **step-assembled-shell-decay** [claim](#stmt-asm-shell-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-asm-shell-decay): Root conversion, common graph entry, radial Cauchy–Schwarz and ordered parameter choices. -> Summable Fourier shell norms after restoring original masses.
- **step-monotone-scalar-reconstruction** [claim](#stmt-asm-monotone-shell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-asm-monotone-shell): Telescoping smooth Fourier cutoffs and summable positive measure increments. -> Absolutely continuous limiting measure.
- **step-distance-measure-completion** [claim](#stmt-asm-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-asm-completion): Apply scalar reconstruction to the nonzero supported limit. -> Positive Lebesgue measure of the original distance set.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-asm-completion-2): Separate the classical affine-plane alternative from the prepared-pair argument. -> The asserted unpinned positive-measure conclusion in every stated dimension.

          "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\ge2$, $\\zeta>0$, and $$k-2<\\ell<\\frac{(k-1)^2}{k}.$$ For every $e>0$ there exist $\\epsilon,c>0$ with the following property for sufficiently small $\\delta$. Suppose that a probability $\\lambda$ in a bounded subset of $\\mathbb R^k$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lambda(B(x,r))&\\le\\delta^{-\\epsilon}r^{k-1},\\\\\n \\lambda(H^{(r)})&\\le\\delta^{-\\epsilon}r^\\zeta\n \\qquad(\\delta\\le r\\le1)\n\\end{aligned}$$ for every affine hyperplane $H$, where $H^{(r)}$ is its $r$-neighborhood. There is a measurable pair set $G$ such that $$(\\lambda\\otimes\\lambda)(G^c)\\lesssim\\delta^c,\n \\qquad\n \\lambda\\{z:(x,z)\\in G,\\ \\pi_x(z)\\in B(\\theta,r)\\}\n       \\le\\delta^{-e}r^\\ell$$ for every $x$, every spherical cap center $\\theta$, and $\\delta\\le r\\le1$. The diagonal is omitted from $G$. The conclusion also holds for balls in projective direction space, with a fixed change of constant, by taking the two antipodal caps.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }

        "Admissible radial exponent"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-linear-gain",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\ge2$ and $$k-1\\le q\\le k-\\tfrac12<t<k.$$ Fix a tube-width constant $C$. In the case $q=k-1$, also fix $\\zeta>0$. There exist $\\epsilon,c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\\delta$, the following holds. Let $\\mu$ be a bounded point subprobability in $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$ and $\\nu$ a direction subprobability on $S^k$ with respective $t$- and $q$-ball bounds of loss $\\delta^{-\\epsilon}$ down to $\\delta$. At $q=k-1$ assume in addition $$\\label{ang:endpoint-plate}\n \\nu\\{w:\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,H)\\le r\\}\\le\\delta^{-\\epsilon}r^\\zeta\n       \\qquad(\\delta\\le r\\le1)$$ for every linear hyperplane $H$. Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^{k+1}\\times S^k$ be a measurable incidence relation, and write $E_w=\\{x:(x,w)\\in E\\}$. If each direction fiber $E_w$ is covered by at most $\\delta^{-q-\\epsilon}$ tubes parallel to $w$ of width $C\\delta$, then $$(\\mu\\otimes\\nu)(E)\\le\\delta^c.$$ All constants are uniform over the measures and the incidence relations with these fixed parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-linear-angular-gain",
      "inputs": [
        "Point and direction laws",
        "Sparse parallel covers",

          "anchor": "stmt-ang-bad-directions",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the parameters of Theorem <a href=\"#ang:linear-gain\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ang:linear-gain\">6</a>. There are $c_1,\\epsilon_1>0$ such that the following holds at sufficiently small $\\delta$. Let $\\mu$ be a bounded point probability satisfying the $t$-ball bound with loss $\\delta^{-\\epsilon_1}$. Let $B_\\delta$ be the set of directions for which at most $\\delta^{-q-c_1}$ width-$C\\delta$ tubes capture $\\mu$-mass at least $\\delta^{c_1}$. There is a Borel set $B_\\delta^+\\supset B_\\delta$, depending only on the point law and this geometric test, such that every direction subprobability $\\nu$ satisfying the $q$-ball bound with loss $\\delta^{-\\epsilon_1}$, and, when $q=k-1$, Equation <a href=\"#ang:endpoint-plate\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:endpoint-plate\">[ang:endpoint-plate]</a> with $\\epsilon_1$ in place of $\\epsilon$, satisfies $$\\nu(B_\\delta^+)\\le\\delta^{c_1}.$$ In particular this bounds the outer $\\nu$-measure of the original geometric bad set. The direction law need not be normalized on either set or on any individual incidence fiber. All predetermined fixed widths are allowed.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prep-classical-plane",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho$ be a compactly supported probability in $\\mathbb R^n$, $n\\ge2$, with an $s$-ball bound for some $s>(n+1)/2$. Then the distances between points of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\rho$ form a set of positive Lebesgue measure.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prep-projections",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact and $\\dim_HE>S=d/2$. There is a probability on $E$ with a ball exponent strictly greater than $S$. Finitely many successive generic orthogonal projections to dimensions strictly greater than $S$ preserve finite energies with exponents strictly greater than $S$. After a positive compact restriction of the original probability, all these projected laws, as well as the original law, have a common ball exponent $S+\\chi$, with $\\chi>0$.*\n\n*If $d=2k-1$ and the original measure gives zero mass to every affine $k$-plane, the projection to $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$ may additionally be chosen to give zero mass to every affine hyperplane.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }

      ],
      "method": "Generic energy-preserving projections and simultaneous pulled-back potential restrictions.",
      "output": "Common original restriction with projected ball bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prep-projections"

          "anchor": "stmt-prep-radial-lp",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h\\ge1$ and $0<\\epsilon<\\min(h,1)$. Let $\\alpha,\\beta$ be bounded, separated probabilities in $\\mathbb R^{h+1}$ with finite $I_{h+\\epsilon}$ energies. For some $p>1$, the radial measures $(\\pi_x)_\\#\\alpha$ have jointly measurable densities $F(x,w)$ and $$\\int\\!\\int_{S^h}F(x,w)^p\\,d\\sigma(w)\\,d\\beta(x)<\\infty.$$ The same assertion holds in the reverse orientation. Positive compact restrictions can be chosen on both sides so that the two radial $L^p$ norms are uniformly bounded at every remaining pin.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prep-starting-pairs",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact, $d\\ge2$, and $\\dim_HE>S=d/2$. Either Lemma <a href=\"#prep:classical-plane\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prep:classical-plane\">18</a> already proves that its distance set has positive Lebesgue measure, or the following construction is possible.*\n\n*After a similarity there are compact separated sets $E_1,E_2\\subset E$ and probabilities $\\mu_i$ supported on them, each satisfying $$\\mu_i(B(x,u))\\le C u^{S+\\chi}$$ for a fixed $\\chi>0$. Their cross directions lie in a fixed arbitrarily small spherical patch. For every sufficiently small fixed $\\omega>0$, there are decreasing Borel pair sets $\\Gamma_N\\subset E_1\\times E_2$ such that*\n\n1.  *$(\\mu_1\\otimes\\mu_2)(\\bigcap_N\\Gamma_N)>0$;*\n\n2.  *$\\Gamma_N$ is a disjoint union of at most $C2^{2d\\omega N}$ products of spatial Borel sets;*\n\n3.  *for every $e>0$, there are $c(e)>0$, $C_e<\\infty$ and Borel $B_N\\subset\\Gamma_N$ with $(\\mu_1\\otimes\\mu_2)(B_N)\\le C_e2^{-c(e)N}$, such that for $G_N=\\Gamma_N\\setminus B_N$, all $2^{-N}\\le u\\le1$, and all spherical caps $B(w,u)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     \\mu_2\\{y:(x,y)\\in G_N,\\ \\pi_x(y)\\in B(w,u)\\}\n         &\\le2^{eN}u^S,\\\\\n     \\mu_1\\{x:(x,y)\\in G_N,\\ \\pi_y(x)\\in B(w,u)\\}\n         &\\le2^{eN}u^S.\n    \\end{aligned}$$*\n\n*The last bounds integrate the original opposite marginal, without normalizing its individual fibers.       "claim_anchor": "stmt-test-profile-bounds",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-regularize-dyadic-tree",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-test-profile-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "There is a dimensional constant $C_d$ such that $$A_0=0,\\qquad |A_i-A_j|\\le d|i-j|/N,\\qquad\nA_n\\ge\\chi n/N-C_d\\sigma.$$ Every occupied depth-$n$ cube has mass $M(n)R^{\\pm C_d\\sigma}$. In particular, a ball of radius $C2^{-g}$ contains at most $$\\label{test:centre-count}\nC'R^{I(g,p)+C_d\\sigma}$$ occupied depth-$p$ cube centres, for $0\\le g\\le p\\le N$. Here $C$ is any fixed constant and $C'$ may depend on $C$. For integers $0\\le p\\le t\\le N$ and an occupied depth-$p$ cube $P$, the full conditional probability $\\rho_P=\\rho|_P/\\rho(P)$, rescaled by $z\\mapsto2^p(z-c_P)$, satisfies the $S$-ball bound down to $H^{-1}$, where $H=2^{t-p}$, with loss $$\\label{test:conditional-ball-loss}\nK=R^{A_p-\\min_{p\\le n\\le t}A_n+C_d\\sigma}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],

      "method": "Compare regular cell masses and divide by actual occupied parent mass.",
      "output": "Excess-profile bounds, occupied-center counts and conditional ball losses.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-test-profile-bounds"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-test-half-dimensional-fourier",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-test-half-dimensional-fourier",
          "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le H\\le R$, $K,L\\ge1$, and $S=d/2$. Suppose $\\alpha$ is a probability in a fixed bounded subset of $\\mathbb R^d$ with $$\\alpha(B(x,r))\\le Kr^S\\qquad(H^{-1}\\le r\\le1),$$ and $\\xi$ is a subprobability on $S^{d-1}$ with $$\\xi(B(w,r))\\le Lr^S\\qquad(R^{-1}\\le r\\le1).$$ Then, with Fourier convention $\\widehat\\alpha(v)=\\int e^{-iz\\cdot v}\\,d\\alpha(z)$, $$\\label{test:fourier-estimate}\n\\int_{-H}^{H}\\int_{S^{d-1}}|\\widehat\\alpha(sw)|^2\\,d\\xi(w)\\,ds\n \\le C LK(1+\\log H).$$ The constant depends only on $d$ and the fixed bound on the support’s diameter. In particular it does not require ball bounds below $H^{-1}$ or any Fourier decay.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"

          "exact_quote": "The constant $C_d$ in Definition <a href=\"#test:good-directions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"test:good-directions\">26</a> can be chosen depending only on $d$ so that the following holds for all fixed parameters and sufficiently large $N$. If $\\xi$ is any spherical subprobability satisfying $$\\xi(B(w,r))\\le R^\\sigma r^S\n       \\qquad(R^{-1}\\le r\\le1),$$ then every level-zero test, uniformly in the class and the occupied anchor cube, has $$\\label{test:exceptional-bound}\n\\xi(S^{d-1}\\setminus G_{0,\\mathfrak t}(P))\\le R^{-\\sigma}.$$",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-test-smooth-masks",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-test-smooth-masks",
          "exact_quote": "For a descriptor $\\mathfrak t$ of nominal length $l'$, put $$D_{l'}=\\max(1,2^{l'}R^{-50\\sigma}).$$ For all sufficiently large $N$, every $0\\le i<J_0$ admits a mask $w_{i,\\mathfrak t}(x,w)\\in[0,1]$, measurable in $x$ and smooth in $w$, such that, on every occupied anchor cube, $$\\label{test:mask-properties}\nw_{i,\\mathfrak t}=1\\text{ on }G_{i,\\mathfrak t},\\qquad\n\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}_w w_{i,\\mathfrak t}\\subset G_{i+1,\\mathfrak t},\\qquad\n\\|T_1\\cdots T_jw_{i,\\mathfrak t}\\|_\\infty\\le C_jD_{l'}^j.$$ Here the $T_j$ are any members of a fixed finite collection of smooth tangent fields spanning the tangent spaces of the sphere. The masks are antipodally symmetric and constant as functions of $x$ on each anchor cube; they are zero on unoccupied cubes and outside the root. Moreover, the $cD_{l'}^{-1}$-neighbourhood of $G_{i,\\mathfrak t}$ lies in $G_{i+1,\\mathfrak t}$, with the same assertion for its closure after decreasing the fixed $c$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],

      ],
      "method": "Compare consecutive width gaps and convolve buffered spherical neighborhoods.",
      "output": "Smooth masks retaining the next-level support condition.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-test-product-symbols",
      "depends_on": [

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-test-product-symbols",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a product with one factor for each occurrence in one or more descriptor lists whose total size is at most $N^{C_*}$, with $C_*$ fixed independently of $N$. A factor may be a mask $w_{i,\\mathfrak t}$ for any $0\\le i<J_0$, or a derivative of order $q$ divided by $D_{l'}^q$, where $l'$ is its nominal length. The indices may differ among factors. Assume the total order of these prescribed derivatives is bounded independently of $N$. A bounded number, independent of $N$, of measurable spatial multipliers and smooth angular factors are also allowed, with fixed numerical bounds for their sup norms and for all angular derivatives under consideration. These bounds are common to the family of products in any later supremum. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length, with $L=0$ for an empty list, and set $$\\label{test:total-symbol-scale}\nD_*=R^\\sigma\\max(1,2^LR^{-50\\sigma}).$$ At each fixed angular derivative order $j$, the resulting product $b$ satisfies $$\\|T_1\\cdots T_jb\\|_\\infty\\le C_jD_*^j.$$ For each factor with descriptor $\\mathfrak t$ and index $i$, the support of the product still imposes its $G_{i+1,\\mathfrak t}$ condition, including when that factor was already differentiated. All these assertions are pointwise in the spatial variables. Constants may depend on the fixed list-size exponent, prescribed derivative budget, and numerical bounds for the extra factors, as well as on the previously fixed parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-products-preserve-tests",
      "inputs": [
        "Mask products",
        "Fixed normalized derivative budget"

      "method": "Fixed-order product rule and polynomial multiplicity absorption.",
      "output": "Product derivative bounds and retained supports.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-test-product-symbols"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-test-descriptor-properties",
