        {
          "anchor": "stmt-test-descriptor-properties",
          "exact_quote": "For a nonterminal $[a,t]$, the union of the blocks eligible for its choice is an integer interval $[a_*,t_*]$ with $$a+q_0\\le a_*\\le a+2q_0,\\qquad\nt-2q_0\\le t_*\\le t-q_0,\n\\qquad A_n\\ge A_p\\quad(a_*\\le n\\le t_*).$$ The lists $\\mathcal M(a,t)$ are nested under interval containment. Across the scheduled lists, there are $O(1/\\kappa)$ possible anchors and polynomially many descriptors. For every $\\mathfrak t\\in\\mathcal M(a,t)$ with anchor $p$ and nominal length $l'$, there is an origin $[a',t']\\subseteq[a,t]$ such that $$\\label{test:descriptor-origin}\n[p-l',p+l']\\subseteq[a',t'].$$ That origin also supplies every parallel descriptor $[g,p]$ with $p-l'\\le g<p$. In particular $l'\\le(t-a)/2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-nested-descriptor-schedule",
      "inputs": [
        "Excess profile",
        "Interval policy"
      ],
      "method": "Fixed block representatives and membership-specific origin witnesses.",
      "output": "Nested lists, polynomial descriptor count and origin geometry.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-test-descriptor-properties"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-osc-symbol-bounds",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-products-preserve-tests"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-osc-symbol-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be the largest nominal length in the symbol’s list, or in all lists for a product of symbols, with $L=0$ when those lists are empty, and put $$\\label{osc:derivative-scale}\n D_*=R^\\sigma\\max(1,2^L R^{-50\\sigma}).$$ For every fixed $j$, an admissible symbol, or a product of a fixed number of endpoint symbols, obeys $$\\label{osc:angular-derivatives}\n \\sup_x\\|b_x\\|_{C^j(S^{d-1})}\\le C_{j,B}D_*^j$$ for sufficiently large $N$. The constant may depend on the fixed list-size exponents, parameters, derivative budgets and common angular bounds, but not on the spatial multipliers. On a depth-$a$ cell, the product of all mask factors whose anchors have depth at most $a$ is independent of the spatial integration variable and has uniformly bounded modulus. We call these anchors *reached* at depth $a$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-symbol-bounds",
      "inputs": [
        "Admissible symbols",
        "Current cell depth"
      ],
      "method": "Apply product estimates and spatial constancy of reached anchor masks.",
      "output": "Uniform derivatives and bounded reached factors.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-osc-symbol-bounds"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-osc-stationary",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-osc-stationary",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $b\\in C^\\infty(S^{n_*})$ obeys $\\|b\\|_{C^m}\\le C_m A^m$ at the finitely many orders needed below, where $A\\ge1$. Let $\\Delta\\ne0$, $e=\\Delta/|\\Delta|$, $\\Lambda=r|\\Delta|$, and $r>0$. A cutoff $\\psi$ is assumed to equal one on a fixed neighborhood of $e$ and zero on a fixed neighborhood of $-e$, with uniformly bounded derivatives. For every integer $J\\ge1$, $$\\label{osc:direct}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\\cdot\\Delta}\\psi(w)b(w)\\,dw\n &=\\Lambda^{-n_*/2}\n   \\left[e^{i\\Lambda}\\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\n                    \\Lambda^{-j}(L_jb)(e)+E_J\\right],\\\\\n |E_J|&\\le C_J\\Lambda^{-J}A^{2J+d+2},\n \\end{aligned}$$ provided $\\Lambda\\ge1$. Without a cutoff the corresponding formula is $$\\label{osc:full-sphere}\n \\int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\\cdot\\Delta}b(w)\\,dw\n =\\Lambda^{-n_*/2}\n   \\left[\\sum_{\\epsilon\\in\\{+1,-1\\}}e^{i\\epsilon\\Lambda}\n       \\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\\Lambda^{-j}\n                 (L_{j,\\epsilon}b)(\\epsilon e)+E_J'\\right],$$ with the same remainder bound. Define global differential operators $$\\label{osc:B-operators}\n B_0=L_0^{-1},\\qquad\n B_j=-L_0^{-1}\\sum_{h=1}^j L_h B_{j-h}.$$ Each $B_j$ is a polynomial in $\\Delta_{S^{n_*}}$ of differential order at most $2j$. If, for a fixed $c>0$, $$\\label{osc:stationary-regime}\n \\Lambda\\ge R^{c\\sigma},\\qquad\n A^2/\\Lambda\\le R^{-c\\sigma},\\qquad 1\\le A\\le R^2,$$ then for every prescribed $T>0$ a fixed sufficiently large $J$ gives $$\\label{osc:inverse}\n e^{i\\Lambda}b(e)\n =\\Lambda^{n_*/2}\\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\\Lambda^{-j}\n       \\int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\\cdot\\Delta}\\psi(w)(B_jb)(w)\\,dw\n       +O(R^{-T}).$$ The extracted remainders in Equations <a href=\"#osc:direct\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:direct\">[osc:direct]</a> and <a href=\"#osc:full-sphere\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:full-sphere\">[osc:full-sphere]</a> are also $O(R^{-T})$ after increasing $J$. All constants are uniform in $e$ in the stipulated geometry.*\n\n*For $r=2^v s$ with $s$ in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, each coefficient $\\Lambda^{-j}(L_{j,\\epsilon}b)(\\epsilon e)$ has uniformly bounded derivatives of every fixed order in $s$ under Equation <a href=\"#osc:stationary-regime\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:stationary-regime\">[osc:stationary-regime]</a>. Every differential coefficient retains the supports of all the mask factors in $b$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-global-stationary-expansions",
      "inputs": [
        "Smooth angular amplitude",
        "Stationary parameter regime"
      ],
      "method": "Quadratic stationary charts, global operator identification and formal inversion with controlled remainders.",
      "output": "Direct and inverse expansions preserving mask supports.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-osc-stationary"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-osc-spatial-separation",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-osc-spatial-separation",
          "exact_quote": "On separated depth-$a$ cells, for every fixed $j\\ge0$ there are bounded measurable functions $f_\\ell$ on $X$ and $g_\\ell$ on $Y$ and constants $c_\\ell$ such that $$\\label{osc:spatial-series}\n (D(x,y)2^a)^{-j}\n   =\\sum_\\ell c_\\ell f_\\ell(x)g_\\ell(y),\\qquad\n |f_\\ell|,|g_\\ell|\\le1,\\qquad\n \\sum_\\ell|c_\\ell|\\le C_{d,j}.$$ The convergence is uniform. The same assertion holds in the fixed outer geometry, with a constant depending on that geometry.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-separated-spatial-series",
      "inputs": [
        "Separated endpoint cells",
        "Fixed inverse distance power"
      ],
      "method": "Extend the smooth distance coefficient to a torus and split its Fourier series.",
      "output": "Uniform absolutely summable endpoint expansion.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-osc-spatial-separation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-osc-shell-conversion",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-symbol-bounds",
        "step-global-stationary-expansions",
        "step-separated-spatial-series"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-osc-shell-conversion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le a\\le v\\le N$ be integer depths, and let $X,Y$ be separated cells at depth $a$ of one or two classes. Let $m_x(w)$ and $n_y(w)$ be products of the undifferentiated masks at one index $i<J_0$ for two specified indexed descriptor lists. Assume both lists have at most $N^{C_{\\mathcal A}}$ occurrences, where one exponent $C_{\\mathcal A}$ is fixed independently of $N$ for the whole family under consideration, as in Definition <a href=\"#osc:symbols\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:symbols\">32</a>. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length in either list, with $L=0$ when both lists are empty. For measurable $|f|,|g|\\le1$ define a possibly complex measure $\\nu$ on the distance line by $$\\label{osc:weighted-measure}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\int F(s)\\,d\\nu(s)\n &=\\frac1{M_1(a)M_2(a)}\n   \\int_{X\\times Y} F(D(x,y))\\,\n       (D(x,y)2^a)^{-n_*/2}\\\\\n &\\qquad\\qquad\\cdot f(x)\\overline{g(y)}\\,\n       m_x(e(x,y))n_y(e(x,y))\\,\n       d\\rho_1(x)d\\rho_2(y).\n \\end{aligned}$$ If $$\\label{osc:shell-regime}\n v-a\\ge20\\sigma N,\\qquad L\\le(v-a)/2+\\sigma N,$$ then for every fixed $T>0$, $$\\label{osc:shell-estimate}\n 2^{-a}\\int_{2^{v-1}}^{2^{v+1}}|\\widehat\\nu(r)|^2\\,dr\n \\le R^{o(1)}\\sup\\mathsf H_F(a,v)+O(R^{-T}).$$ The supremum has the same cells, class normalizations, lists and smoothing index as in Equation <a href=\"#osc:weighted-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:weighted-measure\">[osc:weighted-measure]</a>. Its symbols are admissible with a derivative budget and common bounds for smooth angular factors depending only on $T,d,C_{\\mathcal A}$ and the fixed parameters. All these bounds are independent of $N$. When $f=g=1$, the measure $\\nu$ is positive. The assertion also holds on the fixed outer supports with $a=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-weighted-shell-conversion",
      "inputs": [
        "Distance-weighted measure",
        "Masks and bounded spatial factors",
        "Shell regime"
      ],
      "method": "Inverse stationary expansion, distance-power cancellation and endpoint factorization.",
      "output": "Shared-angle energy bound with unchanged lists.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-osc-spatial-separation-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-graph-localization",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-symbol-bounds"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-graph-localization",
          "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#graph:range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"graph:range\">[graph:range]</a>, replacing the expanded independent energy by its sum over the retained edges changes it by $O(R^{-T})$, for every fixed $T>0$. This replacement is made in the integrated oscillatory form, before taking absolute values of the expanded cell terms.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-nonstationary-edge-localization",
      "inputs": [
        "Expanded independent energy",
        "Graph depth margins"
      ],
      "method": "Radial or tangent-field integration by parts on entire cell-cap terms.",
      "output": "Arbitrary-power error outside retained edges.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-graph-localization"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-graph-degrees",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-profile-conditional-bounds"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-graph-degrees",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed cap pair, both the row degrees and the column degrees of the retained graph are at most $$\\label{graph:degree-bound}\n D_{\\mathrm{gr}}=\n R^{\\min_{j=1,2}k_j(a,p)+O_d(\\sigma)+o(1)}.$$ This estimate does not require the two cap directions to be separated.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-row-column-degree-control",
      "inputs": [
        "Retained cap-pair graph",
        "Parallel support tests"
      ],
      "method": "Use eligible witnesses and parallel tests, then confine the second unknown by scalar and perpendicular constraints.",
      "output": "Matching row and column degree bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-graph-degrees"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-graph-estimate",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-nonstationary-edge-localization",
        "step-row-column-degree-control"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-graph-estimate",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho_1,\\rho_2$ be two regularized class laws, possibly equal, and write $M_j$, $A_{j,n}$, $I_j$ and $k_j$ for their profiles and associated quantities from Section <a href=\"#test:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"test:section\">6</a>. Let $X,Y$ be occupied cells of depth $a$. Put $X_1=X$, $X_2=Y$, and let $b^{(j)}_z(w)$ be an admissible endpoint symbol on $X_j$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#osc:symbols\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:symbols\">32</a>. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length of a descriptor in either symbol, with $L=0$ if both lists are empty. Suppose that the integer depths satisfy $0\\le a<p\\le v\\le N$ and $$\\label{graph:range}\n p-a\\le v-p+2\\sigma N,\\qquad\n L\\le v-p+2\\sigma N,\\qquad v-p\\ge10\\sigma N.$$ At endpoint $j$, suppose that the symbol imposes the parallel test $[g_j,p]$ at a level $\\ell_j\\in\\{0,\\ldots,J_0\\}$: for $\\rho_j$-almost every $z\\in X_j$ and every $w\\in S^{d-1}$, $$\\label{graph:support-test}\n b^{(j)}_z(w)\\ne0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n w\\in G^{(j)}_{\\ell_j,[g_j,p]}(z).$$ The superscript $j$ indicates the class used in the test. Assume $$\\label{graph:anchor-range}\n a\\le g_j<p,\\qquad g_j\\le a+2\\sigma N.$$*\n\n\n*Form the general independent energy $\\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v)$ of Definition <a href=\"#osc:states\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:states\">34</a> using these specified symbols. For every fixed $T>0$, $$\\label{graph:bound}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v)\n &\\le\n R^{-\\sum_{j=1}^2(A_{j,p}-A_{j,a})\n       +\\min_{j=1,2}k_j(a,p)+O_d(\\sigma)+o(1)}\\\\\n &\\qquad\\cdot\n \\max_{\\substack{P\\subset X,\\ Q\\subset Y\\\\\n                   P,Q\\text{ occupied depth-}p\\text{ cells}}}\n       \\mathsf H^{P,Q}(p,v)\n +O(R^{-T}).\n \\end{split}$$ No separation of $X$ and $Y$ is required. Every child in <a href=\"#graph:bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"graph:bound\">[graph:bound]</a> is formed by restricting the same endpoint symbols to $P$ and $Q$. It retains every original mask factor, including any whose anchor is $p$. Constants are uniform over cells and allowed symbols with fixed list-size exponents and derivative budgets, and with fixed common numerical bounds for their smooth angular factors at the derivative orders used. The $o(1)$ tends to zero as $N\\to\\infty$ with these bounds, the parameters, and $T$ fixed.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-independent-angular-graph",
      "inputs": [
        "Parent symbols and support tests",
        "Child cell splitting"
      ],
      "method": "Retain oscillatory edges, apply both degree bounds and cancel profile normalizations.",
      "output": "Child independent energies retaining every endpoint mask.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-graph-degrees-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-graph-separated-form",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-independent-angular-graph"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-graph-separated-form",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#graph:estimate\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"graph:estimate\">38</a>, suppose also that $X,Y$ and their supported laws have the separated geometry used to define $\\mathsf H_F^{X,Y}(a,v)$ in Section <a href=\"#osc:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:section\">7</a>. Then <a href=\"#graph:bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"graph:bound\">[graph:bound]</a> holds with its left-hand side replaced by $\\mathsf H_F^{X,Y}(a,v)$ and with the same child energies. This includes the outer root $a=0$ with separated actual supports inside possibly equal depth-zero cells $X,Y$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-shared-angle-graph-transfer",
      "inputs": [
        "Separated shared-angle form"
      ],
      "method": "Angular Cauchy–Schwarz with identical endpoint data.",
      "output": "The independent graph bound for the shared-angle parent.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-graph-separated-form"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-rec-binning",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-binning",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\eta,\\zeta$ be finite positive measures on the line and let $\\varepsilon>0$. Write $$B_\\varepsilon(\\eta)\n =\\sum_{j\\in\\mathbb Z}\\eta([j\\varepsilon,(j+1)\\varepsilon))^2.$$ For any fixed $C\\ge1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n B_\\varepsilon(\\eta)\n &\\le(\\eta\\otimes\\eta)\\{|s-s'|\\le\\varepsilon\\},\n \\label{rec:bins-lower}\\\\\n \\sup_{u\\in\\mathbb R}\n (\\eta\\otimes\\eta)\\{|s-s'-u|\\le C\\varepsilon\\}\n &\\le C' B_\\varepsilon(\\eta),\n \\label{rec:bins-shift}\\\\\n \\iint(1+|s-s'|/\\varepsilon)^{-2}\\,d\\eta(s)d\\zeta(s')\n &\\le C' B_\\varepsilon(\\eta)^{1/2}\n                    B_\\varepsilon(\\zeta)^{1/2}.\n \\label{rec:bins-kernel}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $$\\label{rec:collision-fourier}\n (\\eta\\otimes\\eta)\\{|s-s'|\\le C\\varepsilon\\}\n \\le C'\\varepsilon\n       \\int_{|r|\\le\\varepsilon^{-1}}|\\widehat\\eta(r)|^2\\,dr.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-positive-collision-binning",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite positive line measures",
        "Collision tolerance"
      ],
      "method": "Cauchy–Schwarz on bin offsets and a positive Fourier majorant.",
      "output": "Shifted, kernel and Fourier collision bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-rec-binning"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-rec-reductions",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-nested-descriptor-schedule",
        "step-buffered-angular-masks",
        "step-weighted-shell-conversion",
        "step-independent-angular-graph",
        "step-shared-angle-graph-transfer",
        "step-positive-collision-binning",
        "step-global-stationary-expansions"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-reductions",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose the current state is nonterminal, its smoothing index satisfies $i<J_0$, and $N$ is sufficiently large with all parameters fixed. For every prescribed $T>0$, it is bounded, apart from an error $O(R^{-T})$ from oscillatory operations, by a sum of child bounds whose number is polynomial in $N$, and possibly a low-frequency stopping term. For every non-low child, if the old start is $a$ and the new start is $a'$, its multiplier is $$\\label{rec:transition-factor}\n R^{-2(A_{a'}-A_a)+\\mathrm{cost}+C_d\\sigma+o(1)}.$$ A low child has cost zero and the entire multiplier $R^{o(1)}$, with no fixed $C_d\\sigma$ loss. A low-frequency stopping term is $R^{C_d\\sigma+o(1)}$. The legal children are*\n\n<div class=\"center\">\n\n| *Reduction* | *Parent and child*                                                | *Cost*                        |\n|:------------|:------------------------------------------------------------------|:------------------------------|\n| *Suffix*    | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,p)$*                       | *$A_p-\\min_{[p,t]}A$*         |\n| *Low*       | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,v)$*                       | *$0$; full factor $R^{o(1)}$* |\n| *High*      | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{[a,t]}(p,v)$*                   | *$k(a,p)$*                    |\n| *Direct*    | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(p,v)$* | *$k(a,p)$*                    |\n| *Near*      | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(h,v)$* | *$0$*                         |\n| *Far*       | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(b,v)$*          | *$0$*                         |\n\n</div>\n\n*For a positive parent, $p$ is its current interval’s fixed policy choice. The suffix row is used when $2p>a+t$. When $2p\\le a+t$, the shell index is an integer $a<v\\le t$: the low row has $t-v\\ge\\sigma N$, and the high row has $0\\le t-v<\\sigma N$. A high child has base $[a,t]$ and carries the fixed enlarged derivative budget and common angular bounds produced by the inverse conversion; these depend on $T$ and the fixed parameters, not on $N$.*\n\n*For an angular parent, use $L_0,h$ from Equation <a href=\"#rec:angular-split-depth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rec:angular-split-depth\">[rec:angular-split-depth]</a>. If $h\\le a$, the direct row chooses a remaining descriptor of maximal length $L$, its anchor $p$, and an origin $[b_0,u_0]\\subseteq\\mathcal B$ for that membership. This choice has $L=L_0$, $a<p<v$, $$b_0\\le p-L,\\qquad p+L\\le u_0,\\qquad v-a\\le2L,$$ and it supplies the parallel descriptor $[\\max(a,p-L),p]$ used by the graph estimate. If $h>a$, the near row uses the displayed depth $h$. The far rows range over integer depths $b$ arising from the comparable far displacements, with $a<b\\le h+O_d(1)<v$, and use separated depth-$b$ cell pairs. Direct and near children keep the same base, index, derivative budget and common angular bounds. A far child has the fresh positive list $\\mathcal M(b,v)$.*\n\n\n*Finite maxima over child cells use the stated geometry. Whenever admissible symbols vary, take a supremum over the symbol family just specified. An empty collection of child terms contributes zero. Constants outside powers of $R$ may depend on the fixed parameters, derivative budgets and angular bounds. The constants $C_d$ in exponent losses depend only on $d$. A terminal state is bounded by $$\\label{rec:terminal}\n R^{C_d(\\kappa+\\sigma)+o(1)}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-legal-state-reductions",
      "inputs": [
        "Nonterminal positive or angular state",
        "Available smoothing index"
      ],
      "method": "Suffix linearization, low shell positivity, high graph entry and near-far angular splitting.",
      "output": "Legal child states with prescribed costs and retained supports.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-rec-reductions"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ledger-payment",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-legal-state-reductions",
        "step-nested-descriptor-schedule"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ledger-payment",
          "exact_quote": "If a reduction of Proposition <a href=\"#rec:reductions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rec:reductions\">45</a> has a potentially positive cost and parent interval $J$ and child interval $J'$, then $\\mathcal V(J')$ is defined and $$\\label{ledger:payment-inequality}\n \\mathrm{cost}\\le\\mathcal V(J')-\\mathcal V(J).$$ For every zero-cost reduction whose child potential is defined, the same inequality holds.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-potential-payment",
      "inputs": [
        "Legal transition",
        "Current-interval two-depth potential"
      ],
      "method": "Replace one admissible depth using the policy's inner-interval minimum.",
      "output": "Transition cost bounded by potential increase.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ledger-payment"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ledger-termination",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-legal-state-reductions"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ledger-termination",
          "exact_quote": "Every branch of the state reductions has at most $20/\\kappa$ non-low transitions and at most $1/\\sigma$ low transitions, for sufficiently small fixed parameters and sufficiently large $N$. Along such a branch the smoothing index increases by at most $20/\\kappa+1/\\sigma$. If the initial index is zero, every index required by the iteration, including the next support level used by a mask, is less than $$J_0=\\left\\lceil100(1/\\kappa+1/\\sigma)+100\\right\\rceil.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-branch-and-index-budget",
      "inputs": [
        "Legal recursive branch"
      ],
      "method": "Charge transitions to decreasing endpoints, passed representatives and angular episodes.",
      "output": "Finite depth and sufficient smoothing budget.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ledger-termination"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ledger-bound",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-potential-payment",
        "step-branch-and-index-budget"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ledger-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathsf H_0^{[c,N]}(c,N)$ be a recursive angular state of nonterminal width, as in Definition <a href=\"#rec:angular-state\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rec:angular-state\">43</a>. Fix its initial derivative budget $B$ and its common numerical angular bounds $Q_j$, independently of $N$, as in Definition <a href=\"#osc:symbols\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:symbols\">32</a>. Then $$\\label{ledger:bound-equation}\n \\mathsf H_0^{[c,N]}(c,N)\n \\le R^{2A_c-\\mathcal V([c,N])\n                   +C_d(\\kappa+\\sigma/\\kappa)+o(1)}.$$ This bound holds uniformly over its two occupied cells, including two copies of the same cell, and over its admissible bounded spatial multipliers and symbols with these fixed bounds. The $o(1)$ term may depend on $B$ and the $Q_j$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-strict-euclidean-distance-threshold**: The conclusion is positive Lebesgue measure of the Euclidean distance set of a compact set whose Hausdorff dimension strictly exceeds half the ambient dimension.
- **invariant-original-space-distances**: Auxiliary projections prepare directional information; every subsequent distance and oscillatory distance phase remains in the original ambient space.
- **invariant-unnormalized-directional-fibers**: The cap estimates integrate the original opposite marginal restricted by the pair condition. Individual directional fibers are not normalized separately.
- **invariant-independent-energy-mask-removal**: Reached mask factors can be removed at bounded cost only after forming the positive independent angular energy. This operation does not justify their deletion inside the shared angular integral before taking its absolute square.
- **invariant-localization-before-absolute-values**: Nonstationary cell terms are removed in the integrated oscillatory expression before absolute values are taken. The retained graph then requires control of both row and column degrees.
- **invariant-potential-pays-transition-costs**: Potentially positive transition costs are bounded by the increase of the potential on the actual current intervals. The child potential must be defined; the retained base and descriptor origin do not replace the current interval.
- **invariant-monotone-shell-reconstruction**: A decreasing sequence of finite positive measures has an absolutely continuous limit when its approximation errors in total variation and the square roots of its approximants' Fourier shell energies are summable.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-endpoint-extension**: Apply the positive-measure conclusion at the critical Hausdorff dimension. -> The strict dimension hypothesis is lost, and the paper makes no endpoint assertion.
- **anti-single-pin-guarantee**: Infer positive Lebesgue measure for distances from a single selected pin under the main theorem's threshold. -> An unpinned distance-set conclusion supplies no corresponding single-pin conclusion at that threshold.
- **anti-finite-sample-certificate**: Use an enumeration of distances in a finite point sample as a certificate of the main positive-measure theorem. -> Finite distance enumeration does not establish the strict Hausdorff dimension hypothesis for the compact support or the limiting measure's absolute continuity.
- **anti-projected-distance-substitution**: Replace original distances with distances between auxiliary projected points. -> The preparation estimates directions and incidences; the stated conclusion is carried by the original distance set.
- **anti-fiber-renormalization**: Normalize each surviving directional fiber to a probability before applying the prepared cap estimates. -> Dividing by an uncontrolled fiber mass changes the cap loss and invalidates the bound asserted for the original opposite marginal.
- **anti-shared-angle-mask-deletion**: Delete reached angular factors inside the shared-angle integral before its absolute square is formed. -> The bounded-modulus removal argument applies inside a positive independent angular integral and does not justify this operation on the shared form.
- **anti-premature-absolute-values**: Take absolute values of every expanded cell term before removing nonstationary interactions. -> This discards the oscillatory cancellation used to obtain the arbitrary-power localization error.
- **anti-shell-agreement-without-monotonicity**: Conclude absolute continuity from shell approximants while omitting monotonicity or either summability requirement. -> The reconstruction uses summable positive increments of the decreasing measures, together with both stated approximation sums.
- **anti-assumed-coarse-convergence-rate**: Assume a quantitative convergence rate for the decreasing coarse pair filters in order to pass to the limiting distance measure. -> The proof explicitly allows coarse pair restrictions without a quantitative convergence rate.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The target is the main positive-measure theorem for arbitrary compact supports. Completion requires an infinite limiting-measure argument: “Then $\alpha_\infty$ is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure.” The monotone measure shell criterion assumes summable approximation errors and summable square roots of Fourier shell energies over an unbounded sequence of scales. Directional preparation also uses compactness to obtain uniform gains. Although the paper contains finite-tree constructions, a finite enumeration of those constructions would not certify the support's Hausdorff dimension hypothesis or the absolute continuity of the nonzero limiting distance measure. No finite executable certificate for this target is supplied.

Let $\\tau_N$ be finite complex Borel measures such that $$\\label{asm:criterion-hypotheses}\n \\sum_{N\\ge K}\\|\\alpha_N-\\tau_N\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{N\\ge K}\n \\left(\\int_{2^{N-1}\\le |r|\\le2^{N+1}}\n                   |\\widehat{\\tau_N}(r)|^2\\,dr\\right)^{1/2}<\\infty.$$",
            "source_object": "stmt-asm-monotone-shell"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "asm:measure-telescoping",
            "exact_quote": "Monotonicity and finiteness give $$\\label{asm:measure-telescoping}\n \\sum_{n\\ge K}\\|\\zeta_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n       =\\alpha_K(\\mathbb R)-\\alpha_\\infty(\\mathbb R)<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\alpha_N-\\alpha_\\infty=\\sum_{n\\ge N}\\zeta_n\n                         \\quad\\hbox{in total variation}.$$",
            "source_object": "asm:measure-telescoping"
          }
        ],
        "failure_mode": "The reconstruction uses summable positive increments of the decreasing measures, together with both stated approximation sums.",
        "id": "anti-shell-agreement-without-monotonicity",
        "safer_action": "Verify all hypotheses of the monotone measure shell criterion before invoking its conclusion.",
        "tempting_use": "Conclude absolute continuity from shell approximants while omitting monotonicity or either summability requirement.",
        "verification": "PENDING",
        "violated_condition": "The underlying measures must decrease, the total-variation errors must be summable, and the square roots of shell energies must be summable."
      },
      {

            "anchor": "pm-h-6",

          },

    "reason": "The target is the main positive-measure theorem for arbitrary compact supports. Completion requires an infinite limiting-measure argument: “Then $\\alpha_\\infty$ is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure.” The monotone measure shell criterion assumes summable approximation errors and summable square roots of Fourier shell energies over an unbounded sequence of scales. Directional preparation also uses compactness to obtain uniform gains. Although the paper contains finite-tree constructions, a finite enumeration of those constructions would not certify the support's Hausdorff dimension hypothesis or the absolute continuity of the nonzero limiting distance measure. No finite executable certificate for this target is supplied.",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite-tree regularization proof supplies a concrete procedure for grouping weighted dyadic leaves into disjoint classes with controlled branching and mass profiles. A software implementation can reproduce this finite grouping when the required cube masses are supplied. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The distance conclusion concerns compact supports and Lebesgue measure, whereas a sampled game scene does not supply the required Hausdorff dimension hypothesis or a continuum distance measure. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniformity argument establishes the existence of gain constants and a scale threshold by contradiction. It does not provide a complete computable contract for an automatic tool that certifies those constants from arbitrary input measures. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The regularization proof constructs labels through successive mass bins and child-count bins. This gives an explicit finite partition algorithm once the dyadic tree, its masses and the selected depths are available. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Regular classes can be represented by labeled leaf groups in a shared dyadic tree. The proof explicitly allows a parent to carry several labels while requiring the corresponding leaf sets to remain disjoint. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The symbol-product estimates control angular derivatives and retained supports. They specify no accelerator execution model, memory layout or hardware resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The incidence gain bounds the mass captured by geometric tube covers under point and direction nonconcentration hypotheses. It supplies no adversary model or cryptographic security construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main result provides a qualitative property of all Euclidean distances from a compact support satisfying a strict Hausdorff dimension condition. It supplies no learning procedure, sample guarantee or embedding validation method. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The depth potential pays costs within a prescribed analytic recursion. Its payment inequality does not establish an operations-research objective, feasible-set model or optimizer for external scheduling problems. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The retained graph has bounded row and column degrees for oscillatory cell terms. Its edges are geometric constraints rather than communication links, and the result contains no distributed protocol construction. |
| other (Harmonic analysis and geometric measure theory) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The monotone shell criterion gives an analytic construction of a density for a limiting positive measure from summable approximation errors, summable shell norms and summable positive increments. It can be adapted to other measure constructions satisfying those exact conditions. |

      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-reg-regularization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$, let $\\rho$ be a probability on a unit dyadic cube in $\\mathbb R^n$, and put $R=2^N$. For every fixed $0<\\lambda<1$ and all sufficiently large $N$, there are pairwise disjoint sets $E_\\alpha$, each a union of depth-$N$ cubes, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *Their number is at most $N^{C_n/\\lambda}$, each has $\\rho(E_\\alpha)\\ge R^{-\\lambda}$, and $$\\rho\\Bigl(\\mathbb R^n\\setminus\\bigcup_\\alpha E_\\alpha\\Bigr)\n          \\le R^{-2}+N^{C_n/\\lambda}R^{-\\lambda}\n          =R^{-\\lambda+o(1)}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For $\\rho_\\alpha=\\rho|_{E_\\alpha}/\\rho(E_\\alpha)$ there is a nondecreasing $n$-Lipschitz function $m_\\alpha:[0,1]\\to[0,n]$, with $m_\\alpha(0)=0$, such that every occupied depth-$j$ cube satisfies $$\\label{reg:cell-mass}\n          R^{-m_\\alpha(j/N)-C_n\\lambda}\n          \\le\\rho_\\alpha(Q)\\le\n          R^{-m_\\alpha(j/N)+C_n\\lambda},\\qquad 0\\le j\\le N.$$*\n\n\n3.  *If $\\rho(B(x,r))\\le Kr^s$ for $R^{-1}\\le r\\le1$, with $0\\le s\\le n$ and $K\\ge1$, then $$\\label{reg:profile-lower-bound}\n          m_\\alpha(u)\\ge su-C_n\\lambda-\\log_R K,\n          \\qquad 0\\le u\\le1.$$*\n\n\n*Here and below an occupied cube means a cube of positive mass for the specified class. In particular, for occupied $P\\subset Q$ at depths $b\\ge a$, $$\\label{reg:conditional-mass}\n\\frac{\\rho_\\alpha(P)}{\\rho_\\alpha(Q)}\n =R^{-[m_\\alpha(b/N)-m_\\alpha(a/N)]\\pm C_n\\lambda}.$$ Constants depend only on $n$; the threshold for $N$ can depend on $\\lambda$.",
          "source_object": "stmt-reg-regularization"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-reg-regularization",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-reg-regularization</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Discard depth-$N$ cubes of mass less than $R^{-n-2}$. There are at most $R^n$ such cubes, so this costs at most $R^{-2}$. Choose depths $$0=j_0<j_1<\\cdots<j_J=N,\n\\qquad j_i-j_{i-1}\\le\\lambda N+1,\n\\qquad J\\le C/\\lambda.$$ Give each remaining leaf its dyadic mass bin as an initial label. There are $O_n(N)$ possible initial labels. Work upwards through the selected depths. If $\\ell$ is a label at depth $j_i$, count, within each depth-$j_{i-1}$ parent, the depth-$j_i$ children carrying $\\ell$; extend $\\ell$ by the dyadic bin of this positive count. The extension is given to all leaves of label $\\ell$ in that parent. A parent can carry several labels, but the sets of leaves having different labels are disjoint. At every extension there are at most $O_n(N)$ possible count bins. Consequently the number of final labels is at most $N^{C_n/\\lambda}$, after adjusting constants for sufficiently large $N$.\n\nFix a final label. For each $i$ there is a positive integer $b_i$, which is a power of two, such that every occupied depth-$j_{i-1}$ parent has between $b_i$ and $2b_i$ occupied depth-$j_i$ children in this label. To check that these comparisons survive the later extensions, fix a partial label and a node at the current selected depth. All leaves with that label in this node receive the same extension, determined by its parent and the old label. At each subsequent, higher step the extension is again constant on this entire group. Backward induction through the selected depths therefore shows that a final label retains or removes each earlier child group as a whole; it never splits that group. Consequently the branching comparisons hold simultaneously. Also $$1\\le b_i\\le 2^{n(j_i-j_{i-1})}.$$ All leaf masses are in one dyadic bin. Counting leaves below a selected node, and then normalizing the final class, therefore gives $$\\rho_\\alpha(Q)=2^{\\pm C J}\n                  \\Bigl(\\prod_{h\\le i}b_h\\Bigr)^{-1}\n\\quad\\text{at depth }j_i.$$ Define $$m_\\alpha(j_i/N)=\\frac1N\\sum_{h\\le i}\\log_2b_h$$ and interpolate linearly. Its slopes lie in $[0,n]$ exactly. At an intermediate depth, an occupied cube is contained in its preceding selected-depth parent and contains an occupied descendant at the next selected depth. Both the possible mass variation and the profile variation across this gap are at most $R^{n\\lambda+O_n(1/N)}$. The remaining comparison error is $2^{O(J)}$, whose base-$R$ logarithm is $O(1/(\\lambda N))$. For fixed $\\lambda$ and sufficiently large $N$ these errors imply Equation <a href=\"#reg:cell-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:cell-mass\">[reg:cell-mass]</a>.\n\nDiscard final classes of original mass less than $R^{-\\lambda}$. Their total mass is bounded by the number of labels times $R^{-\\lambda}$, as asserted. For any retained class, normalization multiplies an original upper ball bound by at most $R^\\lambda$. Covering a depth-$j$ cube by a bounded number of balls of radius $2^{-j}$ gives $$\\rho_\\alpha(Q)\\le C_nKR^\\lambda2^{-sj}.$$ Comparison with the lower bound in Equation <a href=\"#reg:cell-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:cell-mass\">[reg:cell-mass]</a> proves Equation <a href=\"#reg:profile-lower-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:profile-lower-bound\">[reg:profile-lower-bound]</a> at integer depths, with a larger dimensional constant. Linear interpolation gives it at every $u$. Dividing two instances of Equation <a href=\"#reg:cell-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:cell-mass\">[reg:cell-mass]</a> proves Equation <a href=\"#reg:conditional-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:conditional-mass\">[reg:conditional-mass]</a>. ◻",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-reg-regularization"
        }
      ],

Their total mass is bounded by the number of labels times $R^{-\\lambda}$, as asserted. For any retained class, normalization multiplies an original upper ball bound by at most $R^\\lambda$. Covering a depth-$j$ cube by a bounded number of balls of radius $2^{-j}$ gives $$\\rho_\\alpha(Q)\\le C_nKR^\\lambda2^{-sj}.$$ Comparison with the lower bound in Equation <a href=\"#reg:cell-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:cell-mass\">[reg:cell-mass]</a> proves Equation <a href=\"#reg:profile-lower-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:profile-lower-bound\">[reg:profile-lower-bound]</a> at integer depths, with a larger dimensional constant. Linear interpolation gives it at every $u$. Dividing two instances of Equation <a href=\"#reg:cell-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:cell-mass\">[reg:cell-mass]</a> proves Equation <a href=\"#reg:conditional-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"reg:conditional-mass\">[reg:conditional-mass]</a>. ◻",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-reg-regularization"

          "exact_quote": "Let $(\\alpha_N)_{N\\ge K}$ be finite positive Borel measures on $\\mathbb R$ with $\\alpha_{N+1}\\le\\alpha_N$, and let $\\alpha_\\infty=\\lim_N\\alpha_N$. Let $\\tau_N$ be finite complex Borel measures such that $$\\label{asm:criterion-hypotheses}\n \\sum_{N\\ge K}\\|\\alpha_N-\\tau_N\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{N\\ge K}\n \\left(\\int_{2^{N-1}\\le |r|\\le2^{N+1}}\n                   |\\widehat{\\tau_N}(r)|^2\\,dr\\right)^{1/2}<\\infty.$$ Then $\\alpha_\\infty$ is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure. In particular, the conclusion holds if the two quantities inside these sums are respectively $O(2^{-\\eta N})$ and $O(2^{-\\gamma N/2})$ for arbitrary $\\eta,\\gamma>0$.",
          "source_object": "stmt-asm-monotone-shell"

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-asm-monotone-shell",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-asm-monotone-shell</summary>\n\n\n*Proof.* Choose $\\vartheta\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R)$ equal to one on $[-1,1]$ and supported in $[-2,2]$. Let $Q_N$ be convolution with the inverse Fourier transform of $\\vartheta(2^{-N}r)$, and put $P_N=Q_N-Q_{N-1}$. The multiplier of $P_N$ is supported in $\\{2^{N-1}\\le|r|\\le2^{N+1}\\}$. Dilation of the fixed Schwartz kernel gives a constant $C_\\vartheta$ such that, for every finite measure $\\nu$, $$\\label{asm:low-cutoff-bound}\n \\|Q_N\\nu\\|_{L^1}\\le C_\\vartheta\\|\\nu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}},\\qquad\n \\left\\|\\sum_{N=a}^bP_N\\nu\\right\\|_{L^1}\n \\le2C_\\vartheta\\|\\nu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\quad(a\\le b).$$ The second bound follows from telescoping to $Q_b-Q_{a-1}$; in particular its constant is independent of the number of bands.\n\n\nWith the convention $\\widehat\\nu(r)=\\int e^{-irt}\\,d\\nu(t)$, Plancherel (Mattila 1995, equation (12.4)) [[Mattila1995](#ref-Mattila1995)] and the uniformly bounded band multipliers give $$\\|P_N\\tau_N\\|_{L^2}\n \\lesssim\n \\left(\\int_{2^{N-1}\\le |r|\\le2^{N+1}}\n                   |\\widehat{\\tau_N}(r)|^2\\,dr\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Thus $$\\label{asm:two-convergent-series}\n G:=\\sum_{N\\ge K}P_N\\tau_N\\quad\\hbox{converges in }L^2,\n \\qquad\n H:=\\sum_{N\\ge K}P_N(\\alpha_N-\\tau_N)\n                         \\quad\\hbox{converges in }L^1.$$ Both series converge absolutely in the indicated Banach space.\n\n\n\nIt remains to account for the variation of $\\alpha_N$. Set $\\zeta_n=\\alpha_n-\\alpha_{n+1}\\ge0$. Monotonicity and finiteness give $$\\label{asm:measure-telescoping}\n \\sum_{n\\ge K}\\|\\zeta_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n       =\\alpha_K(\\mathbb R)-\\alpha_\\infty(\\mathbb R)<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\alpha_N-\\alpha_\\infty=\\sum_{n\\ge N}\\zeta_n\n                         \\quad\\hbox{in total variation}.$$ For a finite $M\\ge K$, define $Z_M=\\sum_{N=K}^MP_N(\\alpha_N-\\alpha_\\infty)$. Substituting Equation <a href=\"#asm:measure-telescoping\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"asm:measure-telescoping\">[asm:measure-telescoping]</a> into this finite sum and using the bounded operators from measures to $L^1$ gives the exact identity $$\\label{asm:finite-band-identity}\n Z_M=\\sum_{n\\ge K}\n             (Q_{\\min(n,M)}-Q_{K-1})\\zeta_n.$$ For each fixed $M$ the series on the right converges in $L^1$. Moreover, Equation <a href=\"#asm:low-cutoff-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"asm:low-cutoff-bound\">[asm:low-cutoff-bound]</a> shows that $$Z:=\\sum_{n\\ge K}(Q_n-Q_{K-1})\\zeta_n$$ converges absolutely in $L^1$. Subtracting the two series cancels all $n\\le M$, and gives $$\\label{asm:variation-tail}\n \\begin{split}\n \\|Z_M-Z\\|_{L^1}\n &\\le2C_\\vartheta\\sum_{n>M}\\|\\zeta_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\\\\n &=2C_\\vartheta\n             \\|\\alpha_{M+1}-\\alpha_\\infty\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\end{split}$$ This is where monotonicity is used. The original double series has not been rearranged without a convergence justification.\n\n\nFinally, finite telescoping gives $$\\label{asm:reconstruction}\n Q_M\\alpha_\\infty\n =Q_{K-1}\\alpha_\\infty\n  +\\sum_{N=K}^MP_N\\tau_N\n  +\\sum_{N=K}^MP_N(\\alpha_N-\\tau_N)-Z_M.$$ The fixed low-frequency term $Q_{K-1}\\alpha_\\infty$ is a smooth $L^1$ function by convolution with a Schwartz kernel. By Equations <a href=\"#asm:two-convergent-series\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"asm:two-convergent-series\">[asm:two-convergent-series]</a> and <a href=\"#asm:variation-tail\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"asm:variation-tail\">[asm:variation-tail]</a>, the right side converges in $L^1+L^2$, hence in distributions, to $$f=Q_{K-1}\\alpha_\\infty+G+H-Z\\in L^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbb R).$$ The kernels defining $Q_M$ form an approximate identity, so the left side of Equation <a href=\"#asm:reconstruction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"asm:reconstruction\">[asm:reconstruction]</a> converges in distributions to $\\alpha_\\infty$. Therefore $\\alpha_\\infty=f\\,\\mathcal L^1$. Since $\\alpha_\\infty$ is a finite positive measure, this locally integrable density is nonnegative almost everywhere and belongs to $L^1(\\mathbb R)$. This proves absolute continuity. ◻",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-asm-monotone-shell"
        },

Consequently $\\mathcal L^1(\\Delta(E))>0$.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-compact-euclidean-ambient-scope** (hypothesis): The main conclusion requires a compact subset of the stated Euclidean ambient space and the stated integer dimension range. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem to an arbitrary noncompact support without a separate reduction.', 'Replace Euclidean length by a graph metric or another norm.', 'Apply the theorem outside its stated ambient dimension range.']
- **limit-strict-dimension-margin** (edge_case): The Hausdorff dimension inequality is strict; the paper makes no endpoint positive-measure assertion. Blocked use: ['Infer positive Lebesgue measure at equality in the dimension threshold.', 'Replace the strict dimension hypothesis with a finite-sample estimate.']
- **limit-unpinned-conclusion** (formal_scope): The conclusion concerns the full distance set and does not assert a corresponding single-pin result at the main threshold. Blocked use: ['Guarantee positive measure for distances from a prescribed pin.', "Treat the theorem as a guarantee for a single sensor's distance distribution."]
- **limit-qualitative-positive-measure** (formal_scope): The main statement guarantees positive Lebesgue measure without a numerical lower bound, an interval conclusion or a finite distance-enumeration procedure. Blocked use: ['Report a quantitative lower bound for the measure of the distance set from this statement alone.', 'Infer that the distance set contains an interval.', 'Use finite point-cloud enumeration as a certificate of the continuum theorem.']
- **limit-energy-and-projection-ranges** (hypothesis): Energy transfer requires the stated strict exponent ranges. Projection preservation holds for almost every admissible plane, rather than for every chosen projection. Blocked use: ['Deduce finite energy at the ball-bound exponent without an additional argument.', 'Choose an arbitrary projection and assume energy preservation.', "Project to a dimension outside the energy lemma's admissible range."]
- **limit-uniformity-fixed-background** (nonconstructive): Sequential uniformity requires monotonicity of the loss classes and gain conclusions, with background parameters fixed. Its contradiction argument supplies existence of constants rather than their numerical values. Blocked use: ['Vary the background dimension or transversality parameters during the compactness passage.', 'Invoke uniformity without the sequential subsequence hypothesis.', 'Report computed gain constants from the existence argument alone.']
- **limit-regularization-fixed-loss-and-supplied-masses** (hypothesis): Regularization uses a probability on a unit dyadic cube, a fixed loss parameter and sufficiently large depth. Its finite labeling construction presupposes access to the relevant cube masses and does not give an implementation runtime. Blocked use: ['Treat a bound on the number of output classes as an execution-time bound.', 'Assume a fixed deletion exponent follows merely from vanishing regularization losses.', 'Use approximate mass-bin comparisons without checking the resulting class bounds.']
- **limit-pruning-predeletion-density** (hypothesis): Multiplication of local fiber bounds uses density measured in the pair relation before the final density deletion. The physical-line variant additionally requires bounded sets and removal of diagonal pairs. Blocked use: ['Assume the final surviving relation automatically satisfies the same density threshold.', 'Multiply fiber estimates without the local witness tests.', 'Use the line-width variant on diagonal pairs.']
- **limit-angular-gain-endpoint-transversality** (hypothesis): The linear angular gain requires the stated point and direction ball bounds, sparse direction-specific tube covers and, at the integer endpoint, the additional hyperplane nonconcentration bound. Blocked use: ['Use the endpoint gain with only a direction ball bound.', 'Replace the permitted tube-cover count by an unrestricted cover.', 'Drop the strict separation between the point and direction exponents.']
- **limit-original-label-fiber-covers** (formal_scope): Projected coordinates may coincide while retaining different original incidence labels. The covering condition belongs to each original direction label. Blocked use: ['Identify coincident projected direction coordinates and assume a common fiber cover.', 'Discard label identity during planar sampling or slice selection.']
- **limit-radial-integrability-auxiliary-space** (hypothesis): The radial integrability lemma requires bounded separated probabilities with the stated finite energies. Original-space radial surface densities are not assumed by the main proof. Blocked use: ['Infer surface densities for every original-space radial projection from the main dimension hypothesis.', 'Apply the radial integrability lemma without separation or its finite-energy hypotheses.']
- **limit-thin-upgrade-and-coarse-filter-inputs** (hypothesis): The thin-tube upgrade needs separated Frostman laws, uniform plate bounds and availability of the initial exponent. The coarse-filter construction additionally assumes zero mass on every affine hyperplane. Blocked use: ['Bootstrap cap exponents without establishing availability in both orientations.', 'Construct the coarse filter for a hyperplane-charging law without handling the exceptional alternative.', 'Treat the original Frostman bound as a substitute for the plate hypothesis.']
- **limit-parameter-order-and-deletion-gain** (hypothesis): The Frostman gap and measures are fixed before the later parameters. The coarse cutoff is chosen before the plate exponent and thin-tube upgrade; the resulting deletion exponent is only required to be positive. Blocked use: ['Require the deletion exponent to dominate the coarse cutoff or Fourier saving.', 'Choose the plate exponent independently of the coarse-filter construction.', 'Change earlier parameters after invoking the upgrade.']
- **limit-original-distances-after-projection** (formal_scope): Auxiliary projections control directions and incidences. Subsequent distances and oscillatory distance phases remain in the original space. Blocked use: ['Replace the final distance map by the distance between projected endpoints.', 'Transfer positive measure for auxiliary distances directly to the original distance set.']
- **limit-fiber-and-class-normalizations** (hypothesis): Prepared directional fibers integrate the original opposite marginal without individual normalization. Profile model masses and actual occupied-cell masses remain distinct. Blocked use: ['Divide every surviving fiber by its mass while retaining the original cap loss.', 'Replace actual conditional normalization by profile model mass without accounting for the comparison factor.', 'Recombine normalized classes without restoring their original weights.']
- **limit-full-law-tests-and-radius-contracts** (hypothesis): Parallel tests count all occupied centers in their prescribed geometry, and projection tests use the full conditional class law. The critical Fourier estimate requires both bounds on their stated radius intervals. Blocked use: ['Redefine a good-direction test after a pair restriction.', 'Use a restricted conditional law in place of the full law defining the projection test.', 'Apply the Fourier bound when either required scale interval is uncovered.']
- **limit-symbol-budgets-and-stationary-regime** (hypothesis): Symbol estimates require fixed list-size exponents, fixed derivative budgets and common angular bounds independent of depth. Stationary inversion additionally requires its phase-to-derivative-scale regime. Blocked use: ['Let the persistent derivative budget grow with depth while claiming the stated uniform estimate.', 'Invoke inverse stationary expansion outside its scale inequalities.', 'Introduce radial dependence into endpoint symbols without a new argument.']
- **limit-weighted-shell-conversion** (hypothesis): Shell conversion uses separated supported geometry, the specified distance weight, endpoint masks and the stated frequency and nominal-length conditions. Blocked use: ['Remove the distance weight while retaining the same shell normalization.', 'Apply the conversion on unseparated supported laws.', 'Use descriptors longer than the permitted shell regime.']
- **limit-mask-removal-positive-energy** (formal_scope): Reached factors may be removed at bounded cost inside the positive independent energy. This does not justify deletion inside the shared angular integral before its absolute square. Blocked use: ['Delete angular masks before converting the shared-angle form to independent energy.', 'Remove unreached factors merely because they complicate the endpoint symbol.']
- **limit-graph-localization-and-both-degrees** (hypothesis): The graph estimate requires its depth inequalities, admissible symbols and parallel support tests. Nonstationary localization precedes absolute values, and the retained graph needs both row and column degree bounds. Blocked use: ['Take absolute values before removing nonstationary cell terms.', 'Use only a row-degree estimate in the retained-term bound.', 'Drop the parallel support implication or its anchor-range conditions.']
- **limit-recursive-state-contract** (hypothesis): Legal reductions require a nonterminal current state, an available smoothing index and fixed parameters. Angular states retain their specified base and unreached descriptor list, while far children receive fresh positive lists. Blocked use: ['Apply an arbitrary interval transition and assume the published multiplier.', 'Delete masks for a far expansion using the removal performed for a near child.', 'Iterate beyond the available smoothing levels.', 'Treat the retained base as the current interval when deciding terminality.']
- **limit-potential-defined-current-interval** (hypothesis): The potential is undefined on shorter intervals. Payment uses the actual parent and child current intervals with the stated depth separation, rather than substituting a descriptor origin or retained base. Blocked use: ['Evaluate the potential on a shorter interval by silently assigning a value.', 'Pay transition costs using a different interval from the current state.', 'Apply the payment inequality to an external scheduling objective without proving the required profile geometry.']
- **limit-shell-masks-versus-decreasing-filters** (approximation): The coarse filters decrease, but the additional exceptional deletions need not be nested or have a product partition. Shell approximants depend on their scale-specific classes and masks. Blocked use: ['Assume the cap-controlled pair sets themselves form a decreasing sequence.', 'Use the fine exceptional deletion as a product multiplier in the shell estimate without a partition argument.', 'Identify the shell approximant with the decreasing original-mass measure.']
- **limit-monotone-shell-summability** (hypothesis): Absolute-continuity reconstruction requires finite positive decreasing measures and both stated summability conditions. Summability is required for the square roots of shell energies, not merely for the energies. Blocked use: ['Replace decreasing positive measures by arbitrary signed or nonmonotone measures.', 'Infer the conclusion from finitely many measured shells.', 'Replace summability of shell norms by summability of their squares without a separate proof.', 'Claim a globally square-integrable limiting density from the stated conclusion.']
- **limit-no-coarse-convergence-rate** (nonconstructive): The decreasing coarse pair restrictions need not converge at a quantitative rate. Reconstruction accounts for their variation through summable positive increments. Blocked use: ['Assign an exponential convergence rate to the coarse filters from the approximant error bound.', 'Use the approximation rate as a rate for convergence to the limiting measure.']
- **limit-nonzero-supported-limit** (hypothesis): Absolute continuity alone does not prove positive measure of the distance set. Completion also uses a nonzero positive limiting measure carried by the original distance set. Blocked use: ['Conclude positive measure from an absolutely continuous zero limit.', 'Use a limiting measure supported on auxiliary distances as the final witness.', 'Omit positive limiting pair mass when assembling the distance measure.']
- **limit-no-finite-certificate-for-main-target** (nonconstructive): The main target concerns arbitrary compact supports and a limiting absolutely continuous measure. Finite-tree constructions do not by themselves certify the support-level dimension hypothesis or the infinite reconstruction conditions. Blocked use: ['Treat successful finite regularization as an executable certificate of the main theorem.', 'Treat numerical shell agreement as a proof of absolute continuity of the limiting distance measure.']

          "anchor": "stmt-osc-symbols",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an indexed descriptor list $\\mathcal A=(\\mathfrak t_\\nu)_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal I}$ with $\\#\\mathcal I\\le N^{C_{\\mathcal A}}$, a smoothing index $i\\in\\{0,\\ldots,J_0-1\\}$, and a nonnegative integer derivative budget $B$. The exponent $C_{\\mathcal A}$ and budget $B$ are fixed independently of $N$ and are common to the symbol family under consideration. At each angular order under consideration, also fix a common numerical bound $Q_j$ independent of $N$. An admissible endpoint symbol has the form $$\\label{osc:symbol-form}\n b_x(w)=f(x)q(w)\n       \\prod_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal I}\n       D_{l_{\\mathfrak t_\\nu}}^{-|\\alpha_\\nu|}\n       \\Omega^{\\alpha_\\nu}w_{i,\\mathfrak t_\\nu}(x,w),\n \\qquad \\sum_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal I}|\\alpha_\\nu|\\le B.$$ The index $\\nu$ distinguishes occurrences, so repeated descriptors may receive different derivative words. Here $f$ is an arbitrary measurable spatial function with $|f|\\le1$, and $q$ is a smooth angular function satisfying $\\|q\\|_{C^j(S^{d-1})}\\le Q_j$ at every order under consideration. The constants in estimates may depend on $C_{\\mathcal A}$, $B$ and the fixed $Q_j$. Fixed scalar factors can be absorbed in those estimates. Empty lists and empty products are allowed, as are complex conjugates of such symbols. Symbols do not depend on the radial variable $r$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-osc-stationary",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $b\\in C^\\infty(S^{n_*})$ obeys $\\|b\\|_{C^m}\\le C_m A^m$ at the finitely many orders needed below, where $A\\ge1$. Let $\\Delta\\ne0$, $e=\\Delta/|\\Delta|$, $\\Lambda=r|\\Delta|$, and $r>0$. A cutoff $\\psi$ is assumed to equal one on a fixed neighborhood of $e$ and zero on a fixed neighborhood of $-e$, with uniformly bounded derivatives. For every integer $J\\ge1$, $$\\label{osc:direct}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\\cdot\\Delta}\\psi(w)b(w)\\,dw\n &=\\Lambda^{-n_*/2}\n   \\left[e^{i\\Lambda}\\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\n                    \\Lambda^{-j}(L_jb)(e)+E_J\\right],\\\\\n |E_J|&\\le C_J\\Lambda^{-J}A^{2J+d+2},\n \\end{aligned}$$ provided $\\Lambda\\ge1$. Without a cutoff the corresponding formula is $$\\label{osc:full-sphere}\n \\int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\\cdot\\Delta}b(w)\\,dw\n =\\Lambda^{-n_*/2}\n   \\left[\\sum_{\\epsilon\\in\\{+1,-1\\}}e^{i\\epsilon\\Lambda}\n       \\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\\Lambda^{-j}\n                 (L_{j,\\epsilon}b)(\\epsilon e)+E_J'\\right],$$ with the same remainder bound. Define global differential operators $$\\label{osc:B-operators}\n B_0=L_0^{-1},\\qquad\n B_j=-L_0^{-1}\\sum_{h=1}^j L_h B_{j-h}.$$ Each $B_j$ is a polynomial in $\\Delta_{S^{n_*}}$ of differential order at most $2j$. If, for a fixed $c>0$, $$\\label{osc:stationary-regime}\n \\Lambda\\ge R^{c\\sigma},\\qquad\n A^2/\\Lambda\\le R^{-c\\sigma},\\qquad 1\\le A\\le R^2,$$ then for every prescribed $T>0$ a fixed sufficiently large $J$ gives $$\\label{osc:inverse}\n e^{i\\Lambda}b(e)\n =\\Lambda^{n_*/2}\\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\\Lambda^{-j}\n       \\int_{S^{n_*}}e^{irw\\cdot\\Delta}\\psi(w)(B_jb)(w)\\,dw\n       +O(R^{-T}).$$ The extracted remainders in Equations <a href=\"#osc:direct\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:direct\">[osc:direct]</a> and <a href=\"#osc:full-sphere\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:full-sphere\">[osc:full-sphere]</a> are also $O(R^{-T})$ after increasing $J$. All constants are uniform in $e$ in the stipulated geometry.*\n\n*For $r=2^v s$ with $s$ in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, each coefficient $\\Lambda^{-j}(L_{j,\\epsilon}b)(\\epsilon e)$ has uniformly bounded derivatives of every fixed order in $s$ under Equation <a href=\"#osc:stationary-regime\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:stationary-regime\">[osc:stationary-regime]</a>. Every differential coefficient retains the supports of all the mask factors in $b$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-symbol-budgets-and-stationary-regime",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-osc-shell-conversion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le a\\le v\\le N$ be integer depths, and let $X,Y$ be separated cells at depth $a$ of one or two classes. Let $m_x(w)$ and $n_y(w)$ be products of the undifferentiated masks at one index $i<J_0$ for two specified indexed descriptor lists. Assume both lists have at most $N^{C_{\\mathcal A}}$ occurrences, where one exponent $C_{\\mathcal A}$ is fixed independently of $N$ for the whole family under consideration, as in Definition <a href=\"#osc:symbols\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:symbols\">32</a>. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length in either list, with $L=0$ when both lists are empty. For measurable $|f|,|g|\\le1$ define a possibly complex measure $\\nu$ on the distance line by $$\\label{osc:weighted-measure}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\int F(s)\\,d\\nu(s)\n &=\\frac1{M_1(a)M_2(a)}\n   \\int_{X\\times Y} F(D(x,y))\\,\n       (D(x,y)2^a)^{-n_*/2}\\\\\n &\\qquad\\qquad\\cdot f(x)\\overline{g(y)}\\,\n       m_x(e(x,y))n_y(e(x,y))\\,\n       d\\rho_1(x)d\\rho_2(y).\n \\end{aligned}$$ If $$\\label{osc:shell-regime}\n v-a\\ge20\\sigma N,\\qquad L\\le(v-a)/2+\\sigma N,$$ then for every fixed $T>0$, $$\\label{osc:shell-estimate}\n 2^{-a}\\int_{2^{v-1}}^{2^{v+1}}|\\widehat\\nu(r)|^2\\,dr\n \\le R^{o(1)}\\sup\\mathsf H_F(a,v)+O(R^{-T}).$$ The supremum has the same cells, class normalizations, lists and smoothing index as in Equation <a href=\"#osc:weighted-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"osc:weighted-measure\">[osc:weighted-measure]</a>. Its symbols are admissible with a derivative budget and common bounds for smooth angular factors depending only on $T,d,C_{\\mathcal A}$ and the fixed parameters. All these bounds are independent of $N$. When $f=g=1$, the measure $\\nu$ is positive. The assertion also holds on the fixed outer supports with $a=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "There is a second operation available only in the positive independent energy.       "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-graph-estimate",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho_1,\\rho_2$ be two regularized class laws, possibly equal, and write $M_j$, $A_{j,n}$, $I_j$ and $k_j$ for their profiles and associated quantities from Section <a href=\"#test:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"test:section\">6</a>. Let $X,Y$ be occupied cells of depth $a$. Put $X_1=X$, $X_2=Y$, and let $b^{(j)}_z(w)$ be an admissible endpoint symbol on $X_j$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#osc:symbols\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:symbols\">32</a>. Let $L$ be the largest nominal length of a descriptor in either symbol, with $L=0$ if both lists are empty. Suppose that the integer depths satisfy $0\\le a<p\\le v\\le N$ and $$\\label{graph:range}\n p-a\\le v-p+2\\sigma N,\\qquad\n L\\le v-p+2\\sigma N,\\qquad v-p\\ge10\\sigma N.$$ At endpoint $j$, suppose that the symbol imposes the parallel test $[g_j,p]$ at a level $\\ell_j\\in\\{0,\\ldots,J_0\\}$: for $\\rho_j$-almost every $z\\in X_j$ and every $w\\in S^{d-1}$, $$\\label{graph:support-test}\n b^{(j)}_z(w)\\ne0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n w\\in G^{(j)}_{\\ell_j,[g_j,p]}(z).$$ The superscript $j$ indicates the class used in the test. Assume $$\\label{graph:anchor-range}\n a\\le g_j<p,\\qquad g_j\\le a+2\\sigma N.$$*\n\n\n*Form the general independent energy $\\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v)$ of Definition <a href=\"#osc:states\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:states\">34</a> using these specified symbols. For every fixed $T>0$, $$\\label{graph:bound}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v)\n &\\le\n R^{-\\sum_{j=1}^2(A_{j,p}-A_{j,a})\n       +\\min_{j=1,2}k_j(a,p)+O_d(\\sigma)+o(1)}\\\\\n &\\qquad\\cdot\n \\max_{\\substack{P\\subset X,\\ Q\\subset Y\\\\\n                   P,Q\\text{ occupied depth-}p\\text{ cells}}}\n       \\mathsf H^{P,Q}(p,v)\n +O(R^{-T}).\n \\end{split}$$ No separation of $X$ and $Y$ is required. Every child in <a href=\"#graph:bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"graph:bound\">[graph:bound]</a> is formed by restricting the same endpoint symbols to $P$ and $Q$.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-graph-degrees",

      "id": "limit-graph-localization-and-both-degrees",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-angular-state",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal B=[a_0,t_0]\\subseteq[0,N]$ be an integer interval, called the retained base, and let $[a,v]$ be the current interval, with $$\\label{osc:base-contract}\n 0\\le a_0\\le a\\le v\\le t_0\\le N,\n \\qquad t_0\\le v+\\sigma N.$$ Write $$\\mathcal M_{>a}(\\mathcal B)=\n \\{\\mathfrak t\\in\\mathcal M(a_0,t_0):\n                 \\text{the anchor of }\\mathfrak t\\text{ is deeper than }a\\}.$$ A recursive angular state consists of these intervals, an index $i\\in\\{0,\\ldots,J_0-1\\}$, two occupied depth-$a$ cells $X,Y$ of $\\rho$, and two admissible endpoint symbols. No separation of the cells is required. Each symbol’s descriptor list consists of exactly one occurrence of each descriptor in $\\mathcal M_{>a}(\\mathcal B)$, at the common index $i$. Lemma <a href=\"#test:descriptor-properties\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"test:descriptor-properties\">31</a> gives a common fixed polynomial size bound for these lists throughout the recursive family. The state also specifies a fixed derivative budget and common numerical bounds for its smooth angular factors, as in Definition <a href=\"#osc:symbols\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"osc:symbols\">32</a>; these bounds are independent of $N$.\n\nThe value of this state is the already defined general energy for those data: $$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v):=\\mathsf H^{X,Y}(a,v).$$ The compact notation displays the current interval, base and index. The cells, symbols and fixed bounds remain part of the state data even when they are not displayed. We use a maximum only for finite choices of cells, and a supremum whenever admissible symbols vary. Neither operation varies the displayed base or index, or the fixed bounds, unless this is stated.",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-reductions",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose the current state is nonterminal, its smoothing index satisfies $i<J_0$, and $N$ is sufficiently large with all parameters fixed. For every prescribed $T>0$, it is bounded, apart from an error $O(R^{-T})$ from oscillatory operations, by a sum of child bounds whose number is polynomial in $N$, and possibly a low-frequency stopping term. For every non-low child, if the old start is $a$ and the new start is $a'$, its multiplier is $$\\label{rec:transition-factor}\n R^{-2(A_{a'}-A_a)+\\mathrm{cost}+C_d\\sigma+o(1)}.$$ A low child has cost zero and the entire multiplier $R^{o(1)}$, with no fixed $C_d\\sigma$ loss. A low-frequency stopping term is $R^{C_d\\sigma+o(1)}$. The legal children are*\n\n<div class=\"center\">\n\n| *Reduction* | *Parent and child*                                                | *Cost*                        |\n|:------------|:------------------------------------------------------------------|:------------------------------|\n| *Suffix*    | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,p)$*                       | *$A_p-\\min_{[p,t]}A$*         |\n| *Low*       | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(a,v)$*                       | *$0$; full factor $R^{o(1)}$* |\n| *High*      | *$\\mathsf D_i(a,t)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{[a,t]}(p,v)$*                   | *$k(a,p)$*                    |\n| *Direct*    | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(p,v)$* | *$k(a,p)$*                    |\n| *Near*      | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(h,v)$* | *$0$*                         |\n| *Far*       | *$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathcal B}(a,v)\\to\\mathsf D_{i+1}(b,v)$*          | *$0$*                         |\n\n</div>\n\n*For a positive parent, $p$ is its current interval’s fixed policy choice. The suffix row is used when $2p>a+t$. When $2p\\le a+t$, the shell index is an integer $a<v\\le t$: the low row has $t-v\\ge\\sigma N$, and the high row has $0\\le t-v<\\sigma N$. A high child has base $[a,t]$ and carries the fixed enlarged derivative budget and common angular bounds produced by the inverse conversion; these depend on $T$ and the fixed parameters, not on $N$.*\n\n*For an angular parent, use $L_0,h$ from Equation <a href=\"#rec:angular-split-depth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rec:angular-split-depth\">[rec:angular-split-depth]</a>. If $h\\le a$, the direct row chooses a remaining descriptor of maximal length $L$, its anchor $p$, and an origin $[b_0,u_0]\\subseteq\\mathcal B$ for that membership. This choice has $L=L_0$, $a<p<v$, $$b_0\\le p-L,\\qquad p+L\\le u_0,\\qquad v-a\\le2L,$$ and it supplies the parallel descriptor $[\\max(a,p-L),p]$ used by the graph estimate. If $h>a$, the near row uses the displayed depth $h$. The far rows range over integer depths $b$ arising from the comparable far displacements, with $a<b\\le h+O_d(1)<v$, and use separated depth-$b$ cell pairs. Direct and near children keep the same base, index, derivative budget and common angular bounds. A far child has the fresh positive list $\\mathcal M(b,v)$.*\n\n\n*Finite maxima over child cells use the stated geometry. Whenever admissible symbols vary, take a supremum over the symbol family just specified. An empty collection of child terms contributes zero. Constants outside powers of $R$ may depend on the fixed parameters, derivative budgets and angular bounds. The constants $C_d$ in exponent losses depend only on $d$. A terminal state is bounded by $$\\label{rec:terminal}\n R^{C_d(\\kappa+\\sigma)+o(1)}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"
        },

          "anchor": "stmt-ledger-termination",

Along such a branch the smoothing index increases by at most $20/\\kappa+1/\\sigma$. If the initial index is zero, every index required by the iteration, including the next support level used by a mask, is less than $$J_0=\\left\\lceil100(1/\\kappa+1/\\sigma)+100\\right\\rceil.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-073.1"

          "anchor": "asm:measure-telescoping",
          "exact_quote": "It remains to account for the variation of $\\alpha_N$. Set $\\zeta_n=\\alpha_n-\\alpha_{n+1}\\ge0$. Monotonicity and finiteness give $$\\label{asm:measure-telescoping}\n \\sum_{n\\ge K}\\|\\zeta_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n       =\\alpha_K(\\mathbb R)-\\alpha_\\infty(\\mathbb R)<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\alpha_N-\\alpha_\\infty=\\sum_{n\\ge N}\\zeta_n\n                         \\quad\\hbox{in total variation}.$$ For a finite $M\\ge K$, define $Z_M=\\sum_{N=K}^MP_N(\\alpha_N-\\alpha_\\infty)$. Substituting Equation <a href=\"#asm:measure-telescoping\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"asm:measure-telescoping\">[asm:measure-telescoping]</a> into this finite sum and using the bounded operators from measures to $L^1$ gives the exact identity $$\\label{asm:finite-band-identity}\n Z_M=\\sum_{n\\ge K}\n             (Q_{\\min(n,M)}-Q_{K-1})\\zeta_n.$$ For each fixed $M$ the series on the right converges in $L^1$. Moreover, Equation <a href=\"#asm:low-cutoff-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"asm:low-cutoff-bound\">[asm:low-cutoff-bound]</a> shows that $$Z:=\\sum_{n\\ge K}(Q_n-Q_{K-1})\\zeta_n$$ converges absolutely in $L^1$. Subtracting the two series cancels all $n\\le M$, and gives $$\\label{asm:variation-tail}\n \\begin{split}\n \\|Z_M-Z\\|_{L^1}\n &\\le2C_\\vartheta\\sum_{n>M}\\|\\zeta_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\\\\n &=2C_\\vartheta\n             \\|\\alpha_{M+1}-\\alpha_\\infty\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\end{split}$$ This is where monotonicity is used.
