A positive drop places $r$ beyond the inner interval. The depth axis is schematic, with the boundary strips enlarged.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-potential-replacement"></a>

The source figure is an external graphic; no image payload is embedded.

- Green segment for "child interval $[a, p]$" from $a$ to $j = p$.
- Boundary intervals marked "$\le 2q_0$" at each end ($a$ and $t$).
- Shaded region labeled "whole-block inner interval: $A_n \ge A_p$".
- Piecewise-linear curve labeled "profile $A_n$".
- Horizontal dashed line at height $A_p$.
- Points on the horizontal axis:
  - $i$
  - $j = p$
  - $r$ labeled "$r \in [p, t] \text{ minimizes } A$"
- Curved dashed arrow pointing from $j$ to $r$ with label "replace $j$ by $r$".
- Lower dimension intervals:
  - Green interval between $i$ and $j$: "$j - i \ge 10q_0$"
  - Red interval between $i$ and $r$: "$r - i \ge j - i$: the parent pair is admissible"
- Mathematical relation below:
  "$\mathcal{V}([a, t]) \le A_i + A_r = \mathcal{V}([a, p]) - (A_p - A_r)$"

<a id="stmt-ledger-termination"></a>
<div id="ledger:termination" class="lemma">

**Lemma 48** (Finite policy and smoothing budget). *Every branch of the state reductions has at most $20/\kappa$ non-low transitions and at most $1/\sigma$ low transitions, for sufficiently small fixed parameters and sufficiently large $N$. Along such a branch the smoothing index increases by at most $20/\kappa+1/\sigma$. If the initial index is zero, every index required by the iteration, including the next support level used by a mask, is less than $$J_0=\left\lceil100(1/\kappa+1/\sigma)+100\right\rceil.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ledger-termination"><summary>Proof of stmt-ledger-termination</summary>

*Proof.* Along a branch the start depth never decreases and the end depth never increases. Let $K$ be the number of fixed block representatives; for large $N$, $K\le1/\kappa+2$. Every high or direct step strictly advances the start to a representative. Such a representative can never be passed twice, so together these steps number at most $K$. Each suffix step lowers the end by at least $q_0$, and therefore there are at most $N/q_0\le1/\kappa$ suffix steps. Low steps lower the end by at least $\sigma N$, so there are at most $1/\sigma$.

An angular episode starts at the root or immediately after a high step and ends at a far step or a stopping term. Direct and near steps remain in that episode. There are at most $K+1$ episodes, and hence at most $K+1$ far steps.

Consider a near step whose child is nonterminal. That child has width $2L_0>100q_0$, so the floor in the formula for $L_0$ is inactive and $L=L_0$. Remove the factors whose anchors have now been reached. If the maximum remaining nominal length decreases, at least one formerly maximal anchor has just been passed; charge the near step to one such anchor. These charges are distinct along the branch. If the maximum stays equal to $L$, recomputing $v-2L$ at the child gives exactly its start depth, so the very next step is direct. Charge this near step to that next direct step. Finally there is at most one near step with terminal child. Thus near steps number at most $2K+1$. The combined non-low count is at most $5K+2$, which is less than $20/\kappa$ for sufficiently small $\kappa$.

Only suffix, low, and far steps increase the smoothing index; each increases it by one. If $i_{\mathrm{in}}$ is the initial index, the preceding counts therefore give $$i\le i_{\mathrm{in}}+20/\kappa+1/\sigma$$ at every state on the branch. High, direct, and near steps use the level-$i+1$ supports of their index-$i$ symbols without increasing the index. For $i_{\mathrm{in}}=0$, even this additional support level remains below $J_0$, since $20/\kappa+1/\sigma+1<J_0$. Thus every mask and support level required for the iteration from index zero is available. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-ledger-bound"></a>
<div id="ledger:bound" class="theorem">

<a id="ledger:bound-equation"></a>
**Theorem 49** (Single-profile angular bound). *Let $\mathsf H_0^{[c,N]}(c,N)$ be a recursive angular state of nonterminal width, as in Definition <a href="#rec:angular-state" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rec:angular-state">43</a>. Fix its initial derivative budget $B$ and its common numerical angular bounds $Q_j$, independently of $N$, as in Definition <a href="#osc:symbols" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:symbols">32</a>. Then $$\label{ledger:bound-equation}
 \mathsf H_0^{[c,N]}(c,N)
 \le R^{2A_c-\mathcal V([c,N])
                   +C_d(\kappa+\sigma/\kappa)+o(1)}.$$ This bound holds uniformly over its two occupied cells, including two copies of the same cell, and over its admissible bounded spatial multipliers and symbols with these fixed bounds. The $o(1)$ term may depend on $B$ and the $Q_j$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ledger-bound"><summary>Proof of stmt-ledger-bound</summary>

*Proof.* Iterate Proposition <a href="#rec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rec:reductions">45</a>, stopping at every terminal state or low-frequency stopping term. Lemma <a href="#ledger:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ledger:termination">48</a> gives a depth bound depending only on the fixed parameters. Since the root index is zero, it also ensures that every index required to continue a nonterminal child is available. Each transition has at most polynomially many discrete choices in $N$, and all coefficient sums in the oscillatory estimates are bounded or subpower. Thus the total main term is bounded by $R^{o(1)}$ times the largest terminal branch bound. Suprema over symbol classes are already part of the transition bounds and require no count of the members of those classes.

<a id="ledger:cost-sum"></a>
Fix a branch, and let $a_f$ be its final start. The changes of start in <a href="#rec:transition-factor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rec:transition-factor">[rec:transition-factor]</a> telescope exactly to $2A_c-2A_{a_f}$. By Lemma <a href="#ledger:payment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ledger:payment">47</a>, all costs are bounded by increases in the potential while it is defined. If a child has width less than $10q_0$, it is already terminal; moreover, the step reaching it has zero cost, since every potentially positive-cost child has width greater than $10q_0$. Let $J_*=[u,w]$ be the last interval on the branch with defined potential. Then $$\label{ledger:cost-sum}
 \sum\mathrm{cost}
 \le\mathcal V(J_*)-\mathcal V([c,N]).$$ This also applies when a low-frequency term stops at a nonterminal interval.

<a id="ledger:last-potential"></a>
We estimate $\mathcal V(J_*)$ directly; no potential is assigned to a shorter final interval. Nesting gives $a_f\in[u,w]$, and $w-u\ge10q_0$. Set $$i=\max\{u,\min\{a_f,w-10q_0\}\},\qquad j=i+10q_0.$$ Both indices lie in $J_*$, they are separated by $10q_0$, and each is within $10q_0$ of $a_f$. The profile Lipschitz bound therefore gives $$\label{ledger:last-potential}
 \mathcal V(J_*)\le A_i+A_j
 \le2A_{a_f}+20d\,q_0/N
 =2A_{a_f}+O_d(\kappa)+o(1).$$ Combining <a href="#ledger:cost-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ledger:cost-sum">[ledger:cost-sum]</a>–<a href="#ledger:last-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ledger:last-potential">[ledger:last-potential]</a> with the telescoping start factors leaves $2A_c-\mathcal V([c,N])+O_d(\kappa)+o(1)$. The stopping bound contributes $O_d(\kappa+\sigma)$. Only non-low transitions incur $C_d\sigma$ slack, so their combined loss is $O_d(\sigma/\kappa)$, by Lemma <a href="#ledger:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ledger:termination">48</a>. Low transitions contribute only subpower factors. This proves the main-term estimate in <a href="#ledger:bound-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ledger:bound-equation">[ledger:bound-equation]</a>.

For completeness, we fix the error and derivative budgets before taking $N$ large. Since $m$ is nondecreasing and $d$-Lipschitz with $m(0)=0$, all differences of $A$ and all costs in the reductions are bounded in absolute value by a dimensional constant. Each ancestor main factor is consequently at most $R^{C_d+o(1)}$. Choose a target accuracy $$T>C_d(1/\kappa+1/\sigma)+10d+10,$$ with the dimensional constant large enough for the branch bound. Demand this accuracy after all single-step normalizations and sums. Lemma <a href="#ledger:termination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ledger:termination">48</a> then ensures that every propagated additive error is negligible relative to the right side of <a href="#ledger:bound-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ledger:bound-equation">[ledger:bound-equation]</a>.

There is no recursive increase of the persistent angular derivative budget. Each passage from a positive distance count to an angular state begins with fresh undifferentiated masks; choose its inverse stationary expansion order first, large enough for the prescribed $T$. The resulting finite order, together with the initial order in the Theorem, bounds all derivatives carried by angular states. The common bounds for the smooth angular factors can likewise be fixed from the initial $Q_j$ and the bounds in Proposition <a href="#osc:shell-conversion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:shell-conversion">35</a> at the fixed accuracy used for these conversions. The graph estimate returns the original symbols restricted to child cells, with no additional derivatives. Removing reached factors from its positive child energy, as in a near transition, adds no derivatives to the remaining symbols. A far step may use an arbitrarily long direct stationary expansion, but its child is a positive distance count and carries none of those derivatives. Choose the nonstationary integration orders and the direct stationary orders after this persistent budget has been fixed. They may depend on $d,\kappa,\sigma,T$ and the initial budget, but not on $N$. Their large constants and polynomial descriptor counts are absorbed in the permitted constant and $o(1)$ terms; the dimensional exponent losses in the preceding algebra are unchanged. The asserted bound follows. ◻

</details>

<a id="asm:section"></a>
### Entry, shell summation, and the limiting distance measure

<span id="sec:assembly" label="sec:assembly"></span>

The graph estimate and the two-depth potential now give decay on each frequency shell. The measures used on different shells have different classes and masks. We first explain how to restore their mass, and then give a scalar reconstruction argument that accommodates the decreasing pair filters. Throughout this Section all distances are measured in the original space $\mathbb R^d$.

<a id="pm-h-55"></a>
#### The entry depth

Let $S=d/2$. Fix the starting probabilities $\mu_1,\mu_2$ and the number $\chi>0$ supplied by Proposition <a href="#prep:starting-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:starting-pairs">23</a>. Make one common similarity placing $E$ in $(1/4,3/4)^d$, and retain the notation for the transformed sets and measures. This multiplies every distance by one fixed positive constant and preserves the desired positive-measure conclusion. Choose one small common translation of the dyadic grid whose boundaries have zero mass for both starting laws. Its depth-zero cube $Q_0$ still contains $E$ in its interior. Use this same grid for both class decompositions. The two actual supports remain separated by a fixed positive distance, and their cross directions remain in the prepared patch. At scale $R=2^N$, apply Lemma <a href="#reg:regularization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reg:regularization">4</a> with parameter $\sigma$ to both probabilities. Write $\mathcal C_{j,N}$ for the retained classes on side $j$. For $C\in\mathcal C_{j,N}$, let $$a_{j,C}=\mu_j(C),\qquad
 \rho_{j,C}=a_{j,C}^{-1}\mu_j|_C.$$ The classes on each side are disjoint, their number is $R^{o(1)}$, and their discarded original mass is at most $R^{-\sigma+o(1)}$. All the $o(1)$ terms below are uniform over these classes, with the fixed parameters held constant. Every normalized class is supported in the same root cube $Q_0$ and assigns it mass one. Since every profile has $m_j(0)=0$, its root normalization is exactly $M_j(0)=R^{-m_j(0)}=1$.

<a id="asm:profile-input"></a>
For a pair of classes, suppress their labels and write $$A_{j,n}=m_j(n/N)-Sn/N,\qquad
 k_j(g,p)=A_{j,p}-\min_{g\le n\le p}A_{j,n}.$$ Recall from Lemma <a href="#test:profile-bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:profile-bounds">25</a> that $$\label{asm:profile-input}
 A_{j,0}=0,\qquad
 |A_{j,n}-A_{j,n'}|\le d|n-n'|/N,\qquad
 A_{j,n}\ge\chi n/N-C_d\sigma.$$ Here and in what follows, constants denoted by $C_d$ may be enlarged a finite number of times and depend only on $d$.

We seek a common depth $c$ for which one entry cost $k_j(0,c)$ is small and both excess profiles remain above a positive level on $[c,N]$. The graph estimate contributes the smaller cost. After Cauchy–Schwarz separates the profiles, the two single-profile estimates must supply a larger potential saving.

<a id="stmt-asm-entry"></a>
<div id="asm:entry" class="lemma">

<a id="asm:entry-definition"></a>
<a id="asm:entry-range"></a>
<a id="asm:entry-lower"></a>
<a id="asm:entry-cost"></a>
<a id="asm:entry-potential"></a>
**Lemma 50** (Entry depth). *Fix $0<\beta<\min(\chi/10,1/10)$. If $\sigma$ is sufficiently small in terms of $d,\chi,\beta$, then, for all sufficiently large $N$, the integer $$\label{asm:entry-definition}
 c=\max\{n\in\{0,\ldots,N\}:
                  \min(A_{1,n},A_{2,n})\le\beta\}$$ is defined and satisfies $$\begin{aligned}
 \label{asm:entry-range}
 &\left\lfloor\frac{\beta N}{2d}\right\rfloor\le c<N/4,\\
 \label{asm:entry-lower}
 &A_{j,n}\ge\beta-d/N
       \quad(j=1,2,\ c\le n\le N),\\
 \label{asm:entry-cost}
 &\min_{j=1,2} k_j(0,c)\le\beta+C_d\sigma.
\end{aligned}$$ If $\kappa$ is sufficiently small and $q_0=\lceil\kappa N\rceil$, the interval $[c,N]$ is nonterminal. Its potentials from Definition <a href="#ledger:potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ledger:potential">46</a> obey $$\label{asm:entry-potential}
 \mathcal V_j([c,N])
 :=\min_{\substack{c\le u<v\le N\\v-u\ge10q_0}}
                         (A_{j,u}+A_{j,v})
 \ge2\beta-2d/N.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-asm-entry"><summary>Proof of stmt-asm-entry</summary>

*Proof.* The index $0$ qualifies in Equation <a href="#asm:entry-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:entry-definition">[asm:entry-definition]</a>. For $n\le\beta N/(2d)$, the Lipschitz bound in Equation <a href="#asm:profile-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:profile-input">[asm:profile-input]</a> gives $A_{j,n}\le\beta/2$, proving the lower bound in Equation <a href="#asm:entry-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:entry-range">[asm:entry-range]</a>. For $n\ge N/4$, the lower profile bound gives $A_{j,n}\ge\chi/4-C_d\sigma>\beta$ when $\sigma$ is sufficiently small. This proves its upper bound.

By maximality of $c$, both $A_{j,c+1}$ exceed $\beta$, so $A_{j,c}>\beta-d/N$. Both profiles exceed $\beta$ at every later index. For at least one side $j_*$, we also have $A_{j_*,c}\le\beta$. Since $\min_{0\le n\le c}A_{j_*,n}\ge-C_d\sigma$, this gives Equation <a href="#asm:entry-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:entry-cost">[asm:entry-cost]</a>. The width of $[c,N]$ exceeds $3N/4$; thus it exceeds $100q_0$ for sufficiently small fixed $\kappa$ and large $N$. Every pair entering its potential consists of two indices covered by Equation <a href="#asm:entry-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:entry-lower">[asm:entry-lower]</a>, which proves Equation <a href="#asm:entry-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:entry-potential">[asm:entry-potential]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-56"></a>
#### Positive approximants and restoration of mass

Write $$D(x,y)=|x-y|,\qquad e(x,y)=\frac{x-y}{|x-y|},\qquad
 W(x,y)=D(x,y)^{-(d-1)/2}.$$ The fixed separated supports give constants $0<d_0<d_1<\infty$ with <a id="asm:distance-range"></a>$$\label{asm:distance-range}
 d_0\le D(x,y)\le d_1
 \quad\hbox{on }\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_1\times\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_2.$$ In particular $W$ is bounded above and below there by positive constants.

<a id="asm:endpoint-mask"></a>
For each pair of classes $(C_1,C_2)$, use its own entry index $c$ from Equation <a href="#asm:entry-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:entry-definition">[asm:entry-definition]</a>. At endpoint $j$ take the extra parallel descriptor $[0,c]$, together with every descriptor in $\mathcal M_j(c,N)$. Let $$\label{asm:endpoint-mask}
 b_{j;C_1,C_2}(z,w)
 =w^{(j)}_{0,[0,c]}(z,w)
                  \prod_{\mathfrak t\in\mathcal M_j(c,N)}
                         w^{(j)}_{0,\mathfrak t}(z,w).$$ The masks are those of Lemma <a href="#test:smooth-masks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:smooth-masks">29</a>; thus every factor lies in $[0,1]$ and equals one when its level-zero condition holds. Antipodal symmetry allows both endpoints to be evaluated at $e(x,y)$. Define the Borel function $\Theta_N$ on the fixed separated product $E_1\times E_2$ by $$\Theta_N(x,y)=
 b_{1;C_1,C_2}(x,e(x,y))
 b_{2;C_1,C_2}(y,e(x,y))
 \quad\hbox{if }(x,y)\in C_1\times C_2,$$ and set it equal to zero whenever either endpoint belongs to no retained class. In particular $0\le\Theta_N\le1$.

<a id="asm:measures"></a>
Let $\Gamma_N$ be the decreasing pair filters of Proposition <a href="#prep:starting-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:starting-pairs">23</a>. With $D_\#$ denoting pushforward under the scalar map $D$, define $$\label{asm:measures}
 \alpha_N=D_\#\bigl(W\mathbf 1_{\Gamma_N}
                          (\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)\bigr),\qquad
 \tau_N=D_\#\bigl(W\mathbf 1_{\Gamma_N}\Theta_N
                          (\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)\bigr).$$ These measures use the original class masses. Only $\tau_N$ depends on the shell’s class decomposition and angular masks.

<a id="stmt-asm-retained-measures"></a>
<div id="asm:retained-measures" class="lemma">

<a id="asm:tv-approximation"></a>
<a id="asm:limit-measure"></a>
**Lemma 51** (Restoration of mass). *There is $\eta>0$, depending on the fixed parameters and starting measures, such that $$\label{asm:tv-approximation}
 0\le\tau_N\le\alpha_N,\qquad
 \|\alpha_N-\tau_N\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\lesssim2^{-\eta N}.$$ Moreover $\alpha_N$ decreases to the nonzero finite positive measure $$\label{asm:limit-measure}
 \alpha_\infty=D_\#\bigl(W\mathbf 1_{\Gamma_\infty}
                               (\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)\bigr),\qquad
 \Gamma_\infty=\bigcap_N\Gamma_N.$$ All these measures are carried by the compact set of original distances $\Delta(E)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-asm-retained-measures"><summary>Proof of stmt-asm-retained-measures</summary>

*Proof.* Apply the cap preparation in Proposition <a href="#prep:starting-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:starting-pairs">23</a> with loss $R^\sigma$. It gives $\mathcal G_N\subset\Gamma_N$ such that $$(\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)(\Gamma_N\setminus\mathcal G_N)
       \lesssim R^{-c_*}
 \quad\hbox{for some }c_*>0.$$ For $\mu_1$-almost every first endpoint $x$, the angular subprobability $$\xi_{N,x}=e(x,\cdot)_*
             \bigl(\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G_N}(x,\cdot)\mu_2\bigr)$$ has the required $S$-cap bound with loss $R^\sigma$ down to scale $R^{-1}$. The change from $\pi_x(y)$ to $e(x,y)=-\pi_x(y)$ preserves cap bounds, since the antipodal map sends caps to caps. The analogous assertion holds for $\mu_2$-almost every second endpoint with the endpoints reversed. These are bounds for restrictions of the original opposite probability.

Fix $C_1,C_2$ and one descriptor $\mathfrak t$ used at the first endpoint. For $\mu_1$-almost every $x\in C_1$, the anchor cell determined by $\mathfrak t$ and $x$ is occupied and $\xi_{N,x}$ has the cap bound above. Let $F_{C_1,C_2,\mathfrak t,x}$ be the directions failing this level-zero test. For such $x$, Lemma <a href="#test:exceptional-directions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:exceptional-directions">28</a> applies directly to $\xi_{N,x}$ and gives <a id="asm:original-fiber-estimate"></a>$$\label{asm:original-fiber-estimate}
 \mu_2\{y\in C_2:(x,y)\in\mathcal G_N,
       \ e(x,y)\in F_{C_1,C_2,\mathfrak t,x}\}\le R^{-\sigma}.$$ Restricting the set of $y$ to $C_2$ only decreases the left side; no normalization by $\mu_2(C_2)$ occurs. Integrating in $x$ over $C_1$ costs at most $a_{1,C_1}R^{-\sigma}$. Sum first over $C_1$, whose original masses have sum at most one, and then over $C_2$ and the descriptors. The latter two counts are $R^{o(1)}$, by Lemma <a href="#reg:regularization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reg:regularization">4</a> and Lemma <a href="#test:descriptor-properties" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:descriptor-properties">31</a>. Thus the first-endpoint failure mass is $R^{-\sigma+o(1)}$. The same argument in the other orientation gives this bound for second-endpoint failures. There is no sum over anchor cells: a point and descriptor already specify one.

The product in Equation <a href="#asm:endpoint-mask" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:endpoint-mask">[asm:endpoint-mask]</a> equals one if all its level-zero tests hold. Hence $1-\Theta_N$ on retained class pairs is bounded by the indicator of the union of these failures. Including the original mass outside retained class pairs and $\Gamma_N\setminus\mathcal G_N$ gives $$\int_{\Gamma_N}(1-\Theta_N)\,d(\mu_1\otimes\mu_2)
 \lesssim R^{-c_*}+R^{-\sigma+o(1)}.$$ The bounded weight $W$ proves Equation <a href="#asm:tv-approximation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:tv-approximation">[asm:tv-approximation]</a>, for example with any fixed $0<\eta<\min(c_*,\sigma)/2$ and sufficiently large $N$. The set $\mathcal G_N$ was used only in this estimate: it is not a multiplier in Equation <a href="#asm:measures" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:measures">[asm:measures]</a>.

The filters $\Gamma_N$ decrease and have positive limiting product mass. Continuity of a finite measure along decreasing sets proves Equation <a href="#asm:limit-measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:limit-measure">[asm:limit-measure]</a> and monotonicity of $\alpha_N$. The positive lower bound for $W$ shows $\alpha_\infty(\mathbb R)>0$. Finally, the two original supports are compact subsets of $E$, and $D$ is continuous. Every pushforward above is therefore carried by their compact distance image, which is contained in $\Delta(E)$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-57"></a>
#### The shell estimate

<a id="stmt-asm-shell-decay"></a>
<div id="asm:shell-decay" class="proposition">

<a id="asm:shell-bound"></a>
**Proposition 52** (Decay of the approximating distance measures). *The fixed parameters can be chosen, in the order $\beta,\kappa,\sigma,\omega$, so that some $\gamma>0$ satisfies $$\label{asm:shell-bound}
 \int_{2^{N-1}\le |r|\le2^{N+1}}
                   |\widehat{\tau_N}(r)|^2\,dr
       \lesssim2^{-\gamma N}$$ for all sufficiently large $N$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-asm-shell-decay"><summary>Proof of stmt-asm-shell-decay</summary>

*Proof.* For the moment fix one normalized class pair and one product $X_\ell\times Y_\ell$ in the spatial partition of $\Gamma_N$. The corresponding component measure $\nu$ is the pushforward of $$W(x,y)\mathbf 1_{X_\ell}(x)\mathbf 1_{Y_\ell}(y)
 b_{1;C_1,C_2}(x,e(x,y))b_{2;C_1,C_2}(y,e(x,y))
             \,d\rho_{1,C_1}(x)\,d\rho_{2,C_2}(y).$$ The product indicators are permitted bounded measurable spatial multipliers in the oscillatory estimates. At depth zero take $X=Y=Q_0$, each with its own normalized class law. These are occupied root cells, with $M_1(0)=M_2(0)=1$. Their supported laws lie in the fixed separated sets $E_1,E_2$, so the outer-support geometry of Section <a href="#osc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:section">7</a> applies. The cell-center separation in Equation <a href="#osc:separation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="osc:separation">[osc:separation]</a> is the internal-cell convention; the outer root uses the separation of the supported laws. In particular, all root spatial diameters in the graph argument are $O_d(1)$, while the actual cross distances have a fixed positive lower bound. By Lemma <a href="#test:descriptor-properties" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:descriptor-properties">31</a>, the largest nominal length of an endpoint descriptor is at most $$L\le\max\bigl(c,(N-c)/2\bigr)\le N/2.$$ Thus the root shell conversion, Proposition <a href="#osc:shell-conversion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="osc:shell-conversion">35</a>, applies with $a=0$, $v=N$. Its weight is precisely $W$; there is no internal normalization of the distance weight at this root. For any prescribed fixed $T>0$, it bounds the squared Fourier norm on the positive shell by $R^{o(1)}\sup\mathsf H_F(0,N)+O(R^{-T})$. The supremum retains the root cells, class normalizations, lists, and smoothing index zero; its admissible symbols have the fixed derivative budget and common numerical angular bounds supplied by that Proposition for $T$.

<a id="asm:graph-factor"></a>
Apply Corollary <a href="#graph:separated-form" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="graph:separated-form">41</a> at $p=c$ to each energy in this supremum. The required parallel descriptor is the extra $[0,c]$ on each side, with $g=0$. All scale conditions hold: $c>0$ for large $N$, $c<N/4$, $L\le N/2<N-c$, and $N-c\ge10\sigma N$. Since $A_{j,0}=0$, the graph factor is $$\label{asm:graph-factor}
 R^{-A_{1,c}-A_{2,c}+\min_j k_j(0,c)+O_d(\sigma)+o(1)}.$$ The graph estimate produces a general two-profile energy $\mathsf H(c,N)$ on a pair of occupied $c$-cells, still carrying the same endpoint symbols.

On these cells the extra $[0,c]$ factor is spatially constant within each endpoint cell. It, and any of its normalized differentiated versions produced by shell conversion, can be removed at bounded cost from the positive independent angular energies. The remaining descriptor lists are exactly those with base $[c,N]$ and with reached anchors omitted. This removal is made in the general positive energy $\mathsf H$, where the two angular factors are nonnegative.

<a id="asm:two-profile-cs"></a>
<a id="asm:component-shell"></a>
To separate the two profiles, write their nonnegative angular energies as $E_1(r),E_2(r)$ and use the common measure $d\lambda_N(r)=\Psi(r/2^N)\,dr/2^N$. Cauchy–Schwarz gives the exact normalization $$\label{asm:two-profile-cs}
 \begin{split}
 2^{d(N-c)}\int E_1(r)E_2(r)\,d\lambda_N(r)
 &\le
 \left(2^{d(N-c)}\int E_1(r)^2\,d\lambda_N(r)\right)^{1/2}\\
 &\quad\cdot
 \left(2^{d(N-c)}\int E_2(r)^2\,d\lambda_N(r)\right)^{1/2}.
 \end{split}$$ Each parenthesis is the independent angular energy formed by duplicating one endpoint law, cell, and symbol. With the unreached descriptors of $\mathcal M_j(c,N)$, it is the single-profile recursive state $\mathsf H_0^{[c,N]}(c,N)$ for profile $j$; denote this value by $\mathsf H_j(c,N)$. Its symbols have the fixed derivative budget and common numerical angular bounds supplied by shell conversion; restriction to the $c$-cells and duplication preserve these bounds. These states impose no separation condition, so Theorem <a href="#ledger:bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ledger:bound">49</a> applies to each, including the case of two identical cells. It yields $$\mathsf H_j(c,N)
 \le R^{2A_{j,c}-\mathcal V_j([c,N])
                         +O_d(\kappa+\sigma/\kappa)+o(1)}.$$ The profile terms in Equation <a href="#asm:graph-factor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:graph-factor">[asm:graph-factor]</a> and the geometric mean of these bounds combine to <a id="asm:entry-cancellation"></a>$$\label{asm:entry-cancellation}
 \begin{split}
 &-A_{1,c}-A_{2,c}+\min_j k_j(0,c)
       +\frac12\sum_{j=1}^2
                    \bigl(2A_{j,c}-\mathcal V_j([c,N])\bigr)\\
 &\hspace{20mm}
 =\min_j k_j(0,c)-\frac12
                     \bigl(\mathcal V_1([c,N])+\mathcal V_2([c,N])\bigr)
 \le-\beta+C_d\sigma+2d/N,
 \end{split}$$ where the last inequality uses the cost and potential bounds in Lemma <a href="#asm:entry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:entry">50</a>. Including the stated losses proves uniformly for each component that $$\label{asm:component-shell}
 \int_{2^{N-1}\le r\le2^{N+1}}|\widehat\nu(r)|^2\,dr
 \lesssim
 R^{-\beta+C_d(\kappa+\sigma/\kappa)+o(1)}+R^{-T}.$$ Here $T$ can be any fixed sufficiently large number, after choosing the expansion and integration-by-parts orders. The term $O_d(\sigma)$ has been included in $O_d(\sigma/\kappa)$, with $\kappa<1$.

We make the parameter and error choices explicit. First choose $\beta$ as in Lemma <a href="#asm:entry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:entry">50</a>. Next choose $\kappa$ so small that the entry interval is nonterminal and $C_d\kappa<\beta/16$. Then choose $\sigma$ so small that all the profile and transition hypotheses hold and $C_d\sigma/\kappa<\beta/16$. Finally choose $\omega>0$ so small that <a id="asm:omega-choice"></a>$$\label{asm:omega-choice}
 4d\omega<\beta/8.$$ The preparation of $\Gamma_N$ is used with this fixed $\omega$ and cap loss $R^\sigma$. Its deletion exponent $c_*$ may depend on both parameters; only $c_*>0$ was needed in Lemma <a href="#asm:retained-measures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:retained-measures">51</a>.

For completeness, the error accuracy is fixed after these parameters and before $N$ tends to infinity. Theorem <a href="#ledger:bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ledger:bound">49</a> bounds the number of steps on a branch by a constant depending on $\kappa,\sigma$. Choose a fixed local negative-power accuracy large enough to dominate all ancestor factors and all errors on such a branch, leaving the root remainder $R^{-T}$ in Equation <a href="#asm:component-shell" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:component-shell">[asm:component-shell]</a>, with, say, $T>10+4d\omega$. The inverse expansion orders are then fixed and determine a finite symbol derivative budget. The direct expansion and integration-by-parts orders are chosen for that budget and accuracy, as in Theorem <a href="#ledger:bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ledger:bound">49</a>. The graph step and Equation <a href="#asm:two-profile-cs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:two-profile-cs">[asm:two-profile-cs]</a> introduce no persistent new derivative orders; the spatial indicators have zero angular derivatives. These choices affect constants and the threshold for $N$, and do not change the dimensional coefficients in the exponent losses above. We now take $N$ sufficiently large for all these fixed choices and for the uniform $o(1)$ losses.

There are at most $L_N\lesssim R^{2d\omega}$ spatial products in $\Gamma_N$. Let $\nu_{C_1,C_2,\ell}$ denote the preceding component. The exact recombination is $$\tau_N=\sum_{C_1,C_2}a_{1,C_1}a_{2,C_2}
                    \sum_{\ell=1}^{L_N}\nu_{C_1,C_2,\ell}.$$ Triangle inequality in the Fourier $L^2$ norm costs no class-count factor, because $$\sum_{C_1,C_2}a_{1,C_1}a_{2,C_2}
 =\left(\sum_{C_1}a_{1,C_1}\right)
  \left(\sum_{C_2}a_{2,C_2}\right)\le1.$$ It costs at most $L_N$ for the spatial products. Squaring therefore costs $R^{4d\omega}$, giving <a id="asm:assembled-shell"></a>$$\label{asm:assembled-shell}
 \int_{2^{N-1}\le r\le2^{N+1}}
                  |\widehat{\tau_N}(r)|^2\,dr
 \lesssim R^{-\beta+C_d(\kappa+\sigma/\kappa)+4d\omega+o(1)}
                       +R^{-T+4d\omega}.$$ Our successive choices leave a strictly negative exponent; for example, increasing the starting $N$ if necessary, the right side is $O(R^{-\beta/2})$. Each $\tau_N$ is a real positive measure, so $\widehat{\tau_N}(-r)=\overline{\widehat{\tau_N}(r)}$. The same bound holds on the negative shell, proving Equation <a href="#asm:shell-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:shell-bound">[asm:shell-bound]</a> with some $\gamma>0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-58"></a>
#### A monotone shell reconstruction lemma

The following scalar fact isolates the final limiting argument. Its hypotheses require no rate at which the decreasing measures approach their limit. The proof combines a smooth dyadic Fourier decomposition with the summable positive increments of a decreasing family of finite measures. We give this reconstruction explicitly.

<a id="stmt-asm-monotone-shell"></a>
<div id="asm:monotone-shell" class="lemma">

**Lemma 53** (Monotone measure shell criterion). *Let $(\alpha_N)_{N\ge K}$ be finite positive Borel measures on $\mathbb R$ with $\alpha_{N+1}\le\alpha_N$, and let $\alpha_\infty=\lim_N\alpha_N$. Let $\tau_N$ be finite complex Borel measures such that <a id="asm:criterion-hypotheses"></a>$$\label{asm:criterion-hypotheses}
 \sum_{N\ge K}\|\alpha_N-\tau_N\|_{\mathrm{TV}}<\infty,
 \qquad
 \sum_{N\ge K}
 \left(\int_{2^{N-1}\le |r|\le2^{N+1}}
                   |\widehat{\tau_N}(r)|^2\,dr\right)^{1/2}<\infty.$$ Then $\alpha_\infty$ is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure. In particular, the conclusion holds if the two quantities inside these sums are respectively $O(2^{-\eta N})$ and $O(2^{-\gamma N/2})$ for arbitrary $\eta,\gamma>0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-asm-monotone-shell"><summary>Proof of stmt-asm-monotone-shell</summary>

<a id="asm:low-cutoff-bound"></a>
*Proof.* Choose $\vartheta\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$ equal to one on $[-1,1]$ and supported in $[-2,2]$. Let $Q_N$ be convolution with the inverse Fourier transform of $\vartheta(2^{-N}r)$, and put $P_N=Q_N-Q_{N-1}$. The multiplier of $P_N$ is supported in $\{2^{N-1}\le|r|\le2^{N+1}\}$. Dilation of the fixed Schwartz kernel gives a constant $C_\vartheta$ such that, for every finite measure $\nu$, $$\label{asm:low-cutoff-bound}
 \|Q_N\nu\|_{L^1}\le C_\vartheta\|\nu\|_{\mathrm{TV}},\qquad
 \left\|\sum_{N=a}^bP_N\nu\right\|_{L^1}
 \le2C_\vartheta\|\nu\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\quad(a\le b).$$ The second bound follows from telescoping to $Q_b-Q_{a-1}$; in particular its constant is independent of the number of bands.

<a id="asm:two-convergent-series"></a>
With the convention $\widehat\nu(r)=\int e^{-irt}\,d\nu(t)$, Plancherel (Mattila 1995, equation (12.4)) <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[Mattila1995](#ref-Mattila1995)] and the uniformly bounded band multipliers give $$\|P_N\tau_N\|_{L^2}
 \lesssim
 \left(\int_{2^{N-1}\le |r|\le2^{N+1}}
                   |\widehat{\tau_N}(r)|^2\,dr\right)^{1/2}.$$ Thus $$\label{asm:two-convergent-series}
 G:=\sum_{N\ge K}P_N\tau_N\quad\hbox{converges in }L^2,
 \qquad
 H:=\sum_{N\ge K}P_N(\alpha_N-\tau_N)
                         \quad\hbox{converges in }L^1.$$ Both series converge absolutely in the indicated Banach space.

<a id="asm:measure-telescoping"></a>
<a id="asm:variation-tail"></a>
It remains to account for the variation of $\alpha_N$. Set $\zeta_n=\alpha_n-\alpha_{n+1}\ge0$. Monotonicity and finiteness give $$\label{asm:measure-telescoping}
 \sum_{n\ge K}\|\zeta_n\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
       =\alpha_K(\mathbb R)-\alpha_\infty(\mathbb R)<\infty,
 \qquad
 \alpha_N-\alpha_\infty=\sum_{n\ge N}\zeta_n
                         \quad\hbox{in total variation}.$$ For a finite $M\ge K$, define $Z_M=\sum_{N=K}^MP_N(\alpha_N-\alpha_\infty)$. Substituting Equation <a href="#asm:measure-telescoping" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:measure-telescoping">[asm:measure-telescoping]</a> into this finite sum and using the bounded operators from measures to $L^1$ gives the exact identity <a id="asm:finite-band-identity"></a>$$\label{asm:finite-band-identity}
 Z_M=\sum_{n\ge K}
             (Q_{\min(n,M)}-Q_{K-1})\zeta_n.$$ For each fixed $M$ the series on the right converges in $L^1$. Moreover, Equation <a href="#asm:low-cutoff-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:low-cutoff-bound">[asm:low-cutoff-bound]</a> shows that $$Z:=\sum_{n\ge K}(Q_n-Q_{K-1})\zeta_n$$ converges absolutely in $L^1$. Subtracting the two series cancels all $n\le M$, and gives $$\label{asm:variation-tail}
 \begin{split}
 \|Z_M-Z\|_{L^1}
 &\le2C_\vartheta\sum_{n>M}\|\zeta_n\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\\
 &=2C_\vartheta
             \|\alpha_{M+1}-\alpha_\infty\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
 \longrightarrow0.
 \end{split}$$ This is where monotonicity is used. The original double series has not been rearranged without a convergence justification.

<a id="asm:reconstruction"></a>
Finally, finite telescoping gives $$\label{asm:reconstruction}
 Q_M\alpha_\infty
 =Q_{K-1}\alpha_\infty
  +\sum_{N=K}^MP_N\tau_N
  +\sum_{N=K}^MP_N(\alpha_N-\tau_N)-Z_M.$$ The fixed low-frequency term $Q_{K-1}\alpha_\infty$ is a smooth $L^1$ function by convolution with a Schwartz kernel. By Equations <a href="#asm:two-convergent-series" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:two-convergent-series">[asm:two-convergent-series]</a> and <a href="#asm:variation-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:variation-tail">[asm:variation-tail]</a>, the right side converges in $L^1+L^2$, hence in distributions, to $$f=Q_{K-1}\alpha_\infty+G+H-Z\in L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R).$$ The kernels defining $Q_M$ form an approximate identity, so the left side of Equation <a href="#asm:reconstruction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:reconstruction">[asm:reconstruction]</a> converges in distributions to $\alpha_\infty$. Therefore $\alpha_\infty=f\,\mathcal L^1$. Since $\alpha_\infty$ is a finite positive measure, this locally integrable density is nonnegative almost everywhere and belongs to $L^1(\mathbb R)$. This proves absolute continuity. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-59"></a>
#### Completion of the proof

<a id="stmt-asm-completion"></a>
<div id="asm:completion" class="proposition">

**Proposition 54**. *For the starting pair in Proposition <a href="#prep:starting-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:starting-pairs">23</a>, there is a nonzero finite positive measure, absolutely continuous with respect to $\mathcal L^1$, carried by the original distance set $\Delta(E)$. Consequently $\mathcal L^1(\Delta(E))>0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-asm-completion"><summary>Proof of stmt-asm-completion</summary>

*Proof.* Choose the parameters as in Proposition <a href="#asm:shell-decay" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:shell-decay">52</a>. Lemma <a href="#asm:retained-measures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:retained-measures">51</a> gives decreasing $\alpha_N$ with nonzero limit $\alpha_\infty$, and approximants $\tau_N$ with summable total-variation error. Proposition <a href="#asm:shell-decay" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:shell-decay">52</a> gives the second summability hypothesis of Lemma <a href="#asm:monotone-shell" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:monotone-shell">53</a>. That Lemma makes $\alpha_\infty$ absolutely continuous. By Lemma <a href="#asm:retained-measures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:retained-measures">51</a> it is carried by $\Delta(E)$ and has positive mass, so $\Delta(E)$ cannot be a Lebesgue null set. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-asm-completion-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-asm-completion</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Start with a Frostman probability on the given compact set $E\subset\mathbb R^d$, $d\ge2$, with exponent strictly larger than $d/2$. In the odd-dimensional exceptional affine-plane case, Lemma <a href="#prep:classical-plane" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:classical-plane">18</a> already proves the conclusion using original distances inside that plane. In every other case, Proposition <a href="#prep:starting-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:starting-pairs">23</a> supplies the separated original probabilities and filters used above, and Proposition <a href="#asm:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:completion">54</a> proves the conclusion. This includes $d=2$: its cap preparation is the integer case of Lemma <a href="#prep:integer-caps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:integer-caps">21</a>. The auxiliary projections used in preparation control directions only; the measures in Equation <a href="#asm:measures" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:measures">[asm:measures]</a> and their nonzero limit are pushforwards by $|x-y|$ in $\mathbb R^d$. Thus the argument establishes the asserted positive measure for the original distance set in every dimension $d\ge2$. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The main construction. Auxiliary projections prepare directional bounds and pair filters; the cap bounds hold after an additional power-small pair deletion. These data return to the original space, where the argument uses the Euclidean distance of the original pairs. The decreasing filters and shell-dependent approximants meet in the final scalar monotone-shell argument.",
      "figure_id": "fig:proof-map",
      "pdf_page": 5,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:proof-map",
          "exact_quote": "The main construction.\n Auxiliary projections prepare directional bounds and pair filters;\n the cap bounds hold after an additional power-small pair deletion.\n These data return to the original space, where the argument uses the\n Euclidean ",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "Flowchart diagram showing the construction workflow:\n- Top rounded box: \"Frostman data on $E \\subset \\mathbb{R}^d$, with $s > S = d/2$\".\n- Two arrows lead down to two columns under shaded headers:\n  - Left column header: \"AUXILIARY SPACES: DIRECTIONS\" containing a box with:\n    - \"$\\mathbb{R}^{\\lfloor S \\rfloor + 1}$: radial $L^p$ estimate\"\n    - \"Starting cap exponent $\\lfloor S \\rfloor$\"\n    - \"Odd $d = 2k - 1$: upgrade in $\\mathbb{R}^{k+1}$\"\n    - \"$k - 1 \\longrightarrow S$\"\n  - Right column header: \"ORIGINAL $\\mathbb{R}^d$: MEASURES\" containing a box with:\n    - \"Positive separated restrictions\"\n    - \"Dyadic classes and profiles\"\n    - \"$A_{j, n} = m_j(n/N) - Sn/N$\"\n- Two downward arrows converge to a central box; the left arrow is labeled \"Lift cap bounds;\\npull back pair filters\".\n- Central box 1:\n  - \"Original-space tests and smooth angular masks\"\n  - \"$S$-cap bounds after power-small pair deletion; $\\Gamma_N \\downarrow \\Gamma_\\infty$\"\n- Arrow pointing down to Central box 2:\n  - \"Distance shells: $D(x, y) = |x - y|$ in $\\mathbb{R}^d$\"\n  - \"Stationary phase and the graph estimate\"\n  - Left margin annotation: \"DISTANCE ANALYSIS\", \"Pairs remain in $E \\times E$\".\n- Arrow pointing down to Central box 3:\n  - \"State recursion + two-depth potential + entry\"\n  - \"Restore original masses $\\Longrightarrow$ summable shell decay\"\n- Arrow pointing down to Bottom box:\n  - \"SCALAR MONOTONE-SHELL LIMIT\"\n  - \"$\\alpha_N \\downarrow \\alpha_\\infty \\neq 0$ and shell approximants $\\tau_N$\"\n  - \"$\\alpha_\\infty \\ll \\mathcal{L}^1$, supported on $\\Delta(E) \\Longrightarrow \\mathcal{L}^1(\\Delta(E)) > 0$\""
    },
    {
      "caption": "Payment for a suffix transition in Lemma 47. Replacing the later depth of a minimizing child pair by the inner-interval minimizer $p$ preserves separation and cannot increase the sum. Thus a minimizing child pair can be chosen as $(i,p)$. If $r$ minimizes $A$ on $[p,t]$, the pair $(i,r)$ is admissible in the parent. Its sum is lower by $A_{p} − A_{r}$, which pays the transition cost. A positive drop places $r$ beyond the inner interval. The depth axis is schematic, with the boundary strips enlarged.",
      "figure_id": "fig:potential-replacement",
      "pdf_page": 55,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:potential-replacement",
          "exact_quote": "Payment for a suffix transition in Lemma~\\ref{ledger:payment}.\n Replacing the later depth of a minimizing child pair by the inner-interval\n minimizer $p$ preserves separation and cannot increase the sum. Thus a\n minimizing child pair can be",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "Schematic diagram of a suffix transition:\n- Header label: \"A suffix transition $[a, t] \\longrightarrow [a, p]: 2p > a + t$\"\n- Top dimension bar for \"parent interval\" extending from $a$ to $t$.\n- Green segment for \"child interval $[a, p]$\" from $a$ to $j = p$.\n- Boundary intervals marked \"$\\le 2q_0$\" at each end ($a$ and $t$).\n- Shaded region labeled \"whole-block inner interval: $A_n \\ge A_p$\".\n- Piecewise-linear curve labeled \"profile $A_n$\".\n- Horizontal dashed line at height $A_p$.\n- Points on the horizontal axis:\n  - $i$\n  - $j = p$\n  - $r$ labeled \"$r \\in [p, t] \\text{ minimizes } A$\"\n- Curved dashed arrow pointing from $j$ to $r$ with label \"replace $j$ by $r$\".\n- Lower dimension intervals:\n  - Green interval between $i$ and $j$: \"$j - i \\ge 10q_0$\"\n  - Red interval between $i$ and $r$: \"$r - i \\ge j - i$: the parent pair is admissible\"\n- Mathematical relation below:\n  \"$\\mathcal{V}([a, t]) \\le A_i + A_r = \\mathcal{V}([a, p]) - (A_p - A_r)$\"\n- Bottom note: \"Depth axis schematic; boundary strips enlarged.\""
    }
  ],
